# Built-deltas log

*Started in phase DV0, 2026-10-03 (spec §9.5 step 6). It records every change that the pins, probes and spikes force on `docs/design/DESIGN-PROPOSAL.md` (design) and `docs/design/spec4dafny-verus.md` (spec). **This log is authoritative over both documents wherever they differ**: it describes the course as built and as measured. Evidence for each entry is in `docs/design/DV0-REPORT.md` and in the author-only archive `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/author-probes/` (paths below are relative to it). Exact message texts are pinned in `VERSIONS.md` §2.4 (ids `W-D…`, `W-V…`).*

**Rules for this log.**
- Numbers are stable. A later phase never renumbers or edits an entry's meaning; it adds a new entry that supersedes an old one and marks the old one `superseded by Δn`.
- **Status:** `adopted` means the probe settles it and the owner implements it as written. `proposed` means a recommendation the owning phase confirms (or replaces with a new entry) when it starts; until then the design text stands where the proposal is silent, and the proposal wins where the design contradicts a probe. `decision` means the user must choose. `adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)` marks a DV0 proposal the orchestrator adopted at the end of DV0; its entry keeps its text and gains a **Status** line with any condition attached.
- **Orchestrator decisions at the end of DV0 (2026-10-03).** Adopted: Δ10, Δ13, Δ35, Δ45, Δ49, Δ51, Δ52, Δ53, Δ56, Δ62. Decided by the user the same day: Δ60 (re-add the `docker` group) and Δ67 (CI is a local script).
- **The author probe archive** `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/author-probes/` is a read-denied asset of the reference class: spec §7.1 lists it as the "author probe archive" (`~/.cache/<course>/**`), which the fourth canonical deny rule makes unreadable in study mode. Its copies of spike sources (inputs of the Verus gate variants and reference runs) are therefore acceptable; only `Solutions/` and this archive may hold answer material.
- **Owner** names the phase that implements the change (design §15.2): DV1a (registry, checker, generator, hook, CORE, skills), DV1b (`vcheck`/`vaudit`/`specfreeze`, layer crates, check units, `firsttry`, `pins.py`, `get_toolchain.sh`; the layer crates and check units moved to DV2 by Δ94), DV1c (G/V corpora, core CI), DV2 (L1–L2, Q01–Q03), DV3a/DV3b (Q04–Q06 + L3 / Q07–Q09 + L4), DV4 (`fvh`, sandbox, proxy), DV5 (Q10–Q18, L5–L7, labs), DV7 (challenges Q19–Q33), DVR0–DVR3 (R layers and modules).
- **DV1a entries (Δ68–Δ93, 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-04).** `adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)` marks a deviation from the design or spec, or an interface decision, taken while building DV1a and adopted when DV1a was integrated: the course is built that way, and later phases build from these entries, not from the lane reports. Δ68–Δ70 are orchestrator process decisions of the same day; Δ92–Δ93 are orchestrator decisions taken at DV1a's close, marked `adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-04)`. Their **Why** lines cite the implementing files, the test suites and, where one exists, a probe under `dv1a/` in the archive; they describe the code and never paste it (Δ88 quotes the hook header's prose lists, which are documentation, not code). Where an entry and the file it cites disagree later, the file's owner fixes one of them in the same change.
- **Revised in place at DV1a's close (2026-10-04).** DV1a's last fix round (round 3) changed the hook and checker after Δ71–Δ91 were written, so Δ69, Δ74, Δ78, Δ79, Δ82 and Δ84–Δ89 were brought up to the files as installed at DV1a's close. DV1a had not closed, so the stable-number rule above binds these entries from DV1b on.
- **DV1b and DV1c entries (Δ94–Δ107, 2026-10-04).** `adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)` and `adopted (DV1c, 2026-10-04)` mark a deviation from the design, the spec or an earlier entry, or an interface decision, taken while building that phase. Both sets were written at DV1c's close by the orchestration's records lane, from the lanes' reports in the orchestration journal and from the files as built. A DV1b entry that DV1c changed says so in a "Revised in DV1c" line. Their **Why** lines cite the implementing files, the test suites and the evidence under `dv1b/` and `dv1c/` in the archive, and they never paste code. Each earlier entry they amend carries a **Status** line that points forward: Δ3, Δ11, Δ12, Δ22, Δ33, Δ67, Δ69, Δ78, Δ80, Δ82, Δ85 and Δ87. Δ69's step 3 was rewritten in place to match `CLAUDE.md`, which Δ69 requires.
- **The corpus entry (Δ108, 2026-10-04).** The cheat corpus was built after DV1c closed, by the reopened DV1c orchestration, and Δ108 records it as `adopted (DV1c, reopened, 2026-10-04)`. The orchestration's wrap-up lane wrote it from the Dafny, Verus and runner lanes' reports and from the files as built. Its evidence is the corpus itself, whose manifests `scripts/test_corpus.py --ci` re-checks, not the archive. It supersedes Δ106. Δ69, Δ103, Δ104, Δ106 and Δ107 carry **Status** lines that point to it.
- **The DV1c close-out entry (Δ109, 2026-10-04).** The close-out lane of the reopened DV1c orchestration closed Δ108's open items for the ci lane, the guard and the two missing wordings. It also settled the layout naming, added the runner tests and updated DV0-REPORT §8. Δ109 records this as `adopted (DV1c, reopened, 2026-10-04)`, written from the files as built and from the lane's own test runs, which it lists. Δ103, Δ104, Δ105, Δ106 and Δ108 carry **Status** lines that point to it. Each of Δ108's **Open** items is marked closed or still open, in place.

Line counts and gate results come from `Solutions/spikes/`, whose contents stay reference-only.

## A. Holes and hole counting

### Δ1 · Dafny reports lemma holes as "method" — adopted
- **Change:** a bodyless lemma or method with `ensures` warns W-D01 (`This ensures clause is part of a bodyless method. …`), not "bodyless lemma"; functions warn W-D02 ("bodyless function"). The hole counter matches W-D01, W-D02 and W-D03 exactly and nothing else.
- **Why:** D1 (`dafny/D1-hole-kinds/k01`, `k02`, `k05`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 (Port: Dafny, proof hole); spec §11.1 rows 1–2.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ2 · Dafny holes block only exec code, not the whole compile unit — adopted
- **Change:** with `--allow-warnings`, a bodyless lemma, a bodyless ghost function and `assume false;` (even inside a compiled method) translate and run: `dafny test` passes and `Main` runs, exit 0. Without `--allow-warnings` any warning aborts translation (W-D05, exit 3). Bodyless compiled methods and functions are hard errors in `test`/`run` (W-D06, exit 3) even with `{:axiom}`. So: (a) the learner loop may run `dafny test --allow-warnings` beside open **proof** holes; (b) a module with an open **exec** hole still cannot run any test, so the re-supply rule (exercise files import only given layers) and the given-only `dafny/checks/` units stay; (c) an exec-hole form that compiles is still open (DV0-REPORT §8). `assume {:axiom} P` in compiled code verifies, compiles and runs with the unproven assumption (spec row 31 confirmed).
- **Why:** D11, D1, TC3 (`dafny/D11-compile-unit-holes`, `dafny/D1-hole-kinds/*/test-py*`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 ("Holes block compilation of their whole compile unit"), §3.1 rule 3, §3.8.6 `value` (exec), §11.1 row 31, §11.5 item 7; design §14.1 (`dafny/checks/`), §14.2 rule 1.
- **Owner:** DV1b (units, `firsttry`), DV2 (first modules).

### Δ3 · Silent Dafny hole kinds the lint must count — adopted
- **Status:** refined on 2026-10-04. The given role (Δ97) allows a refined abstract module in a given layer or a declared statement file. "Audit is silent too" holds for a bodyless function whose result type is not a subset type: with a `nat` result, the subset constraint is an implicit postcondition and audit reports the declaration (`VERSIONS.md` §2.1, row D2; archive `dv1c/records/`).
- **Change:** these verify with exit 0 and no warning: a bodyless method without `ensures`; a bodyless lemma with only `requires`; a bodyless function without `ensures` (audit is silent too; only compilation refuses it); any bodyless member of an `abstract module` or a trait; `{:axiom}`/`@Axiom` lemmas or methods; `assume {:axiom}`; a `decreases *` method. The static hollow lint counts all of them, plus `{:verify false}`, `{:only}`/`@VerifyOnly`/`assert {:only}`, `expect` outside `{:test}`, `include`, `{:extern}`, and `abstract module`/trait in learner files.
- **Why:** D1-silent-kinds (`dafny/D1-hole-kinds/k04`, `k06`, `k08`–`k11`, `k15`, `k16`, `k18`, `k19`), G5, G17, G11.
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 (function holes; abstract-module trap), §11.4 G1–G5, G11; design §6.3 banned list.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ4 · What `dafny audit` misses; `vaudit` composition — adopted
- **Change:** `dafny audit` always exits 0 and never verifies. It reports 0 findings for bodyless lemmas in an abstract module (trait members are audited), for `expect` outside tests, for code reached by `include` (it is also non-transitive), and for `library` inputs. `vaudit` for Dafny = audit with every file a project root **plus** the Δ3 lint; any row anywhere fails.
- **Why:** D1-abstract-bodyless, G11, D2-include, G10 (`dafny/D1-hole-kinds/k10`, `k11`; `dafny/G11-expect-assumed`; `dafny/D2-audit-include-compare`; `dafny/D13-includes-given/lib-audit`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 3; spec §11.4 G3, G9–G11.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ5 · `@VerifyOnly`/`{:only}` warns twice; one `ensures` per hole confirmed — adopted
- **Change:** W-D16 is printed twice per attribute at the same position, so every warning counter dedupes by (file, line, column). The spec's one-`ensures`-per-hole rule stands: two `ensures` clauses give two warnings, a conjoined clause one; audit counts one row per declaration.
- **Why:** G5-verify-only, D12 (`dafny/D1-hole-kinds/k13`, `k20`; `dafny/D12-two-ensures`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 (one `ensures` per hole), §11.4 G5.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ6 · Scope of `--allow-axioms` — adopted
- **Change:** `--allow-axioms` silences W-D01/W-D02 **and** the `assume` warning W-D03; it does not silence `{:verify false}`, `@VerifyOnly`/`{:only}` or `{:extern}` warnings. It stays on the refusal list (Δ10). This settles the G6 "[verify, D1]".
- **Why:** D1-allow-axioms (`dafny/D1-hole-kinds/kNN/verify-ax`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3, §11.4 G6.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ7 · Verus hole counter — adopted
- **Change:** hole count = the `--no-cheating` error diagnostics **minus** the final `aborting due to N previous errors` line (W-V14), which `--error-format=json` also emits as a level-`error` diagnostic. Each cheat site gives exactly one diagnostic, all in one run, next to any real verification failures. Hole labels come only from `#[verifier::proof_note("<id>")] assume(false);` (reported in `failed_proof_notes` and as `note: <id>`); `proof_note` does not attach to `admit()`, so `admit()` is never a labelled hole.
- **Why:** NC-batch, P8, P7 (`verus/cheat-all_in_one`, `verus/P8-error-format-json`, `verus/P7-proof-note`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 (Port: Verus), §11.1 rows 1–2.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ8 · Verus spec-definition holes are counted by `--no-cheating` — adopted
- **Change:** `uninterp spec fn` and a bodyless `spec fn` (which also warns W-V18) are flagged W-V08 under `--no-cheating`. Only a `spec fn` whose body is `arbitrary()` passes silently; the lint covers that form.
- **Why:** NC-uninterp (`verus/cheat-uninterp_spec_fn`, `cheat-bodyless_spec_fn_no_uninterp`, `cheat-arbitrary_spec_fn`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.3 (last bullet: "not flagged by `--no-cheating`").
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ9 · Verus lint additions and catalog rows V6, V8–V10, V15 — adopted
- **Change:** green under `--no-cheating`, so the Verus lint must forbid them in learner and agent files: `vstd::pervasive::assume` (V15, confirmed green); `#[verifier::external_type_specification]`, with or without `external_body` (V6); `#[verifier::exec_allows_no_decreases_clause]` (V8, confirmed: a diverging body proves `1 + 1 == 3`); `#[verifier::external]` (V10); `arbitrary()`-bodied spec fns (Δ8); code outside `verus!` (V9: `verified: 0`, empty `func-details`). `external_trait_specification` and `external_fn_specification` are red (one W-V08 per trait method), so catalog row V6 splits: type spec green, trait and fn specs red.
- **Why:** P1, P3, P4, NC-extras, V9 (`verus/cheat-pervasive_assume`, `cheat-external_type_specification*`, `cheat-external_trait_specification`, `cheat-external_fn_specification`, `cheat-exec_allows_no_decreases_clause`, `cheat-external_attr`, `V9-outside-verus`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 V6, V8, V9, V10, V15; spec §11.1 row 27; design §6.3 banned list.
- **Owner:** DV1b (lint), DV1c (corpus).

## B. Done gates

### Δ10 · Dafny gate command line and refusal list — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** `vcheck --gate` always passes the explicit project path (never `--find-project`; a bare `dafny verify file.dfy` ignores a sibling `dfyconfig.toml`) and pins on the command line `--allow-warnings false --allow-axioms false --resource-limit <pin> --solver-path <pinned z3-4.12.1> --cores <n>`. Command-line values beat project `[options]`, `base` chains and a lying project `solver-path`, so the gate cannot be poisoned through a project file. It refuses project keys `base`, `solver-path`, `library`, `plugin`; the internal-only options (Δ17); `--boogie`; filters; `--no-verify` (rejected by `verify` anyway); `--verification-time-limit 0` (unbounded); and the legacy CLI. It re-checks the Dafny tree digest first, so the PATH-`z3` fallback (G22) cannot be reached.
- **Why:** D13-options, G21, D10, G15, G22, internal-options, G20, legacy-cli (`dafny/D13-project-options`, `dafny/G21-project-poisoning`, `dafny/D10-solver-path-project`, `dafny/G15-fake-solver`, `dafny/G22-path-z3`, `dafny/G20-timelimit-vs-rlimit`).
- **Touches:** design §7.1 (the verifier part), §11.1 cheat corpus; spec §11.1 rows 15, 18, 19, 32; spec §11.4 G6–G8, G10, G14–G16, G19–G22.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ11 · Gates check positive counts, never exit 0 alone — adopted
- **Status:** built as Δ96 (2026-10-04). The count pins live in `expected/<ID>.counts`; the learner's gate checks symbol presence and a floor, and only `vcheck --ref` checks equality with the twin.
- **Change:** these all exit 0: a Dafny filter that matches nothing (`0 verified, 0 errors`), a misspelled `{:tset}` test, a non-matching `--methods-to-test`, a program with no `Main`; Verus `--no-verify` on a failing file (`success: true`, `verified: 0`); Verus code outside `verus!` (`verified: 0`). Every gate and exit scorer pins, per unit or item, the expected Dafny task count (well-formedness plus correctness, from a reference run) or the Verus minimum `verified`, and the expected `PASSED` lines. Per-symbol Dafny scoring uses `--filter-symbol 'Mod.Name.'` (a trailing `.` makes it a suffix match).
- **Why:** G8, D4, G18, D5, V18-no-verify, V9 (`dafny/G8-filter-exit0`, `dafny/D4-filters-isolate`, `dafny/D5-test-run-py`, `verus/misc/no-verify-json`, `verus/V9-outside-verus`).
- **Touches:** design §7.1; spec §3.10 (port note), §11.1 rows 2 and 5, §11.4 G8, G18, V9, V18.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ12 · Verus done gate is one run; a missing JSON key fails — adopted
- **Status:** amended by Δ95 (2026-10-04): when the only holes are trusted dependency holes, the gate takes its record from a second run without `--no-cheating`.
- **Change:** done = one run of `verus <root> --crate-type=lib --rlimit <pin> --no-cheating --output-json`, requiring exit 0, `success` present and true, `is-verifying-entire-crate` true, `encountered-vir-error` false, `verified` ≥ the pinned minimum, plus the lint at 0. A clean crate returns the full record under `--no-cheating`, so the separate normal run is redundant. A missing key is a failure: partial (`--verify-*`) runs omit `success`, and a `--no-cheating` run that hits a cheat omits `verified`, `errors` and `is-verifying-entire-crate`.
- **Why:** NC-clean, JSON-partial, JSON-fail, V18-no-verify (`verus/misc/no-cheating-clean-json`, `verus/json-fields/*`, `verus/cheat-all_in_one/no-cheating-json`).
- **Touches:** design §7.1 ("[verify names]"); spec §11.1 row 2, §3.3 (the second run), §11.4 V18–V19.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ13 · Verus option and environment policy; exit-code map — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** the wrapper runs with a scrubbed environment (no `VERUS_Z3_PATH`, no `VERUS_EXTRA_ARGS`, no `RUSTFLAGS`) and refuses `--no-verify`, `-V no-solver-version-check`, `-V allow-inline-air`, `--no-lifetime`, `--no-erasure-check`, `--rlimit` other than the pin, and any `--smt-option` other than `smt.random_seed=<int>` (an unknown option panics with exit 134; a missing `=` exits 255). Exit codes: 0 ok; 1 verification or VIR error; 255 CLI usage error; 134 panic (bad SMT option, Z3 version mismatch); 101 cargo failure. A 134 is never a verdict.
- **Why:** P10, smt-seed, JSON-partial, P13 (`verus/P10-env-vars`, `verus/smt-seed`, `verus/json-fields/verify-*`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 V16–V18, V22, V24; spec §11.5 item 10.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ14 · Dafny exit codes and stage order — adopted
- **Change:** 0 ok (including audit with findings, no tests, no `Main`, an empty filter); 1 CLI error, or project `includes` matching nothing; 2 parse or resolution error, warnings without `--allow-warnings`, `--compare-report` mismatch or missing file, `find-dead-code` refusal; 3 translation abort (warnings, bodyless compiled declarations) **and** runtime test or `expect` failure; 4 verification error, out of resource, time out, and `Z3 is not found`. Exit 2 is ambiguous; read the message. A parse or resolution error anywhere stops verification of the whole program, and the summary (W-D13) names the first input, not the faulty file, so planted-error files hold one stage each.
- **Why:** exit-codes (census of 380 runs, `dafny/`; its one exit 124 is a harness `timeout` kill, `dafny/G20-timelimit-vs-rlimit/nl3-tl0-ext90`, not a Dafny code), D3 (`dafny/D3-resolution-then-verify`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 25.
- **Owner:** DV1b (wrapper), DV1c (planted files).

## C. Fake-green catalog and cheat corpus

### Δ15 · G10 (`--library` inputs) is loud, not silent — adopted
- **Change:** each `.dfy` passed as `library` raises W-D20, so the run exits 2 without `--allow-warnings`; the contents are still unverified (a planted wrong lemma goes unreported) and audit shows 0 findings. Row G10 reads "warning, exit 2; contents unverified". The `library` route is not a drop-in replacement for `includes` (Δ27).
- **Why:** G10-library (`dafny/D13-includes-given/lib-project`, `lib-audit`, `lib-vs-incl`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 G10.
- **Owner:** DV1c.

### Δ16 · G12 instruments — adopted
- **Change:** the public instrument for vacuous `requires` is `--analyze-proofs` (W-D22, exit 2). `--warn-contradictory-assumptions` exists but is internal-only. `dafny find-dead-code` works only on code inside a module with `{:testEntry}` methods (otherwise exit 2), and then reports every block of a contradictory-`requires` method as potentially unreachable (W-D31).
- **Why:** G12-analyze-proofs, find-dead-code (`dafny/G12-vacuous-requires`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 G12.
- **Owner:** DV1c (corpus), DV3b (forensics).

### Δ17 · Boogie and solver dumps are internal-only; std units cannot take `--boogie` — adopted
- **Change:** `--bprint`, `--solver-log`, `--print`, `--rprint`, `--pprint` and `--solver-option` appear only under `--help-internal` (35 internal-only verify options); `--boogie` is public. All go on the gate refusal list. Any `--boogie` value is refused next to `--standard-libraries` (exit 1), so std-tier units have no Boogie pass-through: seed variation there uses `dafny measure-complexity --random-seed S --mutations 1`, whose flags at 4.11.0 are `--random-seed`, `--mutations`, `--worst-amount` (no `--iterations`).
- **Why:** internal-options, measure-complexity (`dafny/help/verify-internal`, `dafny/measure-complexity`), spike `spike-dafny/toy-stdlib-options`.
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 33, §11.4 G14, G16, §11.5 item 2.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ18 · Legacy-CLI cheat confirmed — adopted
- **Change:** `dafny /compile:0` exits 0 on a bodyless lemma (new CLI: exit 2), exits 0 on a plain `assume false`, and `/allowAxioms:0` warns but still exits 0. Drop the "[verify]" on the design's cheat-corpus entry; `vcheck` refuses the legacy CLI.
- **Why:** legacy-cli (`dafny/legacy-cli`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (cheat corpus); spec §11.4 G19.
- **Owner:** DV1c.

### Δ19 · New vector V25: the cargo-verus cache — adopted
- **Change:** cargo-verus freshness is a blake3 content checksum that ignores forwarded Verus arguments, so after a green build `cargo verus verify -p X -- --no-cheating` on a crate holding `assume(false)` prints nothing and exits 0; `touch` does not invalidate it. Add V25 "cached cargo-verus result" (X05/X10) to the Verus catalog and the cheat corpus. Gates never trust a cached `cargo verus` result: they call `verus` directly per crate (Δ25), or use a dedicated target directory per flag set that is cleaned first.
- **Why:** P14 (`verus/P13-workspace/cache-no-cheating-after-verify`, `cache-after-touch`, `cache-fingerprint-inspect`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 (Verus table), §10.2; design §11.1 cheat corpus, §11.2 Lab 3 (gate v2).
- **Owner:** DV1b (gate), DV1c (corpus).

### Δ20 · New vector: a Cargo `[patch]` of the `verus!` macro crate — adopted
- **Change:** a `[patch.crates-io]` entry that replaces `verus_builtin_macros` (how `verus-spec-check` works) changes what the verifier sees. CI rejects `[patch]` sections and unknown `Cargo.toml` files in learner and agent trees, and the build-tool poisoning list gains this entry. `verus-spec-check` is at most an optional property-testing rung, never inside a gate (its README example does not verify on this pin); the course's own sampler uses plain `proptest` in `#[test]`s outside `verus!`.
- **Why:** verus-spec-check (`verus/verus-spec-check`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (build-tool poisoning), §13.2 rule 2g, §14.5; spec §11.1 row 14, §11.5 item 13.
- **Owner:** DV1c.

### Δ21 · V24: which Verus environment variables act — adopted
- **Change:** this release ignores `VERUS_EXTRA_ARGS`. It honours `VERUS_Z3_PATH`, and checks the Z3 version inside the SMT session (it never runs `z3 --version`), so a wrapper script that execs the right Z3 passes silently; a wrong Z3 panics (W-V25, exit 134) unless `-V no-solver-version-check`, which then uses it silently. The wrapper scrubs both variables anyway (Δ13).
- **Why:** P10 (`verus/P10-env-vars`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.4 V24, §11.1 row 19.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

## D. Wiring and build units

### Δ22 · Verus module crates depend on the layer chain — adopted
- **Status:** the layer crates moved to DV2 (Δ94, 2026-10-04); the dependency rule stands.
- **Change:** a module crate depends on `vstd`, its tier's layer crate **and every lower layer whose items appear in the specs it imports**; otherwise Verus fails with W-V21 (`` `qg_l1::base` is not supported ``). Layer crates list the whole chain below them too. Later layers stay unreachable (rustc E0433), so the anti-cheat property of design §10.1 holds. The checker group `units` compares Cargo dependencies with the chain, not with one crate.
- **Why:** P13b (`verus/P13-workspace/chain-dep-bare-verify-json`, `P13b-later-layer-call`).
- **Touches:** design §10.1 (last paragraph: "each module crate on exactly its tier's layer"), §14.2 rule 1, §14.3 group `units`.
- **Owner:** DV1a (checker), DV1b (crates).

### Δ23 · `default-members` narrows a bare `cargo verus verify` — adopted
- **Change:** with `default-members`, a bare `cargo verus verify` verifies only the default members and their dependencies (design §14.2 rule 4 holds). The guide's "all `verify = true` crates" reading in spec §10.2 does not apply. An `exclude`d `Solutions` crate is never reached, by a bare run, by `--workspace`, or by `-p solutions` (W-V23, exit 101); `--manifest-path Solutions/Cargo.toml` verifies it in its own target directory and re-verifies vstd (about 15–20 s).
- **Why:** P13 (`verus/P13-workspace/bare-verify`, `p-solutions-from-root`, `solutions-manifest-path`).
- **Touches:** spec §10.2 ("Why the reference crate sits outside"), §11.1 row 15; design §14.2 rule 4.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ24 · cargo-verus forwarding, argument order and `--workspace` — adopted
- **Change:** when forwarding `--no-cheating` through cargo-verus, always add `--fwd-verus-args-to roots` (the default forwards to vstd too: 72 errors). Cargo options that cargo-verus needs (`-p`, `--target-dir`, `--manifest-path`) go before any other cargo option, or it exits 1 (W-V24). `--workspace` verifies every member and holds one vstd in `Cargo.lock`; without `--keep-going` it stops at the first failing crate; it prints one JSON document per crate with no crate label, so a parser attributes documents by `func-details` prefix. `cargo verus new` runs `git init`, so project scaffolding removes `.git`.
- **Why:** P14, WS, P6-new (`verus/P13-workspace/fresh-target-no-cheating-*`, `workspace-flag-fresh-json*`, `cargo-verus-arg-order-error`; `verus/P6-cargo-test/new`).
- **Touches:** design §14.2 rule 5.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ25 · Direct `verus` chain across layer crates — adopted
- **Change:** building layer k with `verus` directly needs `--import` of **every** lower layer's `.vir` (not only k−1) and `--extern` of the crate directly below. Export with `--export <PATH>` plus `--crate-name`: the documented `--export CRATENAME=PATH` writes a file literally named `mylib=mylib.vir`. This direct chain uses the release's bundled vstd, cargo-verus the crates.io vstd (not byte-compared; DV0-REPORT §8).
- **Why:** P9, spike `spike-verus/run3/import-transitive-missing`, `import-transitive-full` (`verus/P9-export-vir`).
- **Touches:** spec §10.2 notes (fast channel calls `verus` directly); design §14.2 rule 1.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ26 · An exported `.vir` is a statement leak — adopted
- **Change:** a reference crate's `.vir` holds public signatures, `requires`/`ensures` spans and open spec bodies, but no proof-fn, exec or closed-spec bodies. Classify it as a statement leak (not an answer leak) and keep `target/**/*.vir` on the read-deny and build-output lists with the rest of `target/`.
- **Why:** P9 (`verus/P9-export-vir`).
- **Touches:** spec §7.2 (port side channels, artifacts row); design §13.2 rule 1d.
- **Owner:** DV1a.

### Δ27 · Dafny units reach `given/`; given layers re-verify per unit — adopted
- **Change:** a per-tier `dfyconfig.toml` with `includes = ['*.dfy', '../../../given/Q/*.dfy']` (or the 2-level form) works and cross-file module imports resolve, so design §14.2 needs no per-(track, tier) fallback. Given files are roots, so they are re-verified in every unit (CI cost grows with units × given size). A wrong-depth glob matches nothing silently and surfaces only as `module X does not exist` (exit 2); `includes` matching nothing exits 1. The generator checks every glob matches at least one file. The `.doo` route for given layers is unprobed (DV0-REPORT §8).
- **Why:** D13 (`dafny/D13-includes-given`).
- **Touches:** design §14.2 rule 1 (D13 "[verify]"; fallback); spec §10.1 notes.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ28 · `dafny server` project scoping — adopted
- **Change:** the server uses the nearest `dfyconfig.toml`, resolves the whole project and verifies only opened files; tier subdirectories `core/` and `std/` are separate projects (the same module name in both is no error); a file with no project is checked alone. `workspace/symbol` returns symbols only from projects with an open file, so reference symbols appear only if a reference file is opened. This is server behaviour; the VS Code client was not probed.
- **Why:** D14, D8-workspace-symbols (`dafny/D14-D8-lsp-scoping/s1`–`s3`, `s5-workspace-symbols`).
- **Touches:** design §14.2 rule 1 (D14 "[verify]"); spec §9.6 (language server lesson).
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ29 · The specimen extension holds on the server side — adopted
- **Change:** opening a `*.dfy.specimen` URI in `dafny server` yields only W-D28 and never verifies the content, so the specimen design stands on the server side. Whether the VS Code extension associates the extension with Dafny is still open (DV2, GUI).
- **Why:** D8-server (`dafny/D14-D8-lsp-scoping/s4-open-specimen`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (specimens "[verify: the IDE extension's file association]").
- **Owner:** DV1b (`pins.py`), DV2 (client check).

### Δ30 · Per-unit Dafny knobs cannot live in module `{:options}` — adopted
- **Change:** module `{:options}` rejects `--manual-lemma-induction`, `--manual-triggers`, `--disable-nonlinear-arithmetic`, `--allow-axioms`, `--allow-warnings`, `--resource-limit`, `--solver-path` and `--isolate-assertions` as parse errors (W-D26, exit 2); among those tried only `--function-syntax` and `--quantifier-syntax` are accepted. Per-unit knobs go in each generated unit's `dfyconfig.toml` `[options]` (from `dafny/options/<tier>.toml`), or on declarations (`{:induction false}`); the generator emits them and the checker verifies them.
- **Why:** module-options (`dafny/escape-hatches/modopt-*`), spike `spike-dafny/toy-module-options`.
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 30; design §14.1 (`dafny/options/`), §14.2 rule 3.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ31 · `{:disableNonlinearArithmetic}` leaks into later modules — adopted
- **Change:** the module attribute also turns nonlinear arithmetic off in every module declared after it in the same file (not earlier modules, not other files). Layer files put nonlinear-off modules last, or attribute every module explicitly (the L4 spike does the latter).
- **Why:** spike `spike-dafny/toy-nl-modattr`.
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 30; design §10.1.
- **Owner:** DV2, DV3b (layer authors).

### Δ32 · Every Dafny compile runs in a scratch copy — adopted
- **Change:** `dafny run`/`test --target py` writes `<stem>-py/` beside the source, so every compile in `pins.py`, check units and CI runs from a scratch copy; otherwise generated reference code lands beside reference sources. Add `*-py/` to the build-output reach list.
- **Why:** toolchain/dafny-run-py, D5.
- **Touches:** spec §7.2 (artifacts row), §3.8.6 (harness); design §13.2 rule 1d.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ33 · `dafny server` writes into the pinned install — adopted
- **Status:** DV1b took the first option (Δ98): `dafny server` runs from a scratch copy of the install, so the pinned tree stays clean. The log file name differs under `LANG=C.UTF-8` (`VERSIONS.md` §1.1).
- **Change:** every `dafny server` start writes an empty `DafnyLanguageServerLog<M_D_YYYY> <h:mm:ss> AM.txt` into `.toolchain/dafny-4.11.0/dafny/`, which breaks `get_toolchain.sh dafny --check` and the CI toolchain check. Either the IDE and `pins.py` LSP runs use a separate copy of the install, or `get_toolchain.sh` ignores and deletes exactly that file pattern before hashing. The "writes nothing into the tree" claim in `VERSIONS.md` is corrected.
- **Why:** D14-server-writes-install (`dafny/D14-D8-lsp-scoping/server-side-effect-logs`).
- **Touches:** design §14.4 (`verify` job, toolchain sha), §14.5; `scripts/get_toolchain.sh`.
- **Owner:** DV1b (the infra lane, which owns `scripts/`).

### Δ34 · Dafny std tier for L3/L4; the recorded `Std` module list — adopted
- **Change:** `--standard-libraries` works with `--target py` and does not re-verify the library. The Dafny L3 and L4 layers import `Std`, so every unit that imports them sets `standard-libraries = true`; Q01–Q03 units that import only L1/L2 stay core tier. The module list recorded in `VERSIONS.md` §2.1 seeds the Dafny import-tier lint.
- **Why:** stdlib (`dafny/stdlib-modules`), spike `spike-dafny/ref-verify`.
- **Touches:** design §10.1 (L3 "std-tier helper"), §7.3 (library rule), §14.1; spec §2.9.
- **Owner:** DV1b (lint), DV3a.

### Δ35 · `Std.Arithmetic.LittleEndianNat` is a near-answer — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** the standard library's `LittleEndianNat` instantiates at `BASE = 2^64` in a two-line refinement (only `BASE` is re-verified), and it holds near-answers for two L4/L5 spine items (pairing in `Solutions/spikes/AUTHOR-NOTES.md`). The std-tier import allowlist excludes `Std.Arithmetic.LittleEndianNat` from BigNat files, unless the course uses it deliberately as a search-then-name item.
- **Why:** spike `spike-dafny/std-le-shortcut`.
- **Touches:** design §10.1, §7.3 (library rule); spec §2.9.
- **Owner:** DV1b (lint), DV3a (decides the search-then-name use).

## E. Golden harness and item formats

### Δ36 · Dafny JSON channel and normalization — adopted
- **Change:** `--json-output` exists for `verify` and `test` (not `audit`, which exits 1): JSON Lines `{type: diagnostic|status|error, value}`; resolver messages are `{0}` templates with an `arguments` array, which `pins.py` fills in; under `test`, `PASSED`/`FAILED` lines and verification errors arrive as status text. `--use-basename-for-filename` shortens text locations and JSON `filename`/`filePath`, but JSON `uri` stays absolute, so normalization strips it. Text locations are 1-based line, 0-based column. Diagnostics come in a stable order that is not source order (top-level members before nested modules), so `pins.py` sorts them. Dafny writes everything to stdout.
- **Why:** D6, basename-normalization, determinism, output-streams, D1a (`dafny/D6-json-output`, `dafny/escape-hatches/path-basename`, `dafny/misc-spec-rows/json-basename`, `dafny/determinism`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (normalization), §11.1 row 6.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ37 · Verus JSON channel and normalization — adopted
- **Change:** `--error-format=json` writes one rustc JSON object per line to stderr; the summary and `--output-json` go to stdout. A `proof_note` is an extra primary span labelled `note: <id>`; `--expand-errors` is a separate level-`note` diagnostic. `func-details` lists every `verus!` item (spec, axiom and external fns too), every imported dependency item and two `vstd::function::axiom_fn_mut_call_*` entries; it is empty when the crate has no `verus!` items; its key order and the order of both note lists change from run to run. Key spellings: `func-details`, `verification-results` (hyphens), `obligation_proof_notes`, `failed_proof_notes` (underscores), and `mode:` with a trailing colon under `--time`. `pins.py` sorts keys and note lists, drops the `aborting due to` diagnostic, and drops `--triggers` notes whose span is in a vstd file.
- **Why:** P8, P7, P11, JSON-pass, triggers (`verus/P8-error-format-json`, `verus/P7-proof-note`, `verus/P11-determinism`, `verus/json-fields/pass-time`, `verus/triggers`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (normalization), §11.1 row 6.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ38 · Obligation specimens: failed asserts are assumed afterwards — adopted
- **Change:** Dafny assumes a failed assertion for the rest of the body: six failing `assert x == k` lines give only 2 errors (also with `--error-limit 0`, and `4 verified, 2 errors` with `--isolate-assertions`). Each labelled obligation in an `obligation` specimen sits in its own lemma or method, or is logically independent of every earlier label, or the answer key is wrong; the spec's worked `obligation` shape is re-checked against this before use. Verus lists every failing `proof_note` label with `--multiple-errors 2` (the default) and only the first with `0`; the harness compares sets.
- **Why:** assume-after-failure, P7-notes-assert (`dafny/determinism/errlimit-*`, `verus/P7-proof-note/asserts-json*`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (`obligation` row and worked shape).
- **Owner:** DV1b (harness), DV2 (first specimens).

### Δ39 · What Dafny's summary counts — adopted
- **Change:** `N verified` from `dafny verify` counts verification tasks: one per symbol per check kind (well-formedness, correctness) that has obligations; bodyless declarations add 0; `--isolate-assertions` and `{:isolate_assertions}` count assertion batches. `--filter-position file:LINE` switches the unit to `N assertions verified` (W-D08), and a line inside a body matches only with `--isolate-assertions`. `dafny test` counts symbols (129 tasks vs 67 symbols on the same sources). Goldens never compare counts across commands.
- **Why:** D4, verified-count-unit (`dafny/D4-filters-isolate`, `dafny/D13-includes-given/v1-textlog`), spike `spike-dafny/test-py-symbols`.
- **Touches:** spec §3.10 (port note), §11.1 row 22.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ40 · What Verus's `verified` counts — adopted
- **Change:** one unit per function body with obligations (proof fns, exec fns, trait impl methods, consts, recursive spec fns), plus one per loop, plus one per `assert … by (nonlinear_arith)`; non-recursive spec fns, `external_body`, axiom and uninterp fns, closures and code outside `verus!` add nothing. `errors` counts failing functions, not diagnostics. Per-item minimum counts (Δ11) are computed in these units.
- **Why:** P5 (`verus/P5-verified-unit`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.6 P5, §11.1 row 2.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ41 · Verus per-symbol channel — adopted
- **Change:** the fast channel is `verus --verify-only-module M --verify-function F` (or `--verify-root` for root-module functions). `--verify-module` with `--verify-function` is refused (W-V15, exit 255) and `--verify-function` alone is refused (W-V16), contrary to `verus --help`. Exact names win over substring matches; an ambiguous name errors (W-V17) and impl methods need `*::name`. Partial runs omit `success`, so a gate-mode wrapper fails them by construction (Δ12).
- **Why:** JSON-partial (`verus/json-fields/verify-*`), spike `spike-verus/run3/verify-function-name-*`.
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 22, §3.10 (port note).
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ42 · Verus value slots and tests — adopted
- **Change:** `cargo test <name>` runs `#[test]`s outside `verus!` on the pinned 1.98.1 and on the host's 1.92.0; ship a `rust-toolchain.toml` anyway, because `cargo verus new` writes none. `verus --compile --test -o <bin>` verifies first, then builds a libtest binary with the same results, so checks prefer it. Tests sit outside `verus!` or carry `#[verifier::external]`; inside `verus!` without it, `--compile --test` fails with W-V20, whose help text suggests writing an `assume_specification` (a cheat the chapter must warn about). An `external_body` stub panics `not implemented` only when reached.
- **Why:** P6, P6-new (`verus/P6-cargo-test`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (`value` exec row), §11.1 row 5; design §14.5 (run row).
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ43 · Dafny value slots and tests — adopted
- **Change:** `dafny test` prints `<Qualified.Name>: PASSED|FAILED`, a tab-indented `file(l,c): <message>` line and `[Program halted] … Test failures occurred: see above.` (W-D21); a failing test exits 3. `--methods-to-test` is a regex on qualified names, and a non-matching regex or a unit with no tests exits 0 silently. `method {:tset}` is silently never run, while `@Tset` is a resolution error. Value-slot checks count the expected `PASSED` lines and the course uses `@Test`.
- **Why:** D5, G18 (`dafny/D5-test-run-py`).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (`value` exec row), §11.1 row 5, §11.4 G18.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ44 · Statement freeze by refinement: constraints — adopted
- **Status:** Δ154 (DV3b, 2026-10-06) sets the form of a refined lemma that is a hole: it keeps the skeleton form below (no restated `requires`) with the body `{ assume false; }`. The constraints stand.
- **Change:** a refining lemma may not add **or restate** a `requires` (W-D19), so learner skeletons use `lemma Name ... { }` or restate only `ensures`. Parent `ensures` stay enforced (a weakened child fails, exit 4). A concrete refinement that leaves a body out re-emits the parent's hole warning, so "refinement result concrete" is a working instrument. An `{:axiom}` child lemma is silent in `verify`, so audit and lint also run over the refined module.
- **Why:** D7 (`dafny/D7-refinement`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 row 29, §11.6 D7; design §7.4 Q08 (refinement predict).
- **Owner:** DV1b (`specfreeze`), DV3b (Q08).

## F. Automation and the empty-body gate

### Δ45 · Manual lemma induction from Q03 on; the empty-body gate strips levers — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03: manual lemma induction from Q03 on; Q02 keeps auto-induction for its predict.
- **Change:** Dafny's default auto-induction closes many multi-line items with an empty body: 24 of 39 BigNat spike items verify with `{}` at maximal automation at the 5M spike limit (25 counting the 50M escalation or seed 2), including 12- and 17-line references and non-recursive call chains, and D9's toys show the same. On every item `{:induction false}` and `--manual-lemma-induction` give the same empty-body verdict, and the whole reference verifies under global `--manual-lemma-induction`. So: from Q03 on, each unit sets `manual-lemma-induction = true` in its `[options]` (or the hole lemma carries `{:induction false}`); Q02 keeps auto-induction for its predict item. The CI empty-body gate first strips the baked levers and runs with auto-induction on (maximal automation), then with the item's lever; a hole passes the gate only if the empty body fails in its shipped configuration under all 3 seeds.
- **Why:** D9, induction (`dafny/D9-empty-body-induction`), spike `spike-dafny/gate`, `gateseeds`, `ref-whole-manual-lemma-induction`.
- **Touches:** design §7.3 (empty-body gate), §6.3 (hand-first: `d:induction-auto`), §16 (risk row); spec §2.8, §11.1 row 12, §11.5 item 9.
- **Owner:** DV1b (gate), DV2 and DV3a (units).

### Δ46 · Dafny levers that work on the spine; fuel dropped — adopted
- **Change:** on the Dafny spine the working levers are `{:induction false}` (13 items) and nonlinear arithmetic off (2 arithmetic-library items). Opacity works only together with `{:induction false}`: with an opaque function but auto-induction on, a reveal-only answer closed the item. Seven L1/L4 helpers are reveal drills (SMT closes them; made opaque, a reveal-only answer suffices), and 3 are not holes at all. Fuel is not a usable lever: `{:fuel F,0,0}` and `{:fuel F,0,1}` make the reference itself fail on 23 and 22 of 25 items, and `{:fuel F,1,1}` is a resolution error ("high value == 1 + low value"). Drop "low fuel" from the spine's lever list.
- **Why:** spike `spike-dafny/gate`, `summary.json`, `toy-fuel-syntax`.
- **Touches:** design §16 (risk row "`{:induction false}`, `opaque`, low fuel"); spec §11.5 item 9, §11.1 row 12.
- **Owner:** DV2, DV3a, DV3b (layer and hole authors).

### Δ47 · Verus empty-body gate modes; `integer_ring` removed — adopted
- **Status:** E0 amended by Δ182 (DV5a, 2026-10-07): E0, E0H, E1, E2, EC and EM keep `let tracked` statements (QPLAN §30 Q65(b)); ghost lets, asserts, proof blocks and invariants are still stripped.
- **Change:** the Verus empty-body gate tries these modes on each hole: empty proof (E0) and E0 at 10× the rlimit; E0 plus `broadcast use` of the vstd seq groups (E1), of the light arithmetic groups (E2), of the course's own group (EC), and of `group_mul_properties` (EM, memory-heavy, Δ57); a one-line whole-goal `nonlinear_arith` or `bit_vector` assert (NL, BV); invariants dropped (INV); and a trivial body (TRIV) to show exec specs are not vacuous. `by (integer_ring)` is rejected at the pin (W-V10), so the `integer-ring` slug leaves the Verus WRITE/RECOGNISE list and the gate's knob list.
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3` (gate tool docstring in `spike-verus/_tools/gate3.py`), integer-ring (`verus/misc/integer-ring-no-singular`).
- **Touches:** spec §11.1 rows 11–12, §2.8; design §7.3.
- **Owner:** DV1a (slug list), DV1b (gate).

### Δ48 · L3 `weight` is opaque — adopted
- **Change:** in the Verus L3 layer, `weight` is `#[verifier::opaque]`. While it was transparent, one extras item planned for Q10 verified with an empty proof in three of the gate modes, 3/3 seeds, so it was not a hole; with `weight` opaque, every mode fails. Which modes, and what closed the item, is in the author notes (`Solutions/spikes/AUTHOR-NOTES.md`). Opacity costs one `reveal` line in each affected proof and fits Q06's opaque-plus-reveal unit.
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3/lever-opaque-weight`, `pre-lever-transparent-weight`.
- **Touches:** design §10.1 (L3 row).
- **Owner:** DV3a.

### Δ49 · Prover-choice items are cards or predicts, not budgeted holes — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** two Verus drills in the extras crate fail every empty-proof mode only because the prover they need is off by default; each closes with a single prover choice. They become "which prover?" predict items (or cards) in the prover units of Q04 and Q07, not minute-budgeted holes. Which item needs which prover is in the author notes (`Solutions/spikes/AUTHOR-NOTES.md`), never in learner-visible files.
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3/summary/table-rl2.txt`, `gate-rl2.json`, `gate-rl2-EC-BV.json` (the two extras proof fns listed in DV0-REPORT §5.3 as predict items).
- **Touches:** design §7.4 (the prover WRITE units).
- **Owner:** DV3a, DV3b.

## G. BigNat spine and module content

### Δ50 · No BigNat fallback; full multiplication exists in both languages — adopted
- **Status:** L4, built as QPLAN §14 plans it (Δ158, 2026-10-06), holds multiplication by one limb, as design §10.1's L4 row asks, and no full multiplication. Full multiplication stays a candidate for L5–L7 or a capstone, and it is the first candidate for DV5's re-plan of Q16 (QPLAN §14.7 F12–F13).
- **Change:** BigNat L1–L4 are complete in both languages, 3-seed stable, and every hole fails its empty-body gate. Full multiplication verifies in Dafny (`MulCorrect`, 14 proof lines) and in Verus (`mul` with `shift_limb`, 37 lines), so the fallbacks "L4 multiplies by one limb" and "Verus-only `mul_limb`" are not triggered, and the single-file Verus fallback is not needed (one crate per layer verifies both directly and through `cargo verus verify --workspace`). Full multiplication is a candidate L5–L7 or capstone item.
- **Why:** spikes (`spike-dafny/summary.json`, `spike-verus/run3/summary/`, `spike-verus/run3/cargo-verus-workspace-3`).
- **Touches:** design §4 (Artifacts), §7.5 (gated items), §10.1.
- **Owner:** DV5.

### Δ51 · Dafny L1 content and Q01's holes — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** Dafny L1 ships only the definitions and the worked lemma, as design §10.1 says; the spike's other L1 helper lemmas move to L2 as re-supplied given code. L1 offers only one gate-passing hole (`ToNatBound`, with `{:induction false}`); the rest are reveal drills or not holes. Q01's 24 hole-minutes need items outside the spine layer (or Verus L1 items).
- **Why:** spike `spike-dafny/summary.json` (L1 rows).
- **Touches:** design §10.1 (L1 row), §7.5 (Q01 "holes 24").
- **Owner:** DV2.

### Δ52 · Verus `add_limb` hole is invariant-only — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** Verus `add_limb` (L2 target, Q02–Q03) is above the intro band (V ≤ 15): 21 proof lines with its lift lemma given, 35 with the lemma invented. The Q02/Q03 hole is the loop invariant alone (10 lines; the INV gate fails 3/3), with the lift lemma and ghost carry updates given; the full proof is re-tiered to practice. Dafny `AddLimbCorrect` (12 lines) is just above the Dafny intro band (≤ 10) and needs the same check.
- **Why:** spikes (`spike-verus/run3/summary/bands.json`, `spike-dafny/summary.json`).
- **Touches:** design §7.4 (Q02–Q03), §7.5 (bands), §10.1 (L2 row).
- **Owner:** DV2.

### Δ53 · Line-band mismatches in Q13, Q14 and Q16 — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03 as authoring guidance for DV2 and DV5; the owning phase re-measures the bands on the real holes. Its proof-line count is the one line-count rule for every item of Q10–Q18 and of the R track (Δ173); Q16's H3 is banded as one problem (Δ178).
- **Change:** measured against the admissibility bands (design §7.5): Q13 "normalize idempotent (D)" is 3 lines against 10–30; bundling it as "normalize gives the unique canonical numeral" gives about 27. Q16 H1 "generalize before induction (D)" via the add carry is about 23 against 30–80. Q16 H2 "`cmp` with invented invariant (V)" is 21 lines (34 with the bound lemmas invented; 39 for a non-canonical `cmp_any` variant) against 45–120: re-tier it to intermediate or pair it with another target. Q16 H3 Dafny mirror is 26 proof lines (about 43 counting the code), in band only if code counts or full multiplication (+14) is added; Verus `mul_limb` (56) is in band. Q14's refinement item (D) is about 10, at the bottom of its band. Q13 "sub with borrow (V)" is 38 as shipped (in band); an earlier version measured 47 (above the band), so its band status depends on proof style (author notes, `Solutions/spikes/AUTHOR-NOTES.md`).
- **Why:** spikes (`spike-dafny/summary.json`, `spike-verus/run3/summary/bands.json`, `lines.json`).
- **Touches:** design §7.5 (Q13, Q14, Q16 lines; gated items).
- **Owner:** DV5 (admissibility), DV2 for any intro item.

### Δ54 · Verus API shape and `&mut` syntax at the pin — adopted
- **Change:** a layer crate cannot add inherent methods to L1's `BigNat` (rustc E0390), so L2–L4 targets are free functions (`normalize(&mut a)`, `add(&a, &b)`, …) or `NatLike` trait methods, and Q05's `&mut self` material uses a trait or a type the module owns. A bare `&mut` parameter in a postcondition is an error (W-V22); specs write `old(a)`/`final(a)`, and chapters and cards use that form.
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3/orphan-inherent-impl`, `mutref-plain`.
- **Touches:** design §10.1 (L2–L4 Verus column), §7.4 (Q05 "`&mut self` + `Result`").
- **Owner:** DV2, DV3a.

### Δ55 · vstd numeric specs available at the pin — adopted
- **Change:** vstd `0.0.0-2026-09-20-0158` specifies `u64` `checked_add`, `wrapping_add`, `checked_mul` and `wrapping_mul` (plus sub, shl, shr); it has no `overflowing_add`, `carrying_add` or `widening_mul` (W-V21 form). `(a as u128) * (b as u128)` needs a one-line bound hint, and `u128` → `u64` lo/hi casts do not prove the div/mod facts even with `#[verifier::truncate]`. Carry code uses the checked/wrapping forms or `u128`; any spec the course trusts instead needs a `TRUST.md` entry. This settles design §10.1's "[verify vstd specs]".
- **Why:** vstd-u64 (`verus/vstd-specs`).
- **Touches:** design §10.1.
- **Owner:** DV2, DV3b.

## H. Resource limits, memory and determinism

### Δ56 · Course resource-limit pins — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03. Course pins: Dafny `--resource-limit 5000000`, Verus `--rlimit 2`.
- **Change:** the course pins Dafny `--resource-limit 5000000` and Verus `--rlimit 2` (Z3 rlimit 6,000,000 per query; `--rlimit N` = 3,000,000 × N) for gates, references, CI and goldens, with `--cores` (Dafny) and `--num-threads` (Verus) always explicit. Headroom over the costliest reference: Dafny 13.3× (374,999 under seed 1), Verus about 14.9× (402,506, `lemma_sub_lift` in the extras crate, unseeded run; the seeded maximum over seeds 1–3 is 369,430, `add`). The DV0 probe pins (1,000,000 and `--rlimit 10`) were probe settings; any golden that will be quoted and that involves a resource outcome is re-captured at the course pin. DV1b records the adopted pins in `VERSIONS.md` §2.5.
- **Why:** rl-calibration (`dafny/rl-calibration`), spikes (`spike-dafny/rlimit-units`, `seed-1`..`3`; `spike-verus/run3/rlimit-unit-*`, `ref`, `summary/ref-rl2.json`, `final-verify-sh-json/outputs`).
- **Touches:** design §6.5, §11.1 (apparatus), §14.5; spec §6.5, §11.1 row 32.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ57 · Memory caps for Verus jobs — adopted
- **Change:** `broadcast use vstd::arithmetic::mul::group_mul_properties` in the BigNat context drove z3 to 6.7–14.0 GiB maximum RSS per job before `--rlimit 2` fired (43.3–105.5 s; 17 of the 28 EM jobs, seed 1), and to 6.8 GiB on a 2-line lemma with only that group added; the div/mod and pow groups stayed near 0.3 GiB. An rlimit bounds work, not memory. `vcheck`, CI and the agent sandbox run every Verus job under a per-job cgroup memory cap, budgeting at least 16 GiB per job (the measured peak, 14,692,492 KiB, is 15.0 GB) wherever learner or agent code may broadcast that group; a memory kill is recorded as `killed(memory)`, never as a verdict. This also makes a Q04 hazard predict.
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3/group-cost-mul`, `group-cost-divmod`, `group-cost-pow`, `summary/gate-rl2-EM.json`; `CLAUDE.md` §Resources.
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (budgets), §13.3 (sandbox), §14.4 (CI runner sizing).
- **Owner:** DV1b, DV1c, DV4.

### Δ58 · Wall-clock kill as a safety net, counted as failure — adopted
- **Change:** `assert(..) by (bit_vector)` on a `Seq`-valued goal spins the Verus front end at 100% CPU with no z3 child for more than 600 s; the rlimit cannot bound it. Every harness keeps a wall-clock kill switch as a safety net, and a killed run counts as a failure, never a success; add the case to the cheat and timeout corpus. Verdicts still come only from pinned resource limits, and `--verification-time-limit 0` (Dafny; unbounded) stays refused (Δ10).
- **Why:** spike `spike-verus/run3/bv-seq-goal-hang`; G20 (`dafny/G20-timelimit-vs-rlimit`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 ("resource limits, never wall-clock, are pinned"); spec §11.5 item 2.
- **Owner:** DV1b, DV4.

### Δ59 · Determinism, seeds and resource-count goldens — adopted
- **Change:** at a pinned limit, Dafny output is byte-identical across runs and `--cores` values, and Verus stdout/stderr are byte-identical over 5 runs (only JSON key order varies), so text goldens are viable and need no `--cores` pin. Plain `dafny verify` resource counts are not fully reproducible (2 of 129 symbols differed over 3 fresh runs) while seeded `measure-complexity` counts are, so any golden that quotes a Dafny resource count comes from a seeded run. Verus `--smt-option smt.random_seed=N` changes rlimit counts, not verdicts, on small files; the brittleness metric is the per-function rlimit from `--time` JSON. Dafny's `--cores` default is 16 on this host, so every Dafny command passes `--cores` (memory, `CLAUDE.md`).
- **Why:** determinism, P11, smt-seed (`dafny/determinism`, `verus/P11-determinism`, `verus/smt-seed`), spike `spike-dafny/determinism`.
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.6 (goldens), §11.5 item 2.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

## I. Agent apparatus and sandbox

### Δ60 · Docker access route for the sandbox — decided (user, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** decided by the user on 2026-10-03: **re-add the `docker` group** (`sudo usermod -aG docker leobwang`, then a fresh login; a host change the user makes). `mksandbox-run` calls plain `docker`. Under the tripwire threat model this needs no further hardening; H2 stays optional. Δ153 (DV4, 2026-10-06) narrows the routes for the course's scripts: no script calls `sudo` or `sg`, so `sudo -n docker` is a route for none of them; the group decision stands. On 2026-10-06 it was not yet carried out: the group listed no member (DV4's host action H1; Δ156).
- **Change:** the DV0 lanes' session reached Docker through a stale `docker` group membership (gid 983) from before the 2026-10-03 removal; `/etc/group` lists no members, and a fresh login is denied the socket. Design §13.3's "Docker 29.1.3 works" holds only for such sessions. `sg docker` is not a route: it worked only in that session, and from a session without the stale gid it fails (`Password: Invalid password.`, exit 1; `dv0-adversarial/rerun/infra_sg-docker-version`). Under D5 the user picks the route `mksandbox-run` uses: `sudo -n docker` (works now, also without the stale gid: `dv0-adversarial/rerun/infra_sudo-n-docker-version`), re-adding the group, or rootless Docker (needs the `uidmap` system package; then a test against `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1` and of host loopback). H2 stays optional; nothing here requires a host change for the course itself.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/docker-identity`, `docker-fresh-login-groups`, `sudo-n-docker-version`, `rootless-tooling`, `rootless-setuptool-check-force`).
- **Touches:** design §13.3 (opening paragraph), §3 D5, §4 H2.
- **Owner:** the user, before DV4.

### Δ61 · Sandbox network: internal network plus gateway forwarder — adopted
- **Status:** amended by Δ146 (DV4, 2026-10-06): an HTTP-aware Ollama gate replaces the plain forwarder, and Route B (no network in the container; the gate on a Unix socket) is the plan of record, subject to DV4's step 2c probe. This entry's pattern, with its gateway closed by a filter (Route A), is used only if Route B fails that probe. Δ146 also records a hazard this probe did not test: the internal network's gateway is the host. The probe results stand.
- **Change:** Ollama listens only on `127.0.0.1:11434`, so a bridge container using `--add-host host.docker.internal:host-gateway` is refused. The probed pattern: per run, `docker network create --internal`, plus a host-side forwarder or allowlist proxy bound only on that network's gateway IP, forwarding to `127.0.0.1:11434` and logging each connection; the container reaches Ollama and nothing else (no external hosts, no docker0). Never `--network host` (it reaches Ollama with no isolation). `mksandbox-run` creates and removes the network per run. The HTTPS allowlist for Claude runs is still unprobed.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/ollama-listen`, `docker-hostgw-ollama-bridge-v2`, `docker-internal-net-forwarder`, `docker-hostnet-ollama`, `docker-run-network-none`).
- **Touches:** design §13.3 item 3.
- **Owner:** DV4.

### Δ62 · Container-tool tripwire covers `sudo docker` — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03.
- **Change:** `sudo -n docker` reaches the daemon (the account has `(ALL) NOPASSWD: ALL`), so rule 1f's container-tool patterns also match `sudo docker`, `sudo -n docker` and `sg docker` (already listed), each with a must-block fixture. This stays a tripwire against accidents, as the hook header says; it is not a requirement to change sudo or group settings.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/sudo-n-list`, `sudo-n-docker-version`, `sg-docker-version`).
- **Touches:** design §13.2 rule 1f.
- **Owner:** DV1a.

### Δ63 · Claude policy mode as probed — adopted
- **Status:** amended by Δ147 (DV4, 2026-10-06): the credentials-only `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` becomes a per-call derived snapshot without the refresh token, and Claude Code enters a container only in one of Δ147's two forms, after DV4's step 4c. Extended by Δ148: every call also asserts that no overage is used.
- **Change:** on Claude Code 2.1.288: `--max-turns` is accepted though absent from `--help` (enforcement untested). With `--json-schema` the init event lists the tool `StructuredOutput` even under `--tools ""`, and a structured answer takes 2 turns, so policy runs use `--max-turns 2` at least. `--system-prompt` replaces the prompt but **does not hide environment context** (the model stated the exact cwd): records say "system prompt replaced; environment context still injected", and every run's empty cwd is a neutral random path that names no task, `Solutions`, repo or session. `--safe-mode` still lists installed plugins in init (their skills are absent), so a credentials-only `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` remains the planned way to exclude them (unprobed). Each record asserts from init `tools`, `mcp_servers == []`, the pinned `model`, `apiKeySource == "none"` and the CLI version, and keeps `usage`, `modelUsage`, `num_turns`, `permission_denials` and the `rate_limit_event` (five-hour and seven-day utilization) as the live signal for rolling-window caps. Auth is subscription (`authMethod claude.ai`, `subscriptionType max`, overage rejected); `total_cost_usd` is a notional list-price figure. Never `--bare`.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/claude-help`, `claude-p-policy-json`, `claude-p-design-argv-stream`, `claude-auth-status-redacted`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (engine 2; apparatus; budgets: "`--max-turns` and full prompt replacement are [verify]").
- **Owner:** DV4.

### Δ64 · opencode isolation recipe — adopted
- **Change:** isolated HOME + `tools {"*": false}` + `mcp {}` is not enough: opencode walks up from the cwd and adds 76 `external_directory` allow rules for `~/.claude/skills/*` whenever the cwd is under `/home/leobwang`, and it fetches `models.json`. The pinned form: config `{"permission": {"*": "deny"}, "tools": {"*": false}, "mcp": {}, "autoupdate": false, "share": "disabled"}`; run with `env -i`, an isolated HOME and XDG_* dirs, `--pure`, and `OPENCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_EXTERNAL_SKILLS=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_PROJECT_CONFIG=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_MODELS_FETCH=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_LSP_DOWNLOAD=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_SHARE=1 OPENCODE_DISABLE_DEFAULT_PLUGINS=1`, from a cwd outside `$HOME`. **What was probed:** every run used `--pure`. B2 (`tools {"*": false}` + `mcp {}`, no `permission` key, plus the variables) and B3 (`permission {"*": "deny"}` + `mcp {}`, no `tools` key, plus the variables) each gave the same clean agent: 12 of 12 tools false, 16 rules, 0 skill rules, no `models.json`. B1 to B2 changed only the variables. In B1, 1.18.25 turned `tools {"*": false}` into a `*` deny permission rule. The pinned config sets both keys as a precaution; that combination was not probed as one run, and no run omitted `--pure`, so neither "both keys are needed" nor "`--pure` is needed" is shown. The per-run gate covers the combination: each run is gated, without a model call, on `opencode debug agent build` showing every tool false and no `external_directory` rule outside the run dir. opencode's tmp dir stays `/tmp/opencode`.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/opencode-iso-baseline`, `opencode-iso-cwd-outside-home`, `opencode-iso-config-B1`, `B2`, `B3`, `opencode-env-flags`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (engine 2, opencode line), §12 (`contract_local.sh`).
- **Owner:** DV4.

### Δ65 · Local model choice and run-record pins — adopted
- **Change:** Ollama holds two flash-next tags, `UD-Q4_K_XL` (112.2 GB) and `UD-IQ4_XS` (94.6 GB); neither lists the `tools` or `thinking` capability, while `qwen3.8:latest` lists completion, tools, thinking and vision. Agent mode defaults to `qwen3.8:latest` until a probe shows flash-next tool calls work through Ollama. `UD-Q4_K_XL` exceeds the 97,887 MiB GPU, so CPU offload is certain for it (`UD-IQ4_XS` untested). Run records pin models by full digest from `/api/tags`, record the Ollama unit settings (`OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH=262144`, `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0`), and every request sets temperature, seed and `num_predict` (qwen3.8's built-in defaults are temperature 1, top_k 20, top_p 0.95). The 122 tok/s figure is n = 1 and only a placeholder for the budget arithmetic.
- **Why:** infra (`infra/ollama-list`, `ollama-api-tags`, `host-gpu`, `ollama-listen`, `ollama-gen-qwen3.8`).
- **Touches:** design §11.1 (apparatus; budgets: "`qwen3.8-flash-next` needs CPU offload [verify]"), §11.2–§11.3 (Qwen arms), §12.
- **Owner:** DV4, DV5.

## J. Authoring process

### Δ66 · Lane scratch directories and solver memory — adopted
- **Status:** the scratch location is superseded by Δ68 (2026-10-03): lanes no longer work under the session scratchpad `/tmp/claude-<uid>/`. The solver-memory half stands.
- **Change:** each lane works in its own scratch subdirectory (the shared session scratchpad caused a helper-script collision between the Verus and infra lanes, which put one infra probe briefly under `verus/`), and every solver lane runs under the `CLAUDE.md` §Resources caps, with its share stated in the lane brief. Lane briefs name both.
- **Why:** infra lane process note; the two out-of-memory events of 2026-10-03 recorded in `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Touches:** spec §9.5 items 5 and 10.
- **Owner:** orchestrator.

### Δ67 · CI is a local script, not GitHub Actions — decided (user, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** decided by the user on 2026-10-03. Built in DV1c as `scripts/ci.sh` (Δ104, 2026-10-04), which supersedes design §14.4's job table.
- **Change:** there is no GitHub Actions workflow and no self-hosted runner (design §14.4's `dvci` account is dropped). `scripts/ci.sh` runs every CI job of design §14.4 locally (static, hook-test, verify × {Q,R} × {dafny,verus}, coexist, harness, paper, golden pins, empty-body gate; brittleness on request), each solver job under the `CLAUDE.md` §Resources memory caps, with logs to files and only locations on the console (spec §8.5 design rules). An authoring session (or the learner) runs it before every merge into `main`; its pass is recorded in the merge commit message. The spec §8.5 invariants keep their meaning; only the trigger changes from "on push" to "before merge".
- **Why:** user decision (CI runner question, 2026-10-03); avoids a host account, private-repo minutes, and small hosted runners running out of memory on the 7–14 GiB Verus jobs (Δ57).
- **Touches:** design §14.4, §15.2 (DV1c "core CI"); spec §8.5 (trigger), §12.1 ("CI never runs on the learner branch" becomes "ci.sh is never run on the learner branch by an authoring session").
- **Owner:** DV1c.

### Δ68 · Lane scratch lives in `/tmp/dvl0-<phase>-<lane>/` — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03. Supersedes Δ66's scratch location; Δ66's solver-memory rule stands.
- **Change:** every lane, probe driver and test harness keeps its scratch in a directory of its own, `/tmp/dvl0-<phase>-<lane>/` (for example `/tmp/dvl0-dv1b-infra/`), never under `/tmp/claude-<uid>/`, and lane briefs name it. Decoy roots and homes for hook tests go there too; `scripts/test_hooks.sh` already uses `mktemp -d /tmp/dvl0-hooktest.XXXXXX`. A session's own scratchpad stays usable by that session alone.
- **Why:** rule 1e (Δ82) treats everything under `/tmp/claude-<uid>/` other than the session's own scratch dir as other sessions' state. Once the guard is armed, a lane directory there is blocked: `ls` and `Read` of `/tmp/claude-<uid>/dv1b-infra/` are rule-1e must-block fixtures, and `/tmp/dvl0-dv1b-infra` is a must-allow fixture (`tests/hooks/templates/`). The DV1a red-team lane built its decoy root and home under `/tmp/claude-1000/`, where allow checks on home paths would wrongly block, and stopped before sending any input. Exempting lane directories there would open other sessions' scratch.
- **Touches:** Δ66; spec §9.5 items 5 and 10.
- **Owner:** orchestrator.

### Δ69 · Authoring and merging once the guard is on `main` — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03, before the primary checkout is updated to a `main` that holds DV1a. `CLAUDE.md` §Authoring mode ("Authoring sessions and the merge into `main`") carries the same four steps, copied below; change the two together. Step 3 was rewritten on 2026-10-04, at DV1c's close (Δ104): `scripts/ci.sh` exists, the interim list is gone, and the hook no longer refuses `scripts/ci.sh`. At the corpus wrap-up later that day (Δ108), step 3 gained the by-hand acceptance test of Δ107 in both texts.
- **Change:** the guard arms on the session's project directory (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`), not on the worktree a command touches, and stands down only for a mode file in a linked worktree that is not on a `leobwang-*` branch. Once DV1a is on `main`, every session whose project directory is the primary checkout runs in study mode. The procedure:
  1. **Project directory.** An authoring session, an orchestration and every lane it starts included, runs with the authoring worktree as its project directory: start `claude` in the authoring worktree and run `scripts/course_mode.sh authoring` there (`scripts/course_mode.sh status` confirms it). The hook reads only the mode file of the session's own project directory, so a session rooted anywhere else is not in authoring mode, whichever worktree it writes to.
  2. **Scratch.** Lane scratch goes under `/tmp/dvl0-<phase>-<lane>/` (for example `/tmp/dvl0-dv1b-infra/`), never under `/tmp/claude-*`: rule 1e treats `/tmp/claude-<uid>/` as other sessions' state, so a guarded session cannot read a lane's files there. This replaces Δ66's per-lane subdirectory of the session scratchpad.
  3. **Before merging `authoring` into `main`**, in the authoring worktree: run `scripts/course_mode.sh study`, which restores the four canonical deny rules and removes the mode file, so `.claude/settings.json` is in its shipping state (do the same before any commit of `.claude/settings.json`). Then run `scripts/ci.sh` (and `scripts/ci.sh --author` in the authoring worktree) and record its summary line in the merge commit message (Δ67). The plain run skips the steps that read the reference and is safe in any checkout. `--author` adds those steps, refuses outside the author's context (a linked worktree off `leobwang-*`, or `CI=true`; never on a `leobwang-*` branch), and sends their output only to `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/ci/`. The hook lets a study session run `scripts/ci.sh`, but it refuses any shell line that sets `CI` (rule 1b). Before a merge that changes the guard, the deny list or the Claude Code pin, also run the by-hand acceptance test `python3 tests/acceptance/guard_doors.py all` in the authoring worktree (Δ107): it calls a model, so `scripts/ci.sh` never runs it. Run `scripts/course_mode.sh authoring` again to resume authoring.
  4. **The primary checkout.** A session whose project directory is the primary checkout is in study mode as soon as `main` carries the guard: the hook arms there and ignores any mode file. So never update the primary checkout to a guarded `main` from inside an authoring orchestration session: the guard would arm under the running orchestration and its lanes, and block their scratch under `/tmp/claude-*` and every path into the authoring worktree's reference trees (rule 1 matches those by name, absolute paths included). Update the primary checkout after the orchestration has ended.
- **Why:** the DV1a integration check and recheck probed the merge hazard against the installed hook: a `Read` and an `ls` of `/tmp/claude-<uid>/dv1b-infra/` exit 2 under rule 1e, and a Write to `<authoring worktree>/Solutions/…` exits 2 under rule 1. The hook behaves as intended and fixtures pin it, so the risk is procedural. At the recheck, `scripts/course_mode.sh status` in the authoring worktree reported STUDY with all eight deny rules (Δ80), so the settings file was safe to commit as it stood. Never edit the deny list or the mode file by hand. Step 3's interim list gained `python3 scripts/test_course_map.py` on 2026-10-04: it is the checker's own regression suite (358 cases, all passing at the DV1a round-2 recheck), and the round-2 recheck found it missing from both texts.
- **Touches:** spec §7.4 (the authoring worktree), §12.1; design §15.2 (rules); Δ66, Δ67, Δ68.
- **Owner:** the orchestrator and every authoring phase.

### Δ70 · The first MU/week figure is measured over Release A authoring — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-03. Re-routes DV0-REPORT §8 item 29 from DV1a to DV2.
- **Change:** the first measured MU/week figure (design §15.2 lists it as DV0 content) is measured over Release A authoring: the MU delivered per account-week across the Release A phases DV0–DV2 (14.5 MU planned), reported when DV2 closes. Until then the supply assumption of design §15.3 (1.5/2.0/2.5 MU per account-week) and the §15 dates stand, as DV0-REPORT §8 says.
- **Why:** DV0 did not produce the figure (DV0-REPORT §9), and no DV1a lane owned it (DV1a integration check and recheck). A quota figure needs a span of authoring to measure, and Release A is the first milestone with a planned MU total.
- **Touches:** design §3 ("Still to measure"), §15.2 (DV0 row), §15.3 (supply), §16 (risk row "Quota below plan"); DV0-REPORT §8 item 29, §9.
- **Owner:** DV2 (the orchestrator measures it).

## K. Registry, checker and generator (DV1a)

### Δ71 · Item-marker grammar: language suffix, `pair:`, `did:`, S13, brace-depth block extent — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** the marker is `// item: <ID>.<K><n><lang> [S<k>] [format] [min=<m>]` (`#` as the leader in Python), parsed only by `scripts/course_items.py`, which every consumer imports. Beyond spec §3.9:
  - `<lang>` is `d`, `v`, `py` or `dv` (one item over both verifiers: a two-column card, a cross-language predict). A `d` item lives in a Dafny file, a `v` item in a Verus file, a `py` item in a Python file, a `dv` item in either verifier file.
  - Strands run S1–S13 (the spec's regex stops at S12). `S<k>` is required on Q items and absent on R items, since the R track has no strands.
  - C items also take `diverge:<slug>`. Markers may be indented.
  - Header lines sit directly after the marker: `uses:` (H, X, T; an error on a `py` item), `need:` (X) and the new `pair:`, which links an H, X or T item in `d` or `v` to its twin of the same kind and number in the other language. The link must be mutual. `did:` receipts may appear anywhere in the block.
  - A block ends at the next marker, at a column-0 `/*!` header, at the `}` that closes the container the marker sits in (a Dafny `module` or the `verus!` macro, found by brace depth while skipping strings, chars and comments), or at end of file. A Python block ends at the next marker or at end of file.
  - `concept_items` keys take `@d`, `@v` or `@py` suffixes, and an unsuffixed key must cite a `d` item and a `v` item (`dv` counts for both). `"prose"` is allowed on S12 and S13 (the spec allows S12 only), because this course moves evaluation into S13.
- **Why:** two verifiers and Python share one id space. Spec §3.9's column-0 anchored regex rejects items inside module or `verus!` bodies: Dafny module bodies are indented, and Verus items sit at column 0 inside `verus!`, so a column-0 rule would end a block at every function's closing brace. Implemented in `scripts/course_items.py` (its docstring states the grammar) and `scripts/check_course_map.py` (`--items`, the coverage group); tested by `scripts/test_course_map.py` on the synthetic armed Q04 in `tests/course_map/armed/`.
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.3, §3.9; design §14.3.
- **Owner:** DV1a (parser); DV1b (`firsttry`, `pins.py` and the reference lint import it).

### Δ72 · Verdict slots: the answer in `slots/<ID>.slots`, enabled by a CHECK switch — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** amended by Δ117 (2026-10-04): the pinned hash is salted with the module and item id.
- **Change:** a verdict item's answer lives in `slots/<ID>.slots`, one line per item: `<K><n><lang> = ???` when unfilled, `<K><n><lang> = "<answer>"` when filled but disabled. The item is enabled only once its block holds a live `// CHECK <K><n><lang>` line. It ships as `// SLOT: CHECK <K><n><lang>`, which is the switch, not a second slot. Every slot of every format (value, obligation, error, verdict) reports one of three states: `unfilled`, `filled-disabled` or `enabled`. Only the answer's hash is ever pinned.
- **Why:** spec §3.8.6 treats a filled slot-file line as enabled. Keeping fill and enable as separate steps, as for the other formats, lets `firsttry` record a first try before the check runs. Implemented in `scripts/course_items.py` (the slot-file parser and the slot states).
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.3, §3.8.6.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1b (`firsttry`, `pins.py`).

### Δ73 · `--items` keys and the registry's field shapes — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:**
  - `check_course_map.py --items <ID> --json` prints one array of `{id, kind, n, lang, strand, format, min, uses[], need[], pair, did[], file, line_start, line_end, slots: [{format, file, line, state}]}`: spec §3.9's keys plus `lang`, `pair`, `did[]` and a per-slot `file`. It works at any status. Exit codes: 0; 3 for an unknown id; 1 for a malformed record or block. `--resolve <ID>` prints one record (0; 3 unknown; 1 broken registry), and `--list-nonplanned <range>` prints the armed ids.
  - The design names these fields but not their shapes:
    - `budget` is an inline table of exactly `claude_calls`, `claude_runs`, `qwen_calls`, `qwen_runs` and `gpu_hours`, as non-negative integers, non-zero iff `engines` holds `policy` or `agent`.
    - `admissible` maps an item id to `{admitted, minutes, fallback}`. The minutes live here because `min=` in a marker is a slot threshold. A gated item has minutes 0 and a fallback.
    - `statements` is a list of `{path, sha256 = <64 hex> | "PENDING"}`, required past planned for Q19–Q33.
    - `deep_dive` lists R ids and is Q-only.
    - `write_d`, `write_v`, `recognise_d` and `recognise_v` hold Dafny and Verus constructs only; there are no Python lists.
    - `card` lists `cheatsheets.md` ids `"NN.N"`, unique across modules, with no hash in the registry: the pin belongs to the problem that grades the card (Δ91).
    - `schema` stays 1.
  - Budget values are the design §11 [A] numbers. Q07's "Qwen replay" is a DV1a assumption: `engines = ["contract", "agent"]`, `qwen_runs = 1`, `gpu_hours = 1`. Challenge records carry `engines = []` until DV7. Their `review_bar` and `solutions_policy` are the DV1a reading of spec §2.3.8: math-like topics get `library-pr` with `verifier-graded` (Q19, Q26, Q28, Q30), Q29 gets `library-pr` with `reference-impl`, the other engineering modules get `production` with `reference-impl`, and the agentic topic gets `harness` with `harness-graded`. Exits are required past planned for Q01–Q15, R06 and R11.
- **Why:** consumers need one shape. The field list, enums and path conventions are in the header of `MODULES.toml`, and `scripts/check_course_map.py` enforces them.
- **Touches:** spec §2.3.8, §3.9, §8.2; design §7.5, §11, §14.3.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV4 (`fvh` reads `budget`); DV5 (fills `admissible` for the gated items); DV7 (challenge engines, budgets and `statements` pins; re-checks the challenge bars and policies).

### Δ74 · Every core module and every lab has a written track (`paper = true`) — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** `paper = true` in all 34 Q01–Q18 and R01–R16 records; only Q19–Q33 have `paper = false`. The checker requires `paper = true` wherever `card` is non-empty and wherever `engines` holds `policy` or `agent`, and a named rubric wherever `paper = true`. OD11's "paper wherever it pays" now scopes only the non-card written problems: R01, R03 and R07 ship pairs that hold cards only, and design §8's "written track (OD11) in 13 modules" counts the R modules with non-card problems. Every core module ships a `paper/<ID>_problems.tex` and `paper/<ID>_answers.tex` pair, with its cards at minimum.
  - **Challenge modules (DV7).** A Q19–Q33 module that ships a written item (a blueprint or sketch that `/grade-paper` grades with the dimensions its rubric declares) sets `paper = true` and names a rubric. All fifteen ship today with `paper = false` and an empty `rubric`, and `/grade-paper` refuses a module with `paper = false`. The checker does not enforce "written item ⇒ `paper = true`"; DV7 sets the fields as it writes each challenge and may add the check (DV0-REPORT §8 item 35).
- **Why:** `/grade-paper` grades a card only as a `[paper-card]` problem inside the pair, and a lab's pre-registration, which unlocks its runs (design §11.1), is a problem of the pair. With `paper = false`, the cards of 12 modules and the pre-registrations of Q12 and Q15 had no grading path (DV1a integration check). Enforced in the registry group of `scripts/check_course_map.py`; the field's comment in `MODULES.toml` states the rule.
