The challenge rule comes from the DV1a round-2 instructor lane: `.claude/skills/grade-paper/SKILL.md` and `paper/rubrics/README.md` route challenge blueprints and sketches through `/grade-paper`.
- **Touches:** design §7.5 (the per-module "paper" minutes), §8 (R written track), §11.1; spec §4, OD11.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV2 and every later content phase (each pair); DV7 (the challenge rule).

### Δ75 · GPU-hour ceilings: ≤ 10 per module, ≤ 45 over the registry — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** the GPU split stands; the six labs' call and run fields, and Lab α's 4,800 Qwen calls in place of 5,120, are Δ176's.
- **Change:** `budget.gpu_hours` is 1 for Q07, 10 for Q12, 3 for Q15, 9 for Q18, 10 for R12, 3 for R15 and 9 for R16: 45 in all. The checker refuses more than 10 in one module (design §3: ≤ 10 GPU-h per lab) and more than 45 summed over the registry (design §11.3: the six labs' Qwen work in ≤ 45 GPU-h). Q07's replay hour comes out of the same pool, which is stricter than the design. The split follows the planned Qwen work: the 4,320- and 5,120-call labs get 10 each, the 1,200- and 960-call labs 3 each, and the two 36-agent-run labs 9 each. These are [A] ceilings, not measurements; `fvh` enforces both.
- **Why:** the first build gave each of the six labs 10, 60 in all, above the design's total (DV1a integration check). Implemented in `MODULES.toml` and `scripts/check_course_map.py`.
- **Touches:** design §3, §11.3.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV4 (`fvh`). DV4 and DV7 revise the split after the Qwen throughput probe (DV0-REPORT §8 item 24).

### Δ76 · Coverage rows: keys, homes and counts — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** `MODULES.toml` ends with 81 `[[coverage]]` rows (design §5: ≈70 [A]), one per goal payload, keyed `payload_id = "S<k>.<kebab-name>"`. Per strand: S1 5, S2 4, S3 6, S4 6, S5 6, S6 6, S7 6, S8 5, S9 5, S10 6, S11 5, S12 9, S13 12.
  - Fields: `payload_id`, `langs`, `q_homes`, `r_homes`, `deepening`, `gap`.
  - A Q home is a strand anchor `Qxx.S<k>`, an item id or a paper item `Qxx.B<n>`. An R home is a module anchor `Rxx` (R has no strands), an item id or `Rxx.B<n>`. `deepening` holds bare Q19–Q33 ids and never counts as a home.
  - Every payload needs a core home in both tracks, in every language it lists. A home in a planned module is pending (273 pending homes at the end of DV1a). Past planned it must resolve, by the rule in the banner above the rows. A home on a gated item needs a non-empty `gap`.
- **Why:** design §5 names the table but not its keys or resolution rules. Implemented in `MODULES.toml` (the banner) and the coverage group of `scripts/check_course_map.py`.
- **Touches:** design §5, §14.3.
- **Owner:** DV1a; the content phases resolve the homes as modules leave planned.

### Δ77 · Import tiers per language — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** amended by Δ131 (DV3a, 2026-10-05): the Verus `prelude` tier, and every wider Verus tier, admits `vstd::calc` and `vstd::calc_macro`.
- **Change:** every course file is declared exactly once, in its module's `[module.imports]`, with a tier:
  - Dafny: `core` (no `Std`) | `std` (`Std` without `Std.Arithmetic.LittleEndianNat`, Δ35) | `std-all`.
  - Verus: `prelude` | `seq_lib` | `arithmetic` | `all`, judged by the top-level vstd modules used, transitively through the given crates reached.
  - Python: `py-std` (the standard library) | `py-fvh` (the standard library, `fvh` and the track's own package).

  A given layer's files are declared by the layer's first module. The R layers A1–A5 count as Dafny `core` until DVR0.
- **Why:** design §7.3 states the library rule but not the tiers; these allowlists are the DV1a reading of it [verify]. Implemented in `scripts/check_course_map.py` (the import-tiers group) and `scripts/gen_units.py` (the Dafny tier of each layer).
- **Touches:** design §7.3, §14.1; Δ34, Δ35; DV0-REPORT §8 item 8.
- **Owner:** DV1a (checker); DV1b (the lint; confirms or replaces the Verus allowlists); DVR0 (the R layers' tiers); DV4 (whether `pytest` or `hypothesis` join the Python tier).

### Δ78 · Generated units: Dafny options per tier; Verus crates `build = false` — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** updated on 2026-10-04. The layer crates moved to DV2 (Δ94). DV1b re-ran the resolve runs on generated units and archived them (`dv1b/dafny/armed-*`; `VERSIONS.md` §2.1). It also probed two of the unprobed forms (`VERSIONS.md` §2.2; `dv1b/verus/d78-*`): `build = false` crates verify under `cargo verus verify --workspace`, and a virtual workspace with `members = []` makes cargo-verus exit 1. No course tool runs cargo-verus (Δ99). `cores` as a `dfyconfig.toml` option is still unprobed.
- **Change:** `scripts/gen_units.py` writes the units of every non-planned module.
  - **Dafny.** One `<unit>/dfyconfig.toml` per library tier. `includes` holds `"*.dfy"` plus one prefix glob `../../../given/<T>/<prefix><layer>_*.dfy` per layer of the module's chain; a core unit gets only the chain's core layers. `[options]` is copied from `dafny/options/core.toml` or `std.toml`, then every `[[override]]` whose module range holds the module is applied. Both files set `resource-limit = 5000000` (Δ56); `std.toml` adds `standard-libraries = true` (Δ34); one override gives Q03–Q18 `manual-lemma-induction = true` (Δ45). The R track's induction setting is DVR1's call. `cores` is not set: `vcheck` passes `--cores` on the command line (Δ59). Allowed option keys: `resource-limit`, `standard-libraries`, `manual-lemma-induction`, `disable-nonlinear-arithmetic`, `isolate-assertions`. Refused keys include `base`, `solver-path`, `library`, `plugin`, `allow-warnings`, `allow-axioms` and `verification-time-limit` (Δ10).
  - **Verus.** Each module crate depends on the pinned `vstd` and on every layer crate of its chain (Δ22), and sets `build = false` and `[package.metadata.verus] verify = true`; the checker refuses a build script in a learner crate (build-tool poisoning). `verus/Cargo.toml` lists the given layer crates the modules reach and the module crates, with `default-members` set to the Q crates (Δ23). `verus/rust-toolchain.toml` pins 1.98.1 with the minimal profile (Δ42). While every module is planned, the workspace has `members = []`.
- **Why:** design §14.2 rules 1–5, as amended by Δ22, Δ23, Δ27 and Δ30. The registry lane ran `dafny resolve` (no solver) on a scratch tree with a synthetic armed Q04. Per-layer prefix globs resolved in `includes`; a std unit with `standard-libraries = true` resolved; a core unit could not reach an L3 module (`module QG_L3_BigNat does not exist`, exit 2). Those runs are in the lane report only, not in the archive. DV1b re-runs them on real units, archives the runs under `dv1b/` and adds a `VERSIONS.md` row (DV0-REPORT §8 item 33). Unprobed: `build = false` under cargo-verus, a virtual workspace with `members = []` and no `default-members`, and `cores` as a `dfyconfig.toml` option.
- **Touches:** design §14.1, §14.2; spec §10.1, §10.2.
- **Owner:** DV1a (generator, options files); DV1b (the layer crates, which are not generated; runs the unprobed forms).

### Δ79 · Registry path conventions the guard relies on — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** the checker enforces the names that the hook's write classes key on (Δ85), as copies of the hook's patterns:
  - A challenge statement file's name contains `Statement` or `statement`; rule 2f freezes `Challenges/**/*[Ss]tatement*`.
  - A Q01–Q18 harness file whose name has `gate` or `gates` as a separate word sits under `harness/q/<pkg>/gate/` or is `gate.py` there (rule 2s). The pattern is `harness/q/<pkg>/gate` followed by `/`, `.py` or the end, in the hook and the checker alike.
  - **Only Q has a harness gate class.** R's gate is R16's trust-report, in Rust (design W11, "gate: Q09/Q18 Python → R16 Rust"; design §13.2 rule 2s, "Q's gate, R16's `trust-report`"). So `harness/r/**/gate*` is ordinary R learner work (rule 2, allowlist only), not 2s; a must-block fixture and a checker case pin it.
  - Only R16 names a trust-report path: a path under `dafny/R/`, `verus/r/` or `harness/r/` whose name holds `trust_report` or `trust-report`. The pattern names the trees as they are spelled; the first build's lower-case `dafny/r/` alternative matched no tree and is gone from both the hook and the checker. A trust-report path never appears in `agentic`, and R16 lists one in `exercises` or `harness` once it leaves planned (rule 2s). The layout is DVR3's.
  - Reference crates, packages and modules start with `solutions`. No learner, given or harness name does, and no learner-side directory is named `Solutions`, `solutions`, `Hidden` or `leak_patterns` (rule 1's bare words, Δ86). No registry path component starts with `solutions` in any case, because rule 1 refuses such identifiers in every path field. A component counts as reference by its stem and by its name up to the first dot, so `specimens/<ID>/solutions.dfy.specimen` is reference.
  - **Reserved names.** `solutions_policy` and `solutions-first`, the registry's field and value that rule 1's shell exemption passes (Δ86), are reserved. They are compared without case, with `-` read as `_` (Cargo and pip treat the two spellings as one name). No registry path component and nothing in the learner-visible tree may carry either name: directories, module files (`.py .pyi .pyc .pyd .so .rs .dfy .doo .specimen`), Dafny `module`, `refines` and `import` names, Rust `mod`, `extern crate` and path segments, Python import heads, Cargo package, lib, target and dependency names (`package` renames included), and pyproject `project.name` and setuptools packages or py-modules. A variable, a string or a document such as `instructor/solutions_policy.md` stays allowed. The isolation group walks the learner-visible tree for this. It never lists or enters a reference or held-out component, does not follow symlinked directories, and prunes build outputs. The reference side is Δ86's remaining gap (DV1b).
  - On every record, well-formed or not, the checker rejects any registry path into a reference tree or the held-out tree.
- **Why:** each hook class is a path pattern, so a misnamed file silently falls outside its protection. The DV1a integration check found the statement and R16 trust-report names unenforced. The first DV1a recheck found the `dafny/r/` alternative dead and the reserved names unenforced, which left Δ86's import exemption safe only while no module carried either name. The registry and guard lanes fixed both in round 2. Implemented in `scripts/check_course_map.py` (`TRUST_REPORT`, `Q_GATE`, `path_hygiene`, `is_reference`, `RESERVED_NAMES` and `walk_learner_tree` in the isolation group) and `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (`class_of`); the two must stay copies of each other. `scripts/test_course_map.py` pins the cases (358 passed at the round-2 recheck), and a scratch mutant with the old patterns failed 21 of them (registry lane, round 2).
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (rules 2s and 2f), §14.2.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DVR3 (R16's trust-report path); DV7 (statement files).

## L. Guard (DV1a)

### Δ80 · Deny list: four canonical rules plus four always-on rules — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** `scripts/ci.sh`'s study-mode check exists (Δ104, 2026-10-04).
- **Change:** `.claude/settings.json` denies:
  - the four canonical reads: `Read(./Solutions/**)`, `Read(./paper/solutions/**)`, `Read(./tests/instructor/leak_patterns/**)` and `Read(~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/**)`;
  - four always-on rules: design §13.2's three transcript rules (`Read(~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl)`, `Read(~/.claude/file-history/**)`, `Read(~/.claude/history.jsonl)`) and `Skill(skill:local-assistant)`.

  `scripts/course_mode.sh` moves only the canonical four (`CANONICAL_DENY`); it never touches the always-on four (`ALWAYS_ON`) or any other rule. The canonical strings appear literally in `course_mode.sh`, `settings.json`, `CLAUDE.md` §Authoring mode and `scripts/test_hooks.sh`, which checks `CLAUDE.md` now and `scripts/ci.sh` once it exists.
- **Why:** in a project's settings, both `Skill(skill:<name>)` and `Skill(<name>)` deny rules refuse a skill (`VERSIONS.md` §2.3; probe `dv1a/hook-input-probe`). That settles design §13.2's "`Skill(local-assistant)` denied if supported [verify]" (DV0-REPORT §8 item 2). The hook's rule 1f also blocks the Skill tool calling it. Not applied: `Read(~/.claude.json)` and `Read(~/.claude/paste-cache/**)` as layer-1 rules, because the lane brief fixed the transcript rules at three. Rule 1e covers both in the hook (Δ82).
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (the deny row, 1f); spec §7.2 (layer 1, "four places that must agree").
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1c (`ci.sh`'s study-mode check carries the four strings and tests canonical ⊆ deny on the checked-out tree).

### Δ81 · Hook wiring: LSP and Skill in the matcher; hooks run as `bash "<path>"` — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** the PreToolUse matcher in `.claude/settings.json` adds `LSP`, `Skill`, `WebFetch` and `WebSearch` to spec §7.2's list. The hook keys on `tool_name`, so the matcher only decides when it runs. Both hooks run as `bash "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh"`, not spec §7.2's bare path, with a 15 s timeout; SessionStart matches `startup|resume|clear`.
- **Why:** LSP is a Claude Code tool (it is in the init tool list) and the Lean guard covered it. The Skill tool's input is `{skill: <name>}` (probe `dv1a/hook-input-probe`). The web tools belong to rule 2c (Δ84). Run through `bash`, the hook works without its execute bit, so a lost mode bit cannot disarm it: in probe `dv1a/e2e-guard-wiring` the hook was copied without the bit and still blocked.
- **Touches:** spec §7.2 (wiring, and the committed `settings.json` shape).
- **Owner:** DV1a.

### Δ82 · Rule 1e: session identity and the state it covers — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** neither DV1b nor DV1c ran the subagent probe; it is still open (DV0-REPORT §8 item 34).
- **Change:** rule 1e blocks other sessions' Claude Code state:
  - under `~/.claude` (or `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`): `projects/`, `file-history/`, `history.jsonl`, `paste-cache/`, `debug/`, `sessions/`, `jobs/`, `todos/`, `backups/`;
  - `~/.claude.json`, where each project entry has a `lastSessionFirstPrompt` key (only key names were listed);
  - `/tmp/claude-<uid>/`.

  It exempts the session's own state: its transcript and session dir (`session_id` from the hook input, else `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`); its scratch dir (`scratchpad_dir` when present, else `/tmp/claude-<uid>/<project slug>/<session_id>`, where the slug comes from `transcript_path`, else from the project dir with every non-alphanumeric character mapped to `-`); and this project's memory dir. Paths inside the checkout are never 1e.
- **Why:** the hook input carries `session_id` and `transcript_path`, a `-p` run carries no `scratchpad_dir`, and the hook's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` equals `session_id` (`VERSIONS.md` §2.3; probe `dv1a/hook-input-probe`). That settles design §13.2's 1e "[verify]" (DV0-REPORT §8 item 2). Design §13.2 lists `projects/`, `file-history/` and `history.jsonl`; the other entries hold prompts or session output too. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh`; a copy of the hook with 1e disabled fails exactly the 1e fixtures. The consequence for authoring is Δ68. Not probed: whether a workflow subagent's hook input carries its parent's `session_id` (and so whether a subagent's own transcript and scratch count as "this session's"). DV1b probes it and records the result in `VERSIONS.md` §2.3 (DV0-REPORT §8 item 34).
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (the 1e row).
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1b (the subagent probe).

### Δ83 · Rule 1f covers every privilege-change command — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** besides design §13.2's container tools and agents, rule 1f blocks `sudo`, `su`, `pkexec`, `doas`, `runuser`, `sg` and `newgrp` as commands, not only in front of `docker` (Δ62). The full list:
  - container tools: `docker`, `docker-compose`, `dockerd*`, `podman`, `nerdctl`, `lxc`, `lxd`, `incus`, `ctr`, `buildah`, `runc`, `crictl`;
  - agents: `opencode`, `codex`, the local-assistant skill, and a nested `claude` unless it is the course's tool-less policy form (`-p` with `--tools ""` and `--strict-mcp-config`, no `--mcp-config`) or `--version`, `--help` or `auth status`;
  - socket forms: `DOCKER_HOST=`, `docker.sock`, `--unix-socket`, `http+unix:`, and the Python docker SDK.

  `ollama` is not on the list. Agent and container runs go only through `scripts/mksandbox-run`, `scripts/contract_local.sh` or `fvh`.
- **Why:** the account has passwordless sudo (Δ62), so any privileged command reaches beyond the course; privileged commands are the human's. Accepted cost: every `sudo` in a session is refused. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (header, rule 1f), with must-block fixtures.
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (the 1f row); Δ62.
- **Owner:** DV1a.

### Δ84 · New rule 2c: a contract is active — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** while `.claude/state/contract-allowlist` is non-empty, the hook blocks, under rule 2c:
  - `instructor/` writes (OD13), `WebFetch`, `WebSearch` and reads of `bench/public/**/answers/`;
  - any git command that rewrites tracked files across the tree: checkout or switch to a revision, `reset --hard`, `--merge` or `--keep`, `stash` other than `list` and `show`, `merge`, `pull`, `rebase`, `cherry-pick`, `revert` and `am` (`am --continue` included);
  - `patch` or `git apply` whose input the hook cannot see, that is, fed on a pipe.

  These still pass under a contract: `git checkout -b` and `git switch -c` (a new branch at HEAD); `git checkout <rev> -- <path>` and `git checkout <rev> <path>`, which restore only the path and so are judged as writes to it (rules 2 to 2g); `git apply --check` or `--stat` and `patch --dry-run`. With or without a contract, a `patch`, `git apply` or `git am` given a patch file has every file the patch names classified (`-p1` by default, `--directory` honoured; Δ85). With no contract, whole-tree git and piped patches are accepted gaps (Δ88). `/contract` writes the allowlist in a command of its own and empties it at close-out.
- **Why:** OD13 (spec §6.1, §12.2) and design §13.2's "other" row have no rule id, and the allowlist is the only role signal the hook can read. A whole-tree git command rewrites listed and unlisted learner files alike, so the allowlist cannot bound it. The first DV1a recheck left the choice as a design call, because a learner may ask Claude to pull course updates. The round-2 guard lane refuses these commands only while a contract runs, which keeps `git pull` working outside a contract. Costs, recorded in the hook header: a coach session running beside an active contract cannot write the ledger, use the web tools, switch branches, reset, stash, merge or apply a piped patch either. Design §13.2's "during experiments" half belongs to the sandbox (DV4), not to the hook. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (header rule 2c; `git_check`, `git_whole`, `patch_check`), with `contract`-keyed must-block and must-allow fixtures in `tests/hooks/templates/`; the round-2 recheck confirmed both halves on its own cases (`cases9`).
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (the "other" row); spec §6.1, §7.2, §12.2.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV4 (experiments).

### Δ85 · Write classes as built: rules 2, 2s, 2f, 2g and the author-only scripts — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** its last bullet, the author-only list, is superseded by Δ103 and Δ105 (2026-10-04). `scripts/ci.sh` and `pins.py --ci` are learner forms, and `vaudit --ref`, `specfreeze --pin` and `firsttry --empty-body` joined the list.
- **Change:** the hook's write classes extend design §13.2. The hook header is the authoritative list; `CLAUDE.md`'s access matrix and `CORE.md` copy it.
  - Rule 2 (learner-owned, allowlist only) adds `specimens/` and `dafny/checks/`, which hold learner-filled slots. It includes `harness/r/**/gate*`: R has no harness gate class (Δ79).
  - Rule 2s (solo work, never writable) is `Challenges/`, `agents/`, Q's gate (`harness/q/<pkg>/gate/` or `gate.py`) and R16's trust-report (a path under `dafny/R/`, `verus/r/` or `harness/r/` whose name holds `trust-report` or `trust_report`), as Δ79 states.
  - Rule 2f (frozen; checked before 2s and 2) adds `harness/tests/**` (the author's acceptance tests for learner Python), `paper/rubrics/**` (what `/grade-paper` grades against) and the bare containers `dafny`, `verus`, `harness` and `paper`.
  - Rule 2g (the guard, the build and the files that steer a session) covers everything under `.claude/` except `.claude/state/`, of which it covers only the mode file. It also covers all of `tests/`, and `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md` at any depth. It adds to the design's list `pytest.ini`, `setup.cfg`, `setup.py`, `tox.ini`, `sitecustomize.py`, `usercustomize.py`, `*.pth`, `.config/dotnet-tools.json`, `.toolchain/`, `dafny/options/`, `.vscode/settings.json`, `.mcp.json` and the user-level settings files; `chmod` and `chown` on these paths count as writes. It also covers verifier-poisoning environment on a shell line: `VERUS_Z3_PATH=`, `VERUS_EXTRA_ARGS=`, `RUSTC_WRAPPER=`, `RUSTFLAGS` with `--cfg` or `verus_only`, a `PATH=` prefix on a verifier command, Dafny `--plugin`, and a `--solver-path` other than the pinned bundled Z3.
  - **The checkout root is a 2g write target.** So is any directory that holds it. This covers `rm -rf .` (also `./`, `"$PWD"` and `..`), `chmod -R .`, `git checkout .` and `git restore .`, `git clean` with no pathspec, `find . -delete`, an archive extracted at the root, and a directory's contents copied there (`rsync src/ .`, `cp -r src/. .`). A single file copied or moved into the root is judged by its own name. Side effect: unpacking into an ancestor such as `~` is refused too.
  - An allowlist line naming a 2f, 2s or 2g path is rejected; 2g lines are rejected since DV1a round 2, matching `/contract`'s own validation (`contract/SKILL.md`). The hook splits shell-written allowlist words at backslashes, so `printf 'X\n' > allowlist` checks `X`. It skips `.claude/state/` and the allowlist path itself, so the `/contract` setup line passes.
  - **Shell writes** (best effort, the header's "Bash write detection"). The line is cut at `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, `&` and command substitutions, and wrappers are peeled. Every existing path word, glob expansion and redirect target is checked, and these commands have handlers:
    - `tee`; `sed`, `perl` and `ruby -i`;
    - `cp`, `install`, `rsync`, `ln`, `scp` and `mv`; copied into a directory, each source is judged by its own name;
    - `rm`, `rmdir`, `unlink`, `shred`, `truncate`, `dd of=`, and `chmod`, `chown`, `chgrp`, `chattr` and `setfacl`;
    - git `checkout`, `switch`, `restore`, `rm`, `mv` and `clean`;
    - archive extraction (`tar x` with `-C` or `--directory`, `unzip -d`, `7z x` or `e` with `-o`): the members named, else the target directory itself;
    - `patch` (its file, `-o`, `-r`, or every name its diff carries, read from `-i`, a `<` file or a heredoc, under `-d`) and `git apply` or `am` (the names in the patch files, `-p1` by default);
    - the file operands of `ex`, `ed`, `vi`, `vim` and `nvim`;
    - `find -delete`, and a `find` or `fd` exec (`-exec`, `-x`, `-X`, `--exec`, `--exec-batch`) of a writing command, where each start or search directory is a write target;
    - script one-liners (`python`, `node`, `ruby`, `perl`, `deno`, `bun`) that call a file-writing function: every protected path named in the text.

    Path words spelled through `$PWD` and its relatives count (Δ86). What is not checked is listed in Δ88.
  - Rule 1b refuses author-only scripts on any shell line, even to read them: `scripts/ci.sh`, `*reference_lint*`, `update_rubric_pins.sh` with or without `--check`, `pins.py --ci|--update|--ref` and `vcheck --ref`. Authors read them with the Read tool.
- **Why:** the DV1a integration check showed that a coach session could fill a learner's specimen or check-unit slot before the first try was recorded, and could rewrite the files that steer a tutor, grader or contractor: CORE, the skills, `CLAUDE.md`, rubrics, acceptance tests and fixtures. The first DV1a recheck found archive extraction, `patch`, `ex` scripts and writes naming the checkout root unclassified. The round-2 guard lane added those handlers, the root rule and the 2g allowlist check. Round 3 added `fd` exec writes through the helper `exec_writes`, which `find` and `fd` now share. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (`class_of`, `classify_write`, `check_write`, `holds_root`, `copy_writes`, `extract_into`, `unzip_check`, `patch_check`, `git_check`, `exec_writes`, `allowlist_lines`, `RX_AUTHOR`), with must-block and must-allow fixtures in `tests/hooks/templates/`. `usercustomize.py` was in `class_of` before the header listed it; the round-3 guard lane added it to the header's 2g list. `CLAUDE.md`, `CORE.md` and `contract/SKILL.md` copy that list.
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (rows 2, 2s, 2f, 2g); spec §7.2, §7.4 (author scripts).
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1b (every new author-only script joins `RX_AUTHOR` with a fixture and gates itself on the three-condition test or `CI=true`).

### Δ86 · Rule 1 bare words, the `solutions_policy` exemption, the content scan and rule 1d's readers — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:**
  - In path-like (STRICT) fields, rule 1 blocks any identifier starting with `Solutions` or `solutions`, plus `<ID>_solutions`, `libsolutions` and `leak_patterns`, following spec §10.2's crate-name rule. The one exception: on a Bash or PowerShell line, the registry field `solutions_policy` and its value `solutions-first` pass as whole words outside a path, so a skill may extract the field by shell. The 1b build test stays exact, and so do the file, search, Glob, LSP and verifier-MCP fields.
  - **An import stays exact.** On a shell line, an import of anything named `solutions…` gives rule 1 whatever the exemption passes: `python3 -c 'import solutions_policy'`, the `from … import` form and the `import_module`/`__import__` forms. Reads of the field by `jq`, `grep` or Python's `json` still pass, and must-allow fixtures pin them.
  - The 64 KiB content scan also covers `*.dfy.specimen` and `*.rs.specimen`, and resolves (through symlinks) the sibling files that a Rust `mod x;` or a Python `import x` pulls in. It skips this checkout's 2f and 2g files: a workspace `Cargo.toml` whose comment names `Solutions/verus/` would otherwise block every command that names it.
  - Rule 1b sees a build tool only in command position: behind `VAR=val` prefixes and simple wrappers, optionally invoked by path. `pytest` whose collection root reaches a reference tree is 1b, so `pytest` runs from `harness/` (`uv run --directory harness pytest`).
  - **Rule 1d's readers.** Each of these is a recursive reader. Each of its operands goes through one reach test, and so does `.` when the tool defaults to the working directory (`grep -r`, `ugrep -r`, `rg`, `fd`, `ctags -R`):
    - recursive searches: `grep -r`, `egrep`, `fgrep` and `zgrep -r`, `ugrep` and `ug -r`, `pcregrep` and `pcre2grep -r`, `rg`, `rga`, `ag`, `ack`, `git grep`, and the Grep tool with no path;
    - a recursive `diff` or `colordiff`: `-r`, `--recursive` or a prefix of it, or a short-option cluster holding `r` (`-ru`, `-Nr`, `-rq`); every operand counts, `--from-file` and `--to-file` included. `diff` without `-r` compares top-level files only and passes;
    - a `find` or `fd`/`fdfind` that feeds `-exec`, `-x`, `-X`, `--exec`, `--exec-batch`, `xargs`, `cpio`, `ctags -L` or `cscope -i`, or is substituted into a command; `git ls-files | xargs`. For `fd`, the search paths are the operands after the pattern plus `--search-path`, resolved against `--base-directory`;
    - a recursive tags or cross-reference index: `ctags -R` or `--recurse` (also in clusters such as `-eR`) and `cscope -R`, which copy the definition lines of every file they walk into `./tags` or `cscope.out`;
    - archives and recursive copies (`tar`, `zip`, `7z`, `pax`, `cp -r`, `rsync -r`, `scp -r`, `ditto`), and globs.
  - Rule 1d's build-output reach adds `build/` as a whole, `.verus-solver-log/`, `TestResults/`, `*-go/` and `*-java/` to the design's list and Δ32's `*-py/`.
  - **Path words.** A path word's leading `~`, `~+`, `$HOME`, `$PWD` or `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (bare or braced) is expanded. `$(pwd)`, `` `pwd` ``, `$(realpath .)` and `$(readlink -f .)` count as `$PWD`, and `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)` as the checkout root. This holds for reach, globs and every write rule (Δ85).
  - **The shell's directory**, against which the hook resolves relative words:
    - `cd DIR` and `pushd DIR` move it (`cd -- DIR` too; `pushd -n` leaves it), and a bare `cd` goes to `$HOME`;
    - `cd -`, `cd ~-`, `popd`, `pushd` with no directory or a stack rotation (`+N`, `-N`), and a target the hook cannot resolve (`$OLDPWD`, a custom variable, `~user`, a target cut off by `$(` or a backquote) reset it to the project root. That is the conservative choice, since a recursive read of the root takes in the reference;
    - a subshell, `( … )` or `$( … )`, restores it at its closing parenthesis, provided the parenthesis is in the same quote state as its opener;
    - a `-C` or `--directory` option moves only its own command.

    A `cd` is followed whether or not it runs; that is an accepted gap (Δ88).
- **Why:** skills read `solutions_policy` from the registry, and the bare-word test refused that on a shell line (DV1a integration check). The narrow exemption keeps `cargo verus verify -p solutions_policy` refused. Accepted cost: a commit message or a `grep` that names the word is refused. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (header rules 1, 1b and 1d, and "The shell's directory").
  - **The import gap is closed** (DV1a round 2). The first build's exemption also passed `python3 -c 'import solutions_policy'`, which was safe only while no module carried either name. Now the hook's shell import test stays exact (`RX_IMP_SH`), and `check_course_map.py`'s isolation group reserves both names across the learner-visible tree (Δ79).
  - **The readers and the directory.** The rechecks found these gaps: the `$PWD` family (first recheck); `diff -r`, `ugrep -r`, `fd -x`, `ctags -R`, and `cd -` or `popd` before a reader (round-2 recheck, cases `cases9`–`cases11`). Round 2 fixed the `$PWD` family (`abspath`, the shell's directory as `SHDIR`). Round 3 added `diff_check`, `fd_check` and `index_check`, put `ugrep`/`ug`/`pcre(2)grep` in the `grep -r` branch, sent `rga` through `search_check` and added `ctags -L`/`cscope -i` to `RX_CONSUMER`. It also made `bash_checks` reset the directory on unresolvable targets (`SEG_NEXT`) and keep a subshell stack. The subshell stack fixed a gap the recheck had not found: `(cd dafny/Q && ls); grep -rn x .` passed because the subshell's `cd` outlived it. Fixtures: the round-3 section of `tests/hooks/templates/` ("recursive diff, ugrep, fd -x, ctags/cscope -R; cd -, popd and subshells (DV1a round 3)"), 32 must-block templates (34 lines) and 17 must-allow templates (21 lines).
  - **Remaining gap, reference side** (owner DV1b). The checker never reads a reference tree, so it cannot see a reference-only crate, package or module under `Solutions/**` named `solutions_policy` or `solutions-first`. Twins keep their exercise file's stem, so the learner-side check covers them. The author-only reference lint (`*reference_lint*`, built in DV1b with the item parser, Δ71) adds the check (DV0-REPORT §8 item 32). Until then, the exact import test keeps such a module from being imported on a shell line, and a script file that imports it is the honesty clause's gap.
- **Touches:** design §13.2 (rows 1, 1b, 1d); spec §7.2 (field classes, the content scan), §10.2.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1b (the reference lint's reserved-name check).

### Δ87 · Block message, fixtures and the hook-test suite — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** the counts at DV1c's close are in Δ105. `scripts/ci.sh` runs `test_hooks.sh` as its `hook-test` job and has the study-mode-shipped check (Δ104).
- **Change:**
  - A block exits 2 with `spoiler-guard: blocked (rule <id>) — <reason>. <legal alternative>` on stderr, with spec §7.2's em dash. Claude receives it as `PreToolUse:<Tool> hook error: [<hook command>]: <stderr>` (`VERSIONS.md` §2.3). The rule ids are 1, 1b, 1c, 1d, 1e, 1f, 2, 2c, 2s, 2f and 2g; `CLAUDE.md`, `CORE.md`, the skills and the chapters cite them. Empty or malformed input exits 0, and `SPOILER_GUARD_NO_JQ=1` forces the jq-free parser.
  - Fixtures are generated. Authors edit `tests/hooks/templates/{must_block,must_allow}.tmpl.jsonl` and run `scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py`, which also builds the decoy roots and homes (`--fake-root`, `--fake-home`) and checks drift (`--check`); `--stats` prints the counts. The first build had 598 templates and 830 lines, against design §13.2's ≈210 and ≈480 [A]. The fix rounds added more. At DV1a's close (`--stats`, 2026-10-04), must_block had 623 templates and 780 lines, and must_allow 268 templates and 378 lines. `bash scripts/test_hooks.sh` passed 5477 checks with 0 failures (round-3 guard lane, on the installed hook).
  - `scripts/test_hooks.sh` is the hook-test job. It checks fixture drift; study mode under jq and jq-free; that the hook stands down only in a linked worktree off `leobwang-*`; that it stays armed outside git, in a primary worktree and on a `leobwang-*` branch; `course_mode.sh`; `review-due.sh`; the committed settings and IDE pins; and the canonical strings in `CLAUDE.md`. It builds its throwaway roots under `/tmp/dvl0-*` (Δ68) and needs `jq`, `git` and `python3`, but no toolchain. `SPOILER_GUARD_HOOK=<copy>` replays the fixtures against a candidate hook; an always-open copy fails every must-block line.
- **Why:** spec §7.2 (the contract) and §7.5 (hook regression tests) fix the behaviour. The counts grew because templates add axes (track, language) and keys (`contract`, `no_registry`, `nosession`) that the design did not count.
- **Touches:** spec §7.2, §7.5; design §13.2 (the fixtures line), §14.4 (the `hook-test` job).
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV1c (`ci.sh` runs `test_hooks.sh` as its hook-test job and adds the study-mode-shipped check: no `.claude/state/mode`, and canonical ⊆ deny).

### Δ88 · The guard's accepted costs and gaps at DV1a's close — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** the guard is a tripwire against accidental exposure, not a sandbox (spec §7.2; design §4: H1 and H2 are optional), and the honesty clause in its header says so. At DV1a's close no gap is open: each one that the DV1a rechecks found is either fixed or listed in the hook header as accepted. A gap found later becomes a fixture (Δ92).
  - **Fixed in DV1a's fix rounds** (round 2 on 2026-10-03, round 3 on 2026-10-04), each pinned by fixtures. These were the "open, not accepted" items of the first recheck and the round-2 recheck's new gaps:
    - the `$PWD`, `${PWD}`, `$(pwd)`, `` `pwd` ``, `~+`, `$(realpath .)`, `$(readlink -f .)` and git-toplevel spellings of the checkout root, for reads and for writes (rules 1d, 2, 2s, 2f, 2g; Δ86);
    - archive extraction into protected directories (`tar -C`, `unzip -d`, `7z -o`), `patch` targets, and the files named by `git apply` and `git am` patch files (Δ85);
    - the file operands of `ex`, `ed`, `vi`, `vim` and `nvim`, `find -delete`, and a writing `find` or `fd` exec (Δ85);
    - a write that names the checkout root itself: it is rule 2g (Δ85);
    - whole-tree git and piped patches: rule 2c while a contract runs (Δ84), accepted otherwise (below);
    - recursive `diff` and `colordiff`; `ugrep`, `ug`, `pcregrep` and `pcre2grep -r`, and `rga`; `fd` with `-x`, `-X`, `--exec`, `--exec-batch` or `| xargs`; `ctags -R` and `cscope -R`; and `find` feeding `ctags -L` or `cscope -i` (rule 1d, Δ86);
    - `cd -`, `cd ~-`, `popd`, `pushd` with no directory or `+N`/`-N`, a `cd` target the hook cannot resolve, and a `cd` inside a subshell that outlived it (Δ86).
  - **Accepted refusals** (the header's "costs"):
    - a shell line or path field that merely names `solutions`, outside the Δ86 exemption;
    - `ls *` at the root; a shell line that names an author-only script; reading build outputs;
    - a one-liner that writes anywhere and names a protected path; a heredoc that names a container socket or the docker SDK;
    - every `sudo`; `pytest` from the checkout root; MCP builds of anything but registry targets;
    - while a contract runs: `instructor/` writes, the web tools, and branch switches, resets, stashes, merges and piped patches;
    - whole-checkout writes (`rm -rf .`, `git checkout .`, `git clean` with no pathspec, `chmod -R .`, an archive unpacked at the root), where the alternative is to name the directory meant;
    - an archive unpacked into a learner, frozen or guard directory, even under a contract (unpack into `instructor/scratch/` or `/tmp`);
    - a file copied into any directory is judged by its own name, so a `Cargo.toml` copied into scratch is a crate manifest (rule 2g);
    - `ex`, `ed` and `vim` on a learner file, even to print it (use `cat` or the Read tool).
  - **Accepted gaps**, from the header and the guard lane's reports. The four quoted lists after the first quote the header, with command forms set in code type:
    - Earlier entries: a script that walks the tree itself; variable or command indirection that the parser cannot see; string splitting; a Verus `--record` zip; Dafny `base` chains beyond the one project file scanned; multi-level `mod` or import chains.
    - **"Write gaps (accepted; a tripwire, not a sandbox). None of these is checked:"**
      - "whole-tree git with no contract active: `checkout/switch <branch>`, `reset --hard`, `stash`, `merge`, `pull`, `rebase`, `cherry-pick`, `revert` (a learner may ask Claude to switch branches or pull course updates; under a contract they are rule 2c); git worktree and submodule updates;"
      - "`patch`, `git apply` and `git am` fed on a pipe with no contract (the diff is not on the line); a patch file is read to its first 1 MiB only;"
      - "archive tools not parsed: `cpio -i`, `ar x`, `jar x`, `pax -r`, `python -m zipfile/tarfile`, and decompressors writing beside their input (`gunzip`, `xz -d`, `zstd -d`);"
      - "writes inside a script file or an interpreter the hook does not parse (awk `system()`, `emacs --batch`, a python script on disk), and writes fed through xargs (`find … | xargs rm`);"
      - "path spellings through other variables (`$OLDPWD`, `~-`, a custom VAR), parameter operators (`${PWD%/*}`, `${PWD:-x}`) and command substitutions other than the ones listed under rule 1d, as path words (as a cd target they reset the shell's directory to the root, above);"
      - "a `find -exec` of a wrapper script, and `mkdir`/`touch` (no content)."
    - **"The shell's directory"**: "A cd is followed whether or not it runs (inside if/case/||, or to a missing directory): accepted."
    - **"exposed"**: "names only, accepted: a language server indexes the reference tree, so a workspace-symbol search can list reference declaration NAMES (never bodies); `ls -R`, `tree`, or `find`/`fd` without -exec from the root lists reference FILE names, as the Glob tool does. Reference-only helpers carry neutral names."
    - **"readers (accepted; not checked)"**: "tree walkers the hook does not parse, each unlikely as an accident or printing fragments only: `ugrep` or `ug` with no FILE operand (it recurses only when its stdin is a terminal); `difft` and GUI diff or merge tools (`meld`, `kdiff3`) given two directories; linters and formatters that walk a tree and print diagnostics, snippets or diffs (`ruff check .`, `black --diff .`, `flake8`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `semgrep`, `ast-grep`, `codespell`) — run them from harness/ or on named directories; `gtags` and `jar c` (an index or archive the hook does not parse); xargs fed a saved or hand-built file list (`xargs -a LIST cat`, `ls -R | xargs cat`); and walks inside a script or an interpreter (`os.walk`, `glob('**')`, `python -m zipfile -c`), as the honesty clause says."
  - **Red team:** no further independent run for DV1a (Δ92).
- **Why:** the threat is accidental exposure, not an agent that sets out to escalate (spec §7.2; `CLAUDE.md` §Roles), and each refusal names a legal alternative. Accepted gaps are those a natural accidental spelling is unlikely to hit, or those the hook cannot see without parsing a script. Listing them in the header, rather than growing the hook without end, is the tripwire's policy (spec §7.2). The round-2 recheck found `diff -r`, `ugrep`, `fd -x`, `ctags -R` and `cd -`/`popd` neither blocked nor listed; round 3 blocked them and wrote the "readers" list. Implemented in `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` (header: the honesty clause, "The shell's directory", "Write gaps (accepted)" and the false-positive policy's costs, exposed and readers lists).
- **Touches:** spec §7.2 (the honesty clause); design §4, §13.2.
- **Owner:** DV1a (guard).

## M. Instructor layer (DV1a)

### Δ89 · Skills, session check and personas as built — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:**
  - Seven skills, named as their slash commands. `tutor` is model-invocable; `discuss`, `review`, `viva`, `grade-paper`, `review-code` and `contract` each carry `disable-model-invocation: true`, whose behaviour is string evidence only (`VERSIONS.md` §2.3). The behavioural probe is DV2's: it must show that the model is refused such a skill and that the slash command still runs. DV2 runs it in its study-clone simulation (spec §9.5 step 8: no mode file, all deny rules, hooks live; DV0-REPORT §8 item 1). CORE is `.claude/skills/instructor/CORE.md`, with `personas.md` and `concepts.md` beside it. `/contract` reads `CLAUDE.md` §Contractor contract, never CORE.
  - One uniform session check, read strictly. An examiner or contractor session refuses if its transcript shows any earlier skill activity, its own earlier run included; a coach session refuses if it shows examiner or contractor activity.
  - Every skill that takes a module id resolves it at Step 0 through `check_course_map.py --resolve` and refuses a planned module. Until DV1b no module is armed. `CLAUDE.md`'s "Not built yet" line is the one list of unbuilt commands, and each phase deletes its names when it ships them.
  - Persona keys match the registry enum: `sharp-dev`, `smt-skeptic`, `ml-engineer`, `journal-referee`, `risk-engineer`, `numerical-analyst`, `compiler-engineer`, `eval-skeptic`. `journal-referee` is re-themed to an algorithms journal and `numerical-analyst` to integer and fixed-point numerics. The middle band follows design §8.1 ("Rust dev who distrusts SMT") rather than spec §6.9's C++ programmer, and the spec's "SRE who trusts fuzzing" folds into `ml-engineer`.
  - `/review-code` takes its bar from the record's `review_bar`. Its hole, audit and brittleness gates skip diagnosis fixtures (forensic fake-green files, planted specimens), which the rubric's Gate lines name. Its `firsttry --report --strict` gate applies to every module with slots or commented checks.
  - `concepts.md` holds 87 tags and 13 aliases, kebab-case and disjoint; its "declared by" column is a seed plan that bank authors confirm.
- **Why:** these are the places where DV1a chose while porting the Lean layer (instructor and registry lane reports; the DV1a integration check confirmed that all seven skills use CORE's session check and six carry the flag). Implemented in `.claude/skills/**` and `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Touches:** spec §6.1, §6.9; design §8.1, §13.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV2 (the `disable-model-invocation` probe).

### Δ90 · CORE's MAY NOT list goes beyond spec §6.2 — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Change:** `CORE.md` adds these to spec §6.2's MAY NOT list:
  - no help with a prediction slot whose first try is not recorded, or with an exit or pretest item before its first complete run is scored;
  - never open or quote a given layer above the record's `layer` (later layers re-supply earlier targets);
  - never read another revision of `docs/design/`, `PLAN.md`, `QPLAN.md`, `VERSIONS.md`, `MODULES.toml`, `expected/` or `scripts/ci.sh`, and never quote them to settle a formal question: they record verified tool behaviour, which answers predictions;
  - never run a verifier bare: every probe goes through `scripts/vcheck`;
  - no delegation: no subagents, workflows, nested agents, opencode, local assistant or containers;
  - never walk anyone into authoring mode.

  The MAY NOT list copies the hook header's write classes (Δ85). The contract skill and `CLAUDE.md` describe escape hatches generically and never enumerate the G/V catalog. Neither `CLAUDE.md` nor the README describes per-language hole forms: those are predict and forensics material, and these files are learner-visible.
- **Why:** each rule closes a route by which an instructor session could answer a closed-book item or reach reference behaviour (instructor lane report). Implemented in `.claude/skills/instructor/CORE.md`.
- **Touches:** spec §6.2; design §13.1 ("fix on day one").
- **Owner:** DV1a.

### Δ91 · Written track: `B<n>` labels, new problem types, the PREREG hash, the paper build — adopted (DV1a, 2026-10-03)
- **Status:** Δ123 (DV2b, 2026-10-05) settles the card pin format that this entry left to the first card module: a [paper-card] problem pins its card's line range in the learner's `cheatsheets.md` with the `sed -n … | sha256sum` command and its output.
- **Change:**
  - Written problems are labelled `B<n>` on both tracks (`\label{prob:B<n>}`, `\label{ans:B<n>}`, margin grammar `% GRADER[B<n>][<dimension>][<1-4>]: …`), not spec §4.2 and §4.5's `P<n>`, because `P<n>` is a predict item on both tracks.
  - Three problem types are new:
    - `[paper-card]`: dimension `card-fidelity`, scored per fixed field against the cheatsheet line range that the problem pins with a sha256; a stale pin refuses that card;
    - `[pre-registration]`: the six method dimensions of design §11.1;
    - `[lab-report]`: those six plus `reproducibility`, graded by `/grade-paper --report` after a lab's runs.
  - The pre-registration's hash is the sha256 of the lines from `% PREREG-BEGIN` to `% PREREG-END`, inclusive, in `paper/<ID>_answers.tex`. The gradebook gains the tracks `prereg` (grade cell `<min> sha256 <first 16 hex>`) and `report`. The grading report's first line is `PREREG <ID>: UNLOCKED …` or `PREREG <ID>: HELD …`, and `fvh` refuses a run whose pre-registration hash is not on an unlocked row.
  - `paper/Makefile`: `make paper` refuses an empty set and unpaired problems or answers files. `make paper-solutions` refuses (exit 3) unless `CI=true` or it runs in a linked worktree off `leobwang-*` (spec §7.4's author-script test); rule 1b refuses it as well. `make clean` never touches `solutions/`. The LaTeX build products are gitignored: `paper/**/*.{aux,log,fls,fdb_latexmk,out,synctex.gz,toc,pdf}`.
  - A Python agentic file carries its CONTRACT header as a line-1 docstring `"""CONTRACT … """`, with the same three keys as the `.dfy` and `.rs` block comment.
- **Why:** both tracks carry predict items, and the design's labs need a gradeable, tamper-evident pre-registration that unlocks runs (design §11.1). Implemented in `.claude/skills/grade-paper/SKILL.md`, `paper/preamble.tex`, `paper/template_problems.tex`, `paper/template_answers.tex`, `paper/Makefile` and `instructor/gradebook.md`.
- **Touches:** spec §3.11.2, §4.2, §4.5, §6.10, §7.4; design §11.1.
- **Owner:** DV1a; DV4 (`fvh`'s run gate reads the `prereg` row); DV2 (where `cheatsheets.md` lives and the card pin format, fixed with the first card module).

## N. DV1a close-out (orchestrator, 2026-10-04)

### Δ92 · No further independent red-team run for DV1a; later gaps become fixtures — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-04, at DV1a's close.
- **Change:** DV1a closes without an independent red-team run of the guard. Its adversarial coverage is:
  - the fixture suite: `tests/hooks/must_block.jsonl` has 780 lines from 623 templates and `tests/hooks/must_allow.jsonl` has 378 lines from 268 templates (`python3 scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py --stats`, 2026-10-04). `bash scripts/test_hooks.sh` replays them in study mode under jq and jq-free, in the three armed passes and in the stand-down checks: 5477 checks, 0 failures on the installed hook (Δ87);
  - 602 probe cases, each replayed under both hook-input parsers (jq and jq-free) against a decoy root, 1,204 runs in all. The first recheck wrote 311 (`cases1`–`cases7`), the round-2 guard lane 82 (`cases8`) and the round-2 recheck 209 (`cases9`–`cases11`). On the installed hook, every result that still differs from its case's expectation is a wrong expectation, an artifact of the decoy root (a decoy scratch symlink into the reference, or `/tmp` holding the decoy root), or a gap the header accepts (Δ88). None is a bypass. The case files and drivers are lane scratch (`/tmp/dvl0-dv1a2-recheck/rt/`, `/tmp/dvl0-dv1a3-guard/rt/`), not archived.

  The guard is a tripwire (spec §7.2). The phase that finds a later gap, by authoring, a simulation or a learner report, adds it as a must-block fixture or as a line in one of the header's "accepted" lists, and records it in a delta.
- **Why:** two independent red-team attempts did not complete. The first built its decoy root and home under `/tmp/claude-1000/`, where rule 1e applies, and stopped before sending any input (Δ68). The second, in round 2, was halted partway by a safety classifier. It made 0 attempts: no driver was written and no input reached the hook (its result in the orchestration journal). The rechecks and the guard lane's own cases have covered the same ground since. The round-2 recheck's 209 cases found the last reader and directory gaps, which round 3 closed (Δ86, Δ88).
- **Touches:** spec §7.2, §7.5 (hook regression tests); Δ68, Δ87, Δ88.
- **Owner:** the orchestrator; every later phase that edits the hook (new gaps become fixtures).

### Δ93 · Lane briefs require scoped recursive commands in the authoring worktree — adopted (orchestrator, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** adopted by the orchestrator on 2026-10-04. It holds until orchestrations are rooted in the authoring worktree (Δ69 step 1).
- **Change:** every lane brief that sends a lane into the authoring worktree requires each recursive command there to be scoped. It names explicit paths, or excludes the reference with `--exclude-dir=Solutions --exclude-dir=.git` or the tool's equivalent, or uses git pathspecs that exclude `Solutions/`.
- **Why:** the lanes of the DV1a orchestration ran with the primary checkout as their project directory, which carries no hook yet. So the guard did not cover their commands in the authoring worktree; only the lane brief did. In DV1a round 2, two lanes slipped and both reported it. The registry lane's unpruned `grep -rln … .` from the authoring worktree's root traversed and read `Solutions/` files, though it printed no content. The instructor lane's `git ls-files` printed tracked file names under `Solutions/spikes/`, though no content was opened. The round-2 recheck routed the fix to the lane briefs, with no file change needed, and round 3's briefs carry the rule. Note: rooting per Δ69 starts the session in authoring mode, where the hook stands down entirely (hook header, "Authoring mode"). After that point too, only the brief enforces scoping.
- **Touches:** Δ68, Δ69; spec §9.5.
- **Owner:** orchestrator.

## O. Verifier libraries and tools (DV1b, revised in DV1c)

### Δ94 · Layer crates and check units arrive with DV2; DV1b and DV1c ship none — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** DV2a built the L1 and L2 crates and Dafny layers, their declarations in Q01 and Q02, and the Q01–Q03 check units as standalone files listed in `exercises` (Δ110, Δ111). L3 and L4 remain with DV3a and DV3b.
- **Change:** DV1b ships no `verus/given/` crates, no `dafny/given/Q/` files and no `dafny/checks/` units, although design §15.2 lists per-layer crates and check units under DV1b and Δ22 and Δ78 name DV1b for the layer crates. DV1c ships none either. DV2 writes them with its first modules:
  - `verus/given/qg_bignat_l1` to `l4`, each `Cargo.toml` depending on `vstd` and every lower layer, with `build = false` and `[package.metadata.verus] verify = true` (Δ22, Δ78);
  - the matching `dafny/given/Q/QG_L<k>_*.dfy`;
  - both declared in the `[module.imports]` of each layer's first module (Q01, Q02, Q04, Q07; Δ77);
  - and the choice between generated and standalone `dafny/checks/` units (design §14.1). `gen_units.py` generates no check unit today; `dvl.dafny` verifies a `dafny/checks/<ID>_*.dfy` file standalone on its module's given chain.

  Until then a specimen under `specimens/<ID>/`, and `vcheck --module <ID> probe.rs`, get the chain of whichever given crates exist (none), and a probe that names a layer crate fails at stage resolve (rustc E0432), never silently. The library and tool wiring is tested on throwaway roots: the checker's armed tree (`tests/course_map/armed/`) and the tools suites' armed Q04 root. `dvl.verus` resolves layer chains from the course root (Δ95), so those roots exercise the real code path.
- **Why:** stub given crates break the checker while their modules are planned. With stubs `verus/given/qg_bignat_l1..l4` in a scratch copy of the worktree, `check_course_map.py` fails with 4 errors, "undeclared course file escapes the tier check" (archive `dv1b/verus/given-stub-checker`; the E0432 probe: `dv1b/verus/probe-no-given-crates`). The DV1b integration check found the deliverable owned by no lane. `scripts/ci.sh`'s verify jobs pass loudly with "no given layer" (Δ104).
- **Touches:** design §14.1 (`given/`, `dafny/checks/`), §15.2 (DV1b row); Δ22, Δ27, Δ78; DV0-REPORT §8 item 10.
- **Owner:** DV2 (crates, layers, check units); the orchestrator (the decision).

### Δ95 · Verus dependency holes, the trust allowlist and `vaudit --check-trust` — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **The tool's behaviour** (`VERSIONS.md` §2.2): under `--no-cheating` a crate is shown each dependency `external_body` or `assume_specification` fn that it calls (W-V08 at the dependency's line, once per called fn, exec or proof). It is never shown a dependency's `assume` or `admit`, nor an `external_body` fn it does not call. That run's `verification-results` keeps only `encountered-error`, `encountered-vir-error` (true) and `success` (false).
  - **The gate:** `dvl.verus` treats a hole whose file is not one of the target's own files as a dependency hole, never as the unit's hole. On the trust allowlist `expected/verus_trust.toml` (format: `expected/README.md`) it becomes an info diagnostic; otherwise it is a `dependency-hole` finding and the gate fails ("N untrusted dependency hole(s)"). When the `--no-cheating` run shows no own hole, no error and only trusted dependency holes, the gate takes its record (`verified`, `success` and the other keys) from a second run of the same command without `--no-cheating`. This amends Δ12's single run; Δ7 still makes the first run's hole list complete. `Result.holes` holds the target's own holes only.
  - **The allowlist is checked on load:** only `[[trust]]` tables, each with a non-empty `path`, `symbol`, `kind` and `reason` and no other key; `path` relative to the course root, under `verus/given/`, naming an existing file; `symbol` a fn or `assume_specification` of that file; `kind` a hole kind; no duplicate entry. A bad allowlist gives `bad-trust-entry` findings and runs nothing, and `dvl.verus.verify` refuses on it.
  - `vaudit` reports each (file, line, kind) once. Before the fix, a dependency `external_body` was counted twice.
  - **`vaudit --check-trust` (DV1c)** wraps `dvl.verus.check_trust` over the course root. It audits every given crate once under `--no-cheating`, from the top of each chain, and reports every untrusted given-layer hole, every entry that matches no hole (`stale-trust-entry`) and every incomplete run. It exits 1 on any finding and 3 without the toolchain, and it takes only `--trust FILE` and `--json`. With no given crate and no entry it passes loudly and runs nothing. `scripts/ci.sh` runs it once per run (Δ104).
  - **The course root (DV1c):** `dvl.verus` resolves layer chains, crate discovery and the allowlist from the course root, never from the scripts' checkout: the caller's `root=`, else the target's nearest ancestor holding `MODULES.toml`, else the checkout. Holes are matched to entries by absolute path.
- **Why:** a trusted `external_body` in a given layer (the Δ55 `TRUST.md` route) would otherwise fail every module crate that calls it, on a hole that is not its own. In the DV1b integration check, a hollowed L2 with an added `external_body` failed the L4 gate (`dv1b/integ/evidence/verus-dep-external-body-l4-gate`). A trust entry that matched nothing also passed silently. Fixtures `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/trusted/{tdep,tuse}`; tests in `tests/wrappers/test_verus_*.py` and `test_tools_*.py`. Evidence: `dv1b/verus/trusted-probe`, `dv1b/verus/integ-dep-holes`, and `dv1c/tools/check-trust-probe.log` (no allowlist: 1 finding; the hole trusted: 0; a stale entry: 1).
- **Touches:** Δ7, Δ12, Δ55; spec §8.5 ("reference is hole-free": the Verus trust allowlist), §3.3; design §7.1.
- **Owner:** DV1b (library), DV1c (`--check-trust`, CI); DV2 and DV3b (the given-layer trust entries, Δ55).

### Δ96 · Count pins as built: `expected/<ID>.counts` — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** amended by Δ115 (2026-10-04): the file also pins the shipped hole count of each exercise, exit and agent file of the module (never a specimen), and the tools check it.
- **Change:** Δ11's per-unit count pins live in `expected/<ID>.counts` (format: `expected/README.md`). There is one `[[unit]]` per registry verification unit, with three fields:
  - `reference_verified`: the twin's gate count (Dafny tasks, Verus `verified`);
  - `symbols`: the learner-declared declarations with a contract that ran a task in the twin's gate;
  - `min_verified`: one per pinned symbol.

  The author writes the file with `vcheck --ref --pin-counts <ID>`, which is author-only and reads the twins. `vcheck --gate` on a registry unit fails in three cases: a pinned symbol that ran no task (`pinned-symbol-missing`); a count below the floor (`count-below-pin`; for Verus the floor goes to `dvl.verus.verify` as `min_verified`); and no pin at all (`count-pin-missing`). The last case fails closed, so every armed module needs its counts. Equality with the twin is checked only on the twin: `vcheck --ref` fails a twin whose count or symbols drifted (`count-pin-stale`). `pins.py --ci` flags a missing, malformed or stale-unit pin, a stale symbol, and a `.counts` file of a module that is not armed.

  Pins are per unit, not per item. A file outside every unit (a specimen, the Dafny exit file, a check unit) still gates on `verified > 0`; for it, `specfreeze --check` and `firsttry`'s marker checks catch a deletion. Δ11's other parts stand as built: a filtered run with 0 verified fails (Δ98); per-symbol Dafny scoring uses `--filter-symbol 'Mod.Name.'`; a test run needs one `PASSED` line per requested name.
- **Why:** the DV1b integration check found no count pinned. Deleting a proved lemma from a scratch copy of the spike L2 still passed `vcheck --gate`, with 28 verified where the reference has 29, and `vaudit` reported nothing. Equality with the twin, or a floor at the twin's count, would fail valid solutions: a learner may add helper lemmas, and a twin's reference-only helpers, tests and isolated batches count tasks that a correct learner solution need not have. Symbol presence catches the deletion: in the tools suite's deleted-lemma tests, Dafny dropped from 7 to 6 tasks and Verus from 6 to 4, each failing with `pinned-symbol-missing`. Implemented in `scripts/vcheck` (`contract_symbols`, `apply_count_pin`, `pin_counts`) and `scripts/pins.py`; tests in `tests/wrappers/test_tools_*.py`.
- **Touches:** Δ11, Δ39, Δ40; design §7.1, §14.4 ("hole counts by tool = chapter = CI pin"); spec §3.10, §8.5.
- **Owner:** DV1b; every phase that arms a module runs `vcheck --ref --pin-counts <ID>`.

### Δ97 · The Dafny given role and `vaudit --mode given` — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **The given role.** The Dafny lint has a fourth mode, `given`, beside `learner`, `agent` and `reference`, and a role that comes from the path, never from a file name. In every mode, these files have the given role:
    - a course tree's given layers (`dafny/given/<T>/<QG|RG>_<layer>_*.dfy` under the root that holds `MODULES.toml`);
    - the files the registry declares in `statements`.
  - **Abstract modules in the given role.** An abstract module is allowed only when two things hold. First, a concrete module of the same program refines it, directly or through abstract refiners. Second, every concrete refiner gives each bodyless member a body and each value-less const a value. Otherwise the lint reports `abstract-not-refined`, plus `bodyless-abstract` or `uninitialized-const` for each open member. A declared statement file may hold abstract modules whatever refines them.
  - **What stays a finding.** Traits stay findings, and so do abstract modules in learner, agent and reference files (Δ3). `dvl.dafny.audit` lints with the whole program as context in every mode.
  - **`vaudit --mode given <file>`** (DV1c) gives the role to every own file of a target outside the course tree. Inside the tree it refuses (exit 3) any target whose own files are not already given layers or declared statements, so a learner file cannot opt out of the abstract-module rule. `--ref --mode given` applies the reference rules plus the given role. On Verus targets, `--mode given` lints with the learner rules, because the trust allowlist covers Verus given crates (Δ95).
- **Why:** `vaudit` could not report 0 findings on the DV0 Dafny L4 reference. That reference's frozen abstract refinement spec, the Δ44 statement-freeze pattern, gave 1 `abstract-module` and 10 `bodyless-abstract` findings (`dv1b/tools/spike-dafny-audit.txt`). Laid out as given layers under a scratch course root, L1–L4 audit with 0 findings, and a refiner with one body removed is reported as `abstract-not-refined`. Fixtures: `tests/wrappers/fixtures/dafny/given/{refined,chain,partial,unrefined,spec_only,impl_only}.dfy`. Evidence: `dv1c/tools/vaudit-given.log`, `vaudit-intree-probe.log`.
- **Touches:** Δ3, Δ4, Δ44; spec §11.4 G3; design §6.3.
- **Owner:** DV1b (library), DV1c (CLI); DV3b (L4 and Q08's frozen spec).

### Δ98 · `dvl.dafny` as built — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **Hole counting** uses `dafny resolve`, which runs no solver and costs little memory. It prints W-D01 to W-D03 at the same positions as `verify` (`VERSIONS.md` §2.1). Only the exact pinned wordings count (Δ1). A program that does not resolve raises an error instead of giving a partial count.
  - **The lint.** Learner mode allows a plain `assume` and a loud bodyless hole with exactly one `ensures`, because those are tool-counted holes (spec §3.3). The `agent` and `reference` modes flag every hole form, since a delivery or a twin is hole-free; design §6.3's banned `assume` is read as "not in a finished file". The lint is an attribute allowlist (G17), with three new rules: `uninitialized-const` (a G4-like oracle), `resource-attribute`, and `options-attribute` (Δ30). Learner mode flags `trait` (Δ3); a content phase that teaches traits relaxes that.
  - **The audit** is `dafny audit` over every root, plus the lint. The bundled standard library's rows are excluded, 81 of them for a std unit. Their count is pinned (`pins.toml` `stdlib_audit_rows`), so a change in that count is a finding. One of those rows uses a W-D14 issue text that DV0 had not seen (`VERSIONS.md` §2.4).
  - **Files outside every unit.** A course-tree file in no unit (an exit or agent file, `dafny/checks/<ID>_*.dfy`, a given layer) is verified on its module's given chain, with its tier's options on the command line. A given layer uses its layer's first module.
  - **Filtered runs.** A pass-through `--filter-symbol` or `--filter-position`, or the symbol channel, that verifies 0 has verdict failed (`filter-matched-nothing`). An unfiltered loop run with 0 tasks stays `verified` with a `nothing-verified` note; the gate fails it. Under `--filter-position` the count is in assertions (W-D08). `count_unit(result)` names the unit (`assertions`; `passed-tasks` for seeded runs; `tasks`), so counts in different units are never compared (Δ39). `vcheck` prints the unit ("N assertions verified"), and its `--json` output carries `count_unit` (DV1c).
  - **Scratch copies.** Every compile, specimen run, `test()`, `empty_body()` and freeze run works in a scratch copy, which is deleted afterwards; only cmd, stdout, stderr, exit and `tasks.csv` are kept (Δ32). So a run on a reference twin leaves no answer text in the scratch directory.
  - **`dafny server`** runs from a scratch copy of the install (`server_install`, `server_argv`); this is Δ33's first option. Its log file name depends on the locale, and with stdin at end of file it does not exit on its own (`VERSIONS.md` §1.1).
  - **The empty-body gate.** `empty_body` gives verdict `failed` when every shipped-configuration run (3 seeds) failed or ran out of resource, which means the hole is real. The maximal-automation run (levers stripped, `--manual-lemma-induction false --disable-nonlinear-arithmetic false`) is reported as `lever-needed` and never gates (Δ45). The gate replaces the whole body; region-level hollowing needs markers in the twin (DV2, DV3a).
  - **Exec holes.** An exec hole whose body is `{ assume false; }` is one W-D03 hole. It compiles under `--allow-warnings`, unrelated tests pass, and a test that relies on the method's result fails at run time (DV0-REPORT §8 item 7).
  - **Diagnostics.** `Diag.col` is 1-based (Dafny prints 0-based columns), and `Diag.file` is absolute, mapped back from the scratch copy.
- **Why:** the DV1b Dafny lane and its fix round. Implemented in `scripts/dvl/dafny.py` and `scripts/dvl/pins.toml`; tests `tests/wrappers/test_dafny_{static,common,tool}.py`. In the DV1b integration check, hollowing 3 items gave exactly 3 tool-counted holes with the right labels. The reference gates verified with 18, 29, 38 and 129 tasks (L1–L4; 129 matches DV0-REPORT §5.1). All 12 planted Dafny fake-greens were caught by the instrument DELTAS assigns, and every pass-through refusal held. Evidence: `dv1b/dafny/`.
- **Touches:** Δ1, Δ2, Δ3, Δ4, Δ11, Δ30, Δ32, Δ33, Δ39, Δ45; spec §3.3, §11.4 G4 and G17.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ99 · `dvl.verus` as built — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **Direct calls only.** Every crate is verified by a direct `verus` call on the Δ25 chain, and `cargo-verus` is never called (Δ19, Δ23, Δ24). So V25 (the cargo-verus cache) cannot answer a gate. DV0's P14 stays as scoped, and P15, never defined, is dropped (DV0-REPORT §8 item 3).
  - **Learner mode** is one `--no-cheating` run. When any hole is present, Verus drops `verified`, `errors` and the SMT breakdown (Δ12). `verified_count` is then None, and per-symbol statuses come from where the diagnostics fall (`hole`, `failed`, `no-error`). A run whose only errors are holes has verdict `verified` with the holes listed, as Dafny does under `--allow-warnings`; done is the gate's verdict.
  - **Hole labels** come from the `proof_note` label (Δ7), else from the item id whose block holds the hole, else from the enclosing fn.
  - **`test()`** is `verus --compile --test` plus the libtest binary with `--test-threads 1`. It never uses `cargo test`, which does not verify, needs the crates.io vstd and writes into the tree (Δ42).
  - **Empty-body gate modes:** E0, E0H (10× the rlimit), E1, E2, EC, EM (first seed only), NL, BV, INV and TRIV (Δ47). The E0H factor and the broadcast-group lists are mode definitions in code, not pins. Item levers (`external_body`, `rlimit`, `spinoff_prover`) are stripped. A variant that does not reach verification counts as that mode failing. Verdict `failed` means the hole passes.
  - **Lint additions beyond Δ9:** `external-body-type` (an `external_body` struct, which `--no-cheating` does not flag: `VERSIONS.md` §2.2), `proof-note-on-admit`, `include-macro`, `path-attribute`, `rlimit-inflation` (V22), `later-layer` and `reference-crate`. Reserved names are also checked in Cargo manifests (Δ79).
  - **Statement freeze:** `spec_hash` uses a tokenizer-based parser over `verus!` items (`scripts/dvl/verus_spec.py`), not tree-sitter-verus (DV0-REPORT §8 item 8).
  - **Scratch:** after every call, a raw dir holds only `<nn>-<step>/{cmd,stdout,stderr,exit}.txt`. Specimen copies, gate variants, `deps/` (`.vir` and `.rlib`, a statement leak: Δ26) and test binaries are deleted.
  - **Environment:** each job's environment is built from nothing (Δ13, Δ21). In DV1c, `RUSTC_WRAPPER` joined `pins.toml`'s `scrub_env`, and the captured W-V05 and W-V14 variants became pinned patterns (`VERSIONS.md` §2.4).
- **Why:** the DV1b Verus lane and its fix round. Implemented in `scripts/dvl/verus.py` and `scripts/dvl/verus_spec.py`; tests `tests/wrappers/test_verus_{static,tool}.py`. In the DV1b integration check, the spike reference gates verified with 3, 7, 7, 17 and 15 and 0 holes. All 12 planted Verus fake-greens were caught: admit, `external_body` and assume as counted holes, and V8, V15, V9, V10, `arbitrary()`, V22, V11 and V6 by the gate's own lint. Every refused flag and every poisoned environment variable was refused or scrubbed. Evidence: `dv1b/verus/`.
- **Touches:** Δ7, Δ9, Δ12, Δ13, Δ19, Δ21, Δ25, Δ26, Δ42, Δ47; spec §11.4 (Verus table).
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ100 · Solver jobs as built: caps, concurrency, the wall-clock net and the lifeline watcher — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **Caps.** `dvl.common.run_capped` runs every solver job under `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=<mem_gb>G -p MemorySwapMax=0`, with the `pins.toml` caps (Dafny 8 GB, Verus 16 GB; Δ57). It refuses to run uncapped (`CapError`).
  - **Concurrency.** The jobs per pool come from `DVL_DAFNY_JOBS` and `DVL_VERUS_JOBS` (default 1) and are enforced across processes by flock slots under `$DVL_SCRATCH/slots`. Scratch defaults to `/tmp/dvl-<uid>` and may never lie inside a course tree.
  - **Kills.** Exit 137 or 143 is `killed(memory)`. The wall-clock net (`wall_clock_s` = 1800, which a caller may only shorten) kills the whole scope; the run gets exit 124 and verdict `inconclusive`, never a pass (Δ58). The scope also carries `RuntimeMaxSec` = the timeout plus 30 s (`RUNTIME_GRACE_S`), so systemd enforces the net even when the parent dies. A signal death at or after the timeout maps to 124, not 137 or 143.
  - **Revised in DV1c: the lifeline watcher.** A detached watcher (`setsid`, outside the cap) holds a pipe whose write end only the parent holds. It stops the whole scope when the parent dies (end of file on the pipe), and once more after a normal return, to stop stragglers. A Ctrl-C in the parent stops the scope too. If the watcher cannot start, the job is refused, never run uncapped. `RuntimeMaxSec` stays as the last net. `scripts/ci.sh` forces both job counts to 1 (Δ104).
- **Why:** in the DV1b integration check, a capped job whose Python parent was SIGKILLed kept running past its wall clock until it was stopped by hand, and the Verus lane saw an orphaned `rust_verify`. A child's OOM kill inside a scope surfaced as 143 (`dv1b/integ/evidence/scope-oom-child-exit.txt`). Implemented in `scripts/dvl/common.py`. `tests/wrappers/test_dafny_common.py` has 13 `run_capped` tests at DV1c's close; in one, a SIGKILLed parent's driver and solver are gone within 10 s although the limit and the net were both 600 s.
- **Touches:** Δ56, Δ57, Δ58; `CLAUDE.md` §Resources.
- **Owner:** DV1b; DV1c (the watcher); DV4 (the sandbox's jobs).

### Δ101 · The tools as built: shared core, exit map, done gates, output and twins — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Change:**
  - **Shared core.** The tools' shared core lives in `scripts/vcheck`: the registry, targets, the author self-gate, shadow trees, calibration I/O, statement tables and output. `vaudit`, `specfreeze`, `firsttry.py`, `pins.py` and `reference_lint.py` load it as a module. `scripts/firsttry.sh`, the name the documents use, is a three-line shim that runs `firsttry.py`.
  - **Exit map** of `vcheck`, `vaudit`, `specfreeze`, `firsttry` and `pins.py`: 0 ok; 1 not verified or findings; 2 usage; 3 refused. Refusals cover an unknown or planned id, a missing file, a refused option or target, a missing toolchain, the author gate, and an unusable registry; each gives a clean refusal line, never a traceback. `killed(memory)` and the wall-clock kill are reported, never passed (Δ14).
  - **Done gates.** The Dafny `vcheck --gate` only verifies (Δ10, Δ11), and the Δ3 lint is `vaudit`'s (Δ4), so Dafny done = `vcheck --gate` and `vaudit`. The Verus gate runs the lint itself (Δ12). The gate refuses a filter, `--isolate` or `--symbol` (exit 3). Verifier arguments after `--` pass only through the libraries' refusal lists.
  - **Targets.** A target is a file, a specimen, a unit directory, a crate directory or a project file, or `<ID> [d|v]` for a module's registry units (also spelled `--module <ID> [--lang d|v]`). `--module <ID> <probe>` gives a probe its module's given chain and unit options. Specimens run only through the libraries. `vcheck` and `vaudit` refuse a specimen whose slots have no recorded first try (H5).
  - **Output.** Learner and gate runs print the verifier's messages with their locations, since that is the learner loop's feedback. The `--ref` modes, pin mismatches and `reference_lint.py` print locations only, and `--ref --json` blanks every message and hole label.
  - **Twins.** A twin sits at `Solutions/<learner path>`, and verdict answers at `Solutions/slots/<ID>.slots`. `vcheck --ref` and `vaudit --ref` run a twin in a scratch shadow tree at its learner path and map locations back. A directory of loose `.dfy` files that mirrors no learner path, such as the DV0 spikes, runs as one scratch unit with the pinned limit, plus `standard-libraries` when a file imports `Std`.
  - **Other modes.** `vcheck --selftest` implements design §7.1's P0. `vcheck --brittleness` runs under every pinned seed and fails on a verdict that differs; the resource-count spread is advisory (Δ59). `vaudit --write` and `--compare` keep a pinned audit report (`expected/README.md`).
- **Why:** the DV1b tools lane, its fix round and the integration check; the DV1c tools lane. Tests: `tests/wrappers/test_tools_{common,static,tool}.py`. The whole `tests/wrappers` suite passed 284 tests at DV1c's close, solver jobs one at a time under the caps (`dv1c/tools/full-3.log`). Evidence: `dv1b/tools/`, `dv1b/integ/`.
- **Touches:** design §7.1, §14.3; spec §3.8.4–§3.8.6, §3.10, §3.13; Δ10–Δ14.
- **Owner:** DV1b.

### Δ102 · `firsttry` and `pins.py` as built — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** amended on 2026-10-04 by three entries. Δ116 decides the exit thresholds of the practice tiers and of R06 and R11 (the DV2 decision named under Owner). Δ117 salts the verdict pins. Δ118 lets `--lint-hollow` skip a planted line marked `// planted: <item id>`. Δ121 (DV2b, 2026-10-05) adds the chapter pins (`--chapter`, `--update --chapter`, `expected/<ID>.chapter.toml`), seals sidecars without output lines and retires the legacy pin reader.
- **Change:**
  - **`firsttry` modes:** `--check`, `--report [--strict]`, `--record`, `--exit`, `--lint-hollow [--all]` and `--empty-body` (author-only, Δ103). The shared slot rules (H1, H2, H3, H6, `need:`, commented checks, receipts, marker keys) sit between marker lines in `firsttry.py`, and `pins.py` and `reference_lint.py` load them from there.
  - **Calibration** is appended to `instructor/calib/<ID>.tsv`, one line per item: the item, its kind (`predict`, `observed` or `exit`), the verdict, the date, and the sha256 of the normalized slot text (or of the block, for exits). The first record wins. The gradebook row reads `| date | ID | calib | first-try a/b (p%); exit x/y | unfilled N; unrecorded M |`.
  - **The shipped baseline** is the merge base of HEAD with `main`. Against it, a deleted slot, check, marker or `need:` line, an edit to given code, and a deleted exercise, specimen or exit file all count as missing, and `--report --strict` fails on them. `--strict` also runs the enabled slots of recorded items; an unrecorded enabled slot never runs.
  - **`--check`** exits 1 when any item was refused: a vacuity rule (H1–H3), fewer slots than shipped, given code not as shipped, a file gone, or a check that could not run. "Nothing new to check" exits 0.
  - **Exit scoring** blanks a block with a parse or resolution error and re-runs, so one block never masks the others. The thresholds follow design §7.1, not spec §3.10: survey 10/13, intro 5/6, and moderate and detailed 3/4 with at least half in each language. A tier with no pinned threshold (the practice tiers, and R06 and R11) prints the score and exits 0. Recording is module-wide: one sitting across the Dafny and Verus exit files.
  - **`pins.py` sidecars** `expected/<repo path>.expect` hold a `command:` line, then the exit, verdict, stage, verified and holes, then the normalized diagnostics (Δ36, Δ37, Δ59), with scratch, checkout, HOME and absolute paths redacted. `--update` refuses (exit 3) any file whose items carry prediction slots, since its output is their answer; `--ci` and `reference_lint.py` flag such a sidecar (`sidecar-on-slot-file`).
  - **Verdict pins** in `expected/<ID>.pins` are hashes only (Δ72). `--ci` checks their structure and, in the author's context, compares each with the hash of its reference answer whenever `Solutions/slots/<ID>.slots` exists. `--ref` runs the reference slot checks that need a tool. Obligation slots compare label sets (Δ38).
  - **Python items** (C8 AST holes, Python value slots) are DV4's. `firsttry` reports a Python value slot as "could not run" and records nothing.
- **Why:** the DV1b tools lane and its fix round. The integration check had found a deleted whole specimen passing `--report --strict`, a `--check` that exited 0 after refusing every slot, and a `--update` sidecar that held the failing lines of an obligation specimen, which are that item's answer. Implemented in `scripts/firsttry.py` and `scripts/pins.py`; tests in `tests/wrappers/test_tools_*.py`.
- **Touches:** spec §3.8.4–§3.8.6, §3.10; design §7.1; Δ36–Δ38, Δ71, Δ72.
- **Owner:** DV1b. DV2 decides the exit thresholds of the practice tiers and of R06 and R11, and where Verus value slots live (specimens or a given-only check crate, since `dvl.verus.test` needs the whole crate to verify).

### Δ103 · Author-only modes and the empty-set rule as built — adopted (DV1b, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** `test_corpus.py --update` was added to the list on 2026-10-04 (Δ108). Its guard rows (`RX_AUTHOR`, `AUTHOR_OPTS`, must-block fixtures) were added the same day (Δ109).
- **Change:**
  - **Author-only modes.** Each refuses (exit 3) unless `CI=true` or the three-condition test holds (inside git, a linked worktree, HEAD not on the learner prefix that `course_mode.sh` names):
    - `reference_lint.py`;
    - `update_rubric_pins.sh`, in any mode;
    - `pins.py --update` and `--ref`;
    - `vcheck --ref`, `--pin-counts` included, and `vaudit --ref`;
    - `specfreeze --pin`;
    - `firsttry --empty-body`;
    - `test_corpus.py --update` (Δ108).

    `specfreeze --pin` and `firsttry --empty-body` joined in DV1b's fix round.
