### Δ109 · DV1c close-out: the canonical corpus layout, the `corpus` job fails on an absent corpus, `test_corpus.py --update` in the guard, two pinned wordings — adopted (DV1c, reopened, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** this entry builds Δ108's decision in `scripts/ci.sh` and closes Δ108's open items for the ci lane, the guard, the two missing wordings, the layout naming, the runner test and DV0-REPORT. Each of Δ108's **Open** items is marked in place. It supersedes Δ106's layout and Δ104's `corpus` bullet. Δ103, Δ104, Δ105, Δ106 and Δ108 carry Status lines that point here.
- **Change:**
  - **Layout.** The canonical layout is `tests/corpus/<lang>/<ID>/`, the directory named by the id alone, as both corpus lanes built it. Δ106's `<id>_<slug>/` is dropped.
    - The runner still accepts an `<ID>_<slug>/` directory (`check_schema` in `scripts/test_corpus.py`), and this is **not harmless**. Such a directory can repeat an id, and the runner has no duplicate-id check: in a scratch probe, a second directory `dafny/G01_axiom` with id `G01` was listed twice, and `--ci --only` passed. With `--jobs` above 1 the runner also keys its results and its observed-output files by language and id, so two fixtures with one id would share both.
    - `scripts/test_corpus.py` belongs to the runner's owner and is unchanged (see Open). Instead, `ci.sh`'s `corpus` job fails any listed fixture whose directory is not `tests/corpus/<lang>/<ID>`, and `tests/corpus/README.md` states the rule.
    - The guard's fixtures and `gen_hook_fixtures.py --fake-root` use real fixture paths. The two clean decoys are `dafny/G01` and `verus/V03`, the decoy whose include reaches the reference is `dafny/G09`, and the redirect fixture writes `dafny/G02b/main.dfy`. No fixture's `why` changed, so the manifests' `covered_by` references to guard fixtures still resolve.
  - **`ci.sh`'s `corpus` job** (supersedes Δ104's `corpus` bullet). Each of these fails the job before any solver step:
    - a missing `scripts/test_corpus.py`;
    - a missing `tests/corpus/`;
    - a failed `--list`, which covers a missing or empty corpus and an unreadable manifest;
    - an empty listing;
    - a listed fixture outside the canonical layout.

    Only then come the memory preflight (31 GB) and `--ci`. Before this, the preflight came first and `--ci` still ran after a failed listing. The console label names the cause, for example `scripts/test_corpus.py does not exist`, and the job log holds the detail. The ABSENT status stays in ci.sh's machinery and in its summary line, but no job reports it.
  - **Tests.** Class `Corpus` in `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py` replaces `Runs::test_corpus_is_absent_until_it_is_built`. It holds eight tests, starts no solver and takes about 3 s. Each test runs a copy of `scripts/` in a throwaway checkout, with a stub runner or with an invented five-fixture corpus: a Dafny construct, a control in each language and a covered row in each language. The tests:
    - a missing runner fails, with a corpus and with nothing at all;
    - a missing `tests/corpus/` fails, and so does an empty one, through `--list`'s exit 1; neither starts a `--ci` step;
    - an empty listing from a stub runner fails;
    - a stub's listing is followed by `--ci`, and the job passes. This test is skipped below 31 GB of MemAvailable, the job's preflight;
    - an `<ID>_<slug>` copy of a fixture is listed but fails the layout check;
    - the real runner lists the invented corpus. `--ci --only` on a covered row passes the static checks of every manifest without running anything, and a broken manifest outside the selection fails the run with its `manifest.toml:<line>`;
    - `--update` refuses (exit 3, manifests byte-identical) outside git, in a primary worktree and on a `leobwang-*` branch. In a linked worktree off that branch it runs, and on a covered row it re-pins nothing. `--up` is a usage error (exit 2);
    - `--update`'s rewrite step, run in process on a copy of the runner with invented results, changes only the `output_sha256` line of a recorded table and keeps the comments. On a new fixture it fills in the missing hash and `plain_exit`.

    A scratch copy of `ci.sh` with the old `job_corpus` reports ABSENT (exit 0) on the missing-runner case, where the new test requires FAIL.
  - **Guard** (Δ105's owner; self-protection workflow: the hook edited as a copy in `/tmp/dvl0-dv1c-close/hook/`, the suite replayed with `SPOILER_GUARD_HOOK`, the copy installed once green).
    - `RX_AUTHOR` adds `test_corpus(.py)` followed by `--u[a-z]*`. Rule 1b therefore refuses `--update`, its prefixes (which the parser rejects anyway, since it sets `allow_abbrev=False`) and `python3 -m test_corpus --update`.
    - The header and the block message name `test_corpus.py --update` as author-only, and `--list`, `--ci` and `--only` as learner forms.
    - `test_hooks.sh`'s `AUTHOR_OPTS` adds `test_corpus.py --update 1b`. The author-only pass probes the one accepted spelling, bare and through `python3`. It also checks that `--list`, `--ci`, `--only`, `--jobs` and `--keep` stay open.
    - New fixtures: 10 must-block templates and 8 must-allow templates. The must-block ones cover the bare form, `python3`, `python3 -B`, two prefixes, the module form, a continuation line, `env -u CI`, `bash -c`, and an `--update` chained after a `--list`. The must-allow ones cover `--list`, `--only`, `--ci` with selections and `--keep`, `--jobs`, a continuation line and `ci.sh --job corpus`.
    - Counts: `must_block` has 684 templates and 842 lines, `must_allow` 317 templates and 434 lines. `test_hooks.sh` passes 6077 checks with 0 failures on the candidate and on the installed hook. The pre-change hook fails 52 of them, all new: the 10 must-block templates in each of the five replays that expect a block (study, study without jq, and the three armed ones), and the 2 author-only probes.
  - **Pinned wordings** (`scripts/dvl/pins.toml`, verbatim from `VERSIONS.md` §2.4):
    - `[dafny.audit_issues]` `extern-requires = "Declaration with `{:extern}` has a requires clause"`;
    - `[verus.messages]` `W-V26 = "error: precondition not satisfied"`.

    `tests/wrappers/test_dafny_static.py::Pins` checks both against §2.4 and passes unchanged. The comment above `[dafny.audit_issues]` no longer says the trait-termination text is missing from §2.4.
    - **D-18 moves.** With the requires-clause text listed, `vaudit` reports D-18's second audit row as `audit-extern-requires` instead of `audit-other`, so its hash changed. A `--ci --only dafny/D-18` run showed only that difference. D-18's `kinds`, `summary` and provenance comment were edited by hand, and its `vaudit` hash was re-pinned with `test_corpus.py --update --only dafny/D-18`. Both runs agreed, and the gate's table did not move.
    - **W-V26 moves nothing in the corpus.** It changes only the `code` of that diagnostic in `--json` records. No corpus instrument reads `--json`, the text outputs print no code, and no hash moved: see the whole-corpus run below.
  - **DV0-REPORT §8:** items 11, 13 and 41 now point here and to Δ108.
- **Why:** Δ108's Open list and the orchestrator's wrap-up decision that an absent corpus fails. The runs, on the authoring worktree on 2026-10-04, solver jobs one at a time (`--jobs 1`, the dvl caps of 8 GB and 16 GB, the whole run inside a 24 GB `systemd-run` scope):
  - `python3 scripts/test_corpus.py --list`: exit 0, 105 fixtures (Dafny 36 constructs, 9 controls and 10 covered rows; Verus 32, 8 and 10).
  - `python3 scripts/test_corpus.py --ci`: exit 0. All 105 fixtures passed, with 85 plain runs and 167 instrument runs, in 321 s (the hook suite ran beside it, with no solver). 68 of 68 constructs were flagged as recorded, V13c and V14 only by the grader-only UNKNOWN, and 17 of 17 controls were clean. Both halves of each language cover its 14 X families. V12 and V12b's `witness` hashes, which see W-V26, did not move.
  - `scripts/ci.sh --job corpus`: PASS in 265 s, with 105 fixtures listed (`ci.sh: PASS — 1 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 absent`).
  - `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/ci`: 34 tests, OK, in 281 s. These are 27 static tests (one removed, eight added) and the 7 tool tests.
  - `bash scripts/test_hooks.sh`: 6077 passed, 0 failed, on the installed hook.
  - `python3 scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py --check` and `python3 scripts/check_course_map.py`: exit 0.
  - `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/wrappers -p 'test_*static*.py'`: 155 tests, OK.

  The lane's scratch, `/tmp/dvl0-dv1c-close/`, is not archived: the suites and the manifests are the evidence.
- **Open:**
  - **The runner (its owner).** Drop the `<ID>_<slug>` branch from `check_schema`, or add a duplicate-id check. Until then, `ci.sh`'s layout check holds the pre-merge gate, but not a direct `test_corpus.py --ci`.
  - **`VERSIONS.md` (its owner).** The §2.1 row "`dafny audit` on an `{:extern}` method with `nat` parameters" still says that `pins.toml` does not list the requires-clause row and that `vaudit` reports it as `audit-other`. It is now listed, and the kind is `audit-extern-requires`.
  - **DV0-REPORT §8 item 12** still routes V21 to "DV1c (reopened, Δ106)". Δ108 settled V21's part (`VERSIONS.md` §2.2); V11, V16 and V17 stay unprobed as tool behaviour. The report's owner updates it.
  - The rest of Δ108's Open list stands as marked there.
- **Touches:** Δ103, Δ104, Δ105, Δ106 and Δ108; DV0-REPORT §8 items 11, 13 and 41; `scripts/ci.sh`, `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`, `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh`, `scripts/test_hooks.sh`, `scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py`, `tests/hooks/` (templates and generated files), `scripts/dvl/pins.toml`, `tests/corpus/README.md` and `tests/corpus/dafny/D-18/manifest.toml`.
- **Owner:** DV1c, reopened (the close-out lane). The open items belong to the runner's owner, `VERSIONS.md`'s owner and the report's owner.

## Q. BigNat spine L1–L2 and modules Q01–Q03 (DV2a)

### Δ110 · The BigNat spine L1–L2 as built; layer crates and reference scaffolding — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** builds Δ94's DV2 part for L1 and L2 (the crates, the Dafny layers and their declarations), within Δ51 and Δ52. L3 and L4 stay with DV3a and DV3b.
- **Change:**
  - **Dafny.** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L1_BigNat.dfy` (`module QG_L1_BigNat`) holds Δ51's L1: `BASE` (the 2^64 literal), `Valid`, `ToNat`, `Pow`, the total helpers `Head`, `Tail` and `Drop`, and the worked lemma `ToNatAppendZero`. `dafny/given/Q/QG_L2_BigNat.dfy` (`module QG_L2_BigNat`, which imports L1 opened) holds the spike's L1 helper lemmas, moved to L2 by Δ51 (`ToNatSingleton`, `ToNatCons`, `ToNatHeadTail`, `ValidTail`, `ValidCons`, `DropStep`, `ToNatBound`), and the intro-tier definitions `Canonical`, `Normalize`, `FromNat` and `AddLimb`. Proof bodies are the spike's. The recursive definitions and lemmas state their `decreases` explicitly; the reason is in the author notes (`Solutions/plans/Q01-Q03-author-notes.md` §1).
  - **Verus.** Crate `qg_bignat_l1` (`verus/given/qg_bignat_l1`): `BASE`, `seq_to_nat`, `seq_wf`, `struct BigNat { pub limbs: Vec<u64> }` with its `View`, the open spec fns `BigNat::to_nat` and `BigNat::wf`, and the worked proof fn `lemma_seq_to_nat_push_zero`. Crate `qg_bignat_l2` (`pub use qg_bignat_l1::*;`): `limb_at` and `lemma_add_limb_lift`, the lift lemma Δ52 gives Q03's `add_limb`. Each `Cargo.toml` depends on `vstd` (the pinned version) and every lower layer, with `build = false` (Δ22, Δ78). New code is free functions; no module adds an inherent impl on `BigNat` (Δ54).
  - **Headers** follow spec §3.12 (PROVIDED, complete; not an exercise; `Imported by:`; what the layer holds). They name no import tier or standard library and explain neither the measures nor the induction attribute, because Q01 and Q02 predictions ask about those.
  - **Declarations.** Q01 declares the L1 files and Q02 the L2 files in `[module.imports]` (Δ77, Δ94).
  - **No layer pins.** Count pins are per registry unit (Δ96), so a layer gates on `verified > 0` and has no `expected/` count pin. No given layer holds a hole, so `expected/verus_trust.toml` is not created (Δ95).
  - **Reference scaffolding.** The twins of a module live at `Solutions/<learner path>`. The reference Dafny projects are hand mirrors of each core unit's `dfyconfig.toml` under `Solutions/dafny/Q/<Mod>/core/`, with `includes` re-rooted to the learner's `dafny/given/Q/`. The reference Verus crates are packages `solutions_<crate>` (design §14.2 rule 2; Δ79's reserved prefix) in their own workspace `Solutions/verus/Cargo.toml`, beside a copy of `verus/rust-toolchain.toml` (cargo looks for the toolchain file upward from `Solutions/verus/`). The tools never read these manifests: `vcheck --ref`, `vaudit --ref` and `--pin-counts` lay the twins over the learner unit in a scratch tree.
- **Why:** the spine lane's runs on the authoring worktree, solver jobs one at a time: `vcheck --gate` on L1 (9 verified) and L2 (22, the L1–L2 chain); the Verus crates 3 and 1 (L2 through the `--import`/`--extern` chain); `vaudit` and `vaudit --mode given` 0 findings on all four; `vaudit --check-trust` 2 given crates, 0 entries, 0 findings; `vcheck --brittleness` stable on seeds 1–3 (widest resource spread 0.8 %, advisory); the L1–L2 pair re-verified under `manual-lemma-induction = true` (Q03's unit options), 22 verified. The integration run of `scripts/ci.sh` gates and audits all four layers (Δ113).
- **Touches:** Δ22, Δ51, Δ52, Δ54, Δ77, Δ78, Δ79, Δ94, Δ95, Δ96; design §10.1, §14.1, §14.2; spec §3.12.
- **Owner:** DV2 (the spine lane).
- **Open:**
  - **Tools owner (low).** No check compares the hand-written reference manifests with `gen_units.py`'s output; a `reference_lint.py` rule could.
  - **Tools owner (low).** Now that `Solutions/<unit>` directories exist before a twin does, `vcheck --ref --pin-counts` and `ci.sh --author`'s missing-twin test see the directory and run the learner files in reference mode, so a missing twin shows as a failing reference gate rather than "has no reference twin". `reference_lint.py`'s `twin-missing` still names it.

### Δ111 · Q01–Q03 armed: the registry records as built; the suites' fixture roots — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator took both halves of the Q03 alternative (Δ114, 2026-10-04): Q03's `agentic` and `engines` lists stay empty, and the contract skill's refusal names contract v0.
- **Change:**
  - **Status.** Q01, Q02 and Q03 are `authoring`. `gen_units.py` writes their six units: three Dafny core `dfyconfig.toml` files (Q01 on the L1 glob; Q02 and Q03 on L1 and L2; Q03 with `manual-lemma-induction = true`, Δ45) and three module `Cargo.toml` files, plus `verus/Cargo.toml`'s members.
  - **Fields** are QPLAN §2.6, §3.6 and §4.6 verbatim. `exercises` lists each module's given-only check unit `dafny/checks/<ID>_checks.dfy`: Δ94's choice for check units is the standalone file, verified on the module's given chain, never a generated unit. `recognise_d`/`recognise_v` are filled. `imports` and `concept_items` are `[module.imports]` and `[module.concept_items]` sub-tables after each record's inline fields.
  - **Q03's record** has `agentic = []` and `engines = []`, replacing the two `Q03_AgentContract` paths DV1a listed: Q03's agentic rung is contract v0, written by hand (`CLAUDE.md` §Contractor contract), so `/contract Q03` refuses through the registry. QPLAN §6 Q2 leaves the orchestrator the alternative of a contract-skill rule that names contract v0.
  - **Concept keys.** Three Q03 keys were renamed before arming (`S10.compiled-code`, `S11.failure-messages`, `S13.spec-assumptions`) so that no key name states what its prediction tests.
  - **Arming order.** The records were armed before the module files existed, because every module-id author tool refuses a planned module (`vcheck --ref --pin-counts`, `specfreeze --pin`, `firsttry --empty-body`, `pins.py`) and the Verus module crates need their generated `Cargo.toml` to reach the layers. In between, `check_course_map.py` failed with 128 "not written yet" errors; with the module files in place it passes every group.
  - **The test suites' fixture roots.** The suites built their throwaway roots from the committed registry and assumed that every real module was planned. Arming broke that assumption in three places, and the integration lane adapted the fixtures, never a tool:
    - `tests/wrappers/test_tools_common.py`: `build_root()` sets every real module back to `planned` (`plan_real_modules`), so only the synthetic Q04 is armed, and declares the layer files in Q01 and Q02 only where the record lacks them (`_declare_given`). Before, `armed_registry()` asserted an inline `imports =` line and 50 tests failed in setup.
    - `tests/wrappers/test_tools_static.py`: the three "every module planned" cases (`LintHollow.test_all_and_empty`, `PinsStatic.test_no_armed_module_passes_loudly`, `ReferenceLint.test_no_armed_module`) run on a copy of the registry with every module planned, instead of on the real checkout.
    - `tests/ci/ci_common.py`: `course_root()` keeps its documented planned-only invariant: every module set to planned, `dafny/` and `verus/` copied without the module trees (`dafny/{Q,R,checks}`, `verus/{q,r}`), and the units regenerated with `gen_units.py`.
  - **The checker's suite.** `scripts/test_course_map.py` assumed an inline `imports =` line in Q01's and Q02's tables and counts that held only while every real module was planned, so it aborted at setup once the records were armed. The integration lane applied the spine lane's validated fixture patch: `setf` replaces a `[module.<key>]` sub-table by an inline line; a `declare_given` helper adds the layer files only where the record lacks them; the armed-fixture counts (authoring modules, units, import-tier files, coverage ids) are derived from the committed registry; the "WRITE in the other language only" case uses `frame` (Q01 now lists `forall` as a Verus RECOGNISE construct); the empty-glob case also deletes `dafny/Q`, `dafny/checks` and `verus/q`. The checker itself is unchanged.
- **Why:** QPLAN §2.6, §3.6, §4.6 and §6 Q2; the spine lane's staged runs (state A2 "course map OK"; armed without files 128 errors, every one "not written yet"; armed with the plan's marker stubs, all six groups pass). Integration: `check_course_map.py` "course map OK: 49 modules (33 Q, 16 R; 0 shipped, 3 authoring, 46 planned); imports: 23 files; coverage: 81 rows (233 pending)", each `--only` group passing (coverage: "3 module(s) past planned (Q01, Q02, Q03)"); `gen_units.py --check` "6 unit(s) and verus/Cargo.toml match the registry"; `check_course_map.py --items` parses Q01 to 49 items (19 slots), Q02 to 47 (10) and Q03 to 45 (14); `test_course_map.py` 358 passed, 0 failed.
- **Touches:** Δ45, Δ77, Δ79, Δ94, Δ104; design §14.1; QPLAN §2.6, §3.6, §4.6; `MODULES.toml`, `verus/Cargo.toml`, the six generated units, `scripts/test_course_map.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_common.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_static.py`, `tests/ci/ci_common.py`.
- **Owner:** DV2 (spine lane: the records and units; integration lane: the fixture adaptations). The suites' owner (DV1c) may replace the adaptations with its own.

### Δ112 · Q01–Q03 code as built: decisions and deviations from the plan — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** amended by Δ118 (2026-10-04): a planted trust leap in an S12 delivery specimen carries `// planted: <item id>`, which only the shipping lints honour.
- **Change** (QPLAN §7 lists each deviation by item; this entry keeps the ones later phases build on):
  - **Dafny specimens are self-contained.** `dvl.dafny` verifies a `.dfy.specimen` alone, with no given chain, so a Dafny prediction specimen or card demo carries verbatim copies of the given declarations it uses. Verus specimens get the module's chain.
  - **Agent files.** No sidecar pins an agent file's RED output, because a repaired file would then fail `pins.py --ci` in the learner's checkout; each header states the shipped diagnostic count, and the empty audit pins `expected/<name>.audit` pin the delivered state. The reference section of a spec-first agent file (delivery, the shipped suite verbatim, adversarial checks, the soft-spot note) lives in the agent twin; the main twin ends with spec §3.13's loud heading and a pointer to it, so only one copy is verified (spec §3.13 puts it in a sub-module of the main twin).
  - **Fake-green delivery specimens** (Q03's S12 items) may have no twin: where the twin would be the delivery itself and the reference lint would reject it, the answer is pinned through `Solutions/slots/<ID>.slots` and `expected/<ID>.pins` only (specimen twins are optional). `Solutions/plans/Q03-code-notes.md` §4 names them.
  - **Divergence and false-friends demos** carry no item marker, are named by item id, state no outcome in prose and have `.expect` sidecars under `expected/specimens/<ID>/`. WRITE-card demos carry in-file `// EXPECT` pins.
  - **Rosetta cards** have one marker per module, in the Dafny file; the Verus column sits in `lib.rs` outside every item block, under a comment that points to the marker.
  - **Q03.H1v's given code order.** The ghost carry push follows the index step, and the lift call sits in a `proof` block before the result is returned, so that `dvl.verus`'s E0/E0H/E1 variants reach verification (the tool issue is in Δ113's Open list). Δ52 holds: the lift lemma and the ghost carry updates are given and the hole is the invariant only.
  - **Prediction captures.** Verdict answers were captured with `scripts/vcheck --module <ID> <probe>` (the module's options and chain) and, where the item says "in this unit", on scratch copies of the real unit; each docstring quotes its scenario exactly. Two scenarios changed from the plan after capture (Q01.P9dv, Q02.P8dv); the author notes give the reasons.
  - **Empty-body evidence.** Every H/X/T hole of Q01–Q03 passed `firsttry.sh <ID> --empty-body` with the modes E0, E0H, E1, E2, EC (where a broadcast group exists), EM, NL, INV and TRIV under seeds 1–3; the Verus `BV` mode ran apart through `dvl.verus.empty_body` at a 150 s wall clock (killed by the wall clock or refused on every proof hole, which counts as failing, Δ58), and TRIV with per-item trivial bodies for exec holes that return a structure. The Dafny holes the plan marks as needing a lever report `lever-needed` (reported, never gating, Δ45), as planned.
- **Why:** the Q01, Q02 and Q03 lane reports and their runs: reference gates `vcheck --ref` (Dafny/Verus units 31/16, 48/21, 48/21; exits, check units and agent twins all verified, 0 holes, silent); `vcheck --ref --pin-counts`; `pins.py --update --ref` and `--ref` (Q01 19 checks, Q02 10, Q03 14, 0 problems); `pins.py --ci --module <ID>` (50, 25 and 38 pins, 0 problems); `specfreeze --pin`/`--check` (77, 89 and 87 statements, 0 findings); `firsttry --lint-hollow`; `reference_lint.py --module <ID>` 0 findings; learner-flow simulations in scratch copies (first tries recorded, exits scored, the strict report and the gate passing on the twins).
- **Touches:** Δ45, Δ52, Δ58, Δ96, Δ101, Δ102; spec §3.8, §3.11, §3.13, §3.14; QPLAN §7.
- **Owner:** DV2 (the module lanes).

### Δ113 · DV2a integration: CI, the leak check and the open items — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** on 2026-10-04 the orchestrator decided three of the tools owner's open items below (Δ115, Δ117, Δ118) and closed its own item (Δ114, Δ116, Δ119). Each of these is marked in place. The DV2a fix round then built or closed most of the remaining items (Δ120); each is marked in place, and the items still open are listed in Δ120's Open. DV2b closed the `paper` and `rubric` item (Δ124).
- **Change:**
  - **CI.**
    - **Study run** (`scripts/ci.sh`, 808 s, before the fixture adaptations of Δ111): FAIL, 9 passed, 3 failed, 2 skipped. `static` failed only at `firsttry --lint-hollow --all` (below); `wrappers` failed because the suites' fixture roots assumed a planned-only registry (50 tests in setup, 4 in `tests/ci`); `paper` failed on the missing written-track pairs of Q01–Q03 (below). After the adaptations, `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/ci` ran 34 tests OK and `-s tests/wrappers` 284 tests OK (1 skipped). The final study run, after every change listed here, ended `ci.sh: FAIL — 10 passed, 2 failed, 2 skipped, 0 absent, in 1247 s`: only `static` (`firsttry --lint-hollow --all`) and `paper` failed, for the reasons below.
    - **Author run** (`scripts/ci.sh --author` with every job but `empty-body`, 1345 s, after the fixture adaptations): `ci.sh: FAIL — 10 passed, 3 failed, 0 skipped, 0 absent, in 1345 s`. Passed: `hook-test`; `verify-q-dafny` (L1 9 and L2 22 verified, gated and audited clean; the Q01–Q03 units' tool-counted holes equal their shipped hole forms, 3, 6 and 5; the unit twins' reference gates 31, 48 and 48 verified, audits clean); `verify-q-verus` (L1 3 and L2 1; `vaudit --check-trust` 2 given crates, 0 entries, 0 findings; unit holes 3, 6 and 5; twins 16, 21 and 21); `verify-r-*` (loud: nothing armed); `pins` (`pins.py --ci` 113 pins, 0 problems; `pins.py --ref` 43 checks, 0 problems); `corpus`; `wrappers`; `coexist`; `harness`. Failed: `static` (only `firsttry --lint-hollow --all`; `reference_lint.py` 0 findings over 26 twins in 3 modules and 67 reference files; `specfreeze --check` 0 findings for each module; `test_course_map.py` 358/358), `paper` and `rubric` (below).
    - **Outside CI's verify jobs**, which gate only the units: every exit, check-unit and agent twin passes `vcheck --ref` (exits 13, 30, 28 / 6, 4, 4; check units 10, 23, 23; agent twins 15, 35 / 6, 16 verified, 0 holes), and the shipped learner states match their headers: exit holes Q01 1 + 1, Q02 1 + 3, Q03 2 + 3; check units green with 0 holes; agent files RED with Q01 4 errors in each language, Q02 Dafny 3 holes and 6 errors, Verus 6 holes and 1 error.
    - **Empty-body.** The empty-body job's exact commands (`firsttry.sh <ID> --empty-body --root <worktree>`, every applicable mode with its default seeds and the pinned 1800 s wall clock, `BV` included) ran once per module, the three modules in parallel, each with its own dvl scratch and one solver job at a time, inside one 24 GB scope (three Verus z3 budgeted at 8 GB each; a sample during the run showed 0.8 GiB of solver memory in all). All 39 holes pass the gate: Q01 8/8 in 16318 s, Q02 16/16 in 16437 s, Q03 15/15 in 5515 s, exit 0 each. The Dafny holes the plan marks as needing a lever report `lever-needed`, which never gates (Δ45). Run in sequence, as `ci.sh --author --job empty-body` runs them, the job would take about 10.6 h, almost all of it `BV` runs killed by the wall clock on the Verus proof holes (Δ58: a kill counts as failing).
  - **The leak check.** The integration lane compared the 33 Q01–Q03 twins under `Solutions/` with the 603 learner-visible text files of the worktree (everything outside `Solutions/`, `.git`, `.toolchain`, `build/` and `target/`). A twin's answer regions are its lines that a line diff does not match to its learner counterpart; the check looked for every answer line of at least 12 code characters and every 7-token shingle lying wholly inside an answer region (comments stripped), then a second pass for 7-word shingles of the twins' reference comments. All 337 (twin, file) pairs with a hit were reviewed by hand. No hole, slot or exit answer appears in a learner-visible file. The hits are signatures and checks that the prompts quote (QPLAN's rows, the exit headings), the worked lemma's induction idiom reused in answers (L1's worked lemma, Q01's W1d and W1v), generic loop skeletons, copies of given definitions in self-contained Dafny specimens, and pre-existing fixtures under `tests/`. No L1 or L2 proof is the answer of an item of a module that imports it (QPLAN §1.6 holds as built). Every reference slot answer, obligation answer and value answer was also searched for verbatim: none occurs in the learner-visible tree, and `expected/` holds only hashes. The exceptions are in Open: Q01.C6dv's Rosetta example, a generic obligation example, Q01.X5dv, and the unsalted hashes. The two Q02 agent audit pins named their `Solutions/` twin in the header that `vaudit --write` prints; they were re-captured in learner mode on a scratch root holding the twins at the learner paths (same findings, 0, and `vaudit --compare` matches), so `expected/` names no reference path.
  - **Mechanical fixes by the integration lane.** `scripts/test_course_map.py` (Δ111); the comment above `dafny/options/core.toml`'s Q03–Q18 override now names only the option and Δ45 (QPLAN §6 Q12); the two Q02 agent audit pins re-captured (above); `tests/wrappers/test_tools_common.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_static.py` and `tests/ci/ci_common.py` (Δ111). QPLAN §7 records the module lanes' deviations.
- **Why:** the integration lane's runs on the authoring worktree (`authoring` @ 557f98f plus the DV2a working tree), solver jobs one at a time inside a 24 GB `systemd-run` scope. Logs: `/tmp/dvl0-dv2a-integ/` (scratch, not archived; the reference steps' logs under `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/ci/`).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Tools owner (blocks a green `static`; high).** **Decided by Δ118** (the `// planted:` marker). **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120): `static` passes. `firsttry --lint-hollow` applies the learner-mode lint to fake-green delivery specimens, so a line that one Q03 delivery is built around fails it (one problem; the file and line are in the integration report and `Solutions/plans/Q03-code-notes.md` §4). `reference_lint.py` has the same conflict with delivery twins (Δ112). Suggested fix: exempt the marked lines of a verdict P item's delivery specimen (for example a `// planted:` marker honoured only by the shipping lints, never by `vcheck` or `vaudit`).
  - **DV2b and the orchestrator (block a green `paper` and `rubric`; high, by phase order).** **Still open** after the fix round (Δ120); **closed** by DV2b (Δ124). Arming Q01–Q03, whose records say `paper = true`, makes `ci.sh` require `paper/Q0x_problems.tex`, `paper/Q0x_answers.tex` and `paper/rubrics/Q0x_rubric.md`, which are DV2b deliverables. No DV2a-only merge can pass these two jobs; either the merge into `main` waits for DV2b, or the orchestrator decides how a code-only arming is gated.
  - **Tools owner (CI wall time; medium).** **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120: `BV` is bounded at 120 s). The empty-body job takes about 10.6 h in sequence (above): `BV` runs to the 1800 s wall clock on every seed of the Verus proof holes whose goals involve `Seq` (QPLAN §6 Q5), seven holes in Q01–Q03. A short per-mode wall clock for `BV`, or skipping `BV` on `Seq` goals, would bring the job to minutes.
  - **Tools owner (medium).** **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120: `pins.py`'s value and obligation judges). A wrong `by (compute_only)` value is reported as an error before verification, which `pins.py`'s value judge treats as "could not run", so `firsttry --check` records nothing for a wrong Q01.P6v or Q03.P10v answer (the Q01 lane's simulation). The obligation judge, conversely, accepts a run that stopped at parse or resolve, so `FAILS none` can pass vacuously on a specimen that does not resolve.
  - **Tools owner (medium).** **Decided by Δ117** (salted with the module and item id). **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120); the legacy reader goes at DV2b. Verdict pins are unsalted sha256 hashes of short answers: `expected/Q01.pins` holds the well-known hashes of one-letter and one-digit strings, and equal answers share a hash (three Q01 exit items). Salting with the module and item id would remove the equality signal; the "do not open `expected/`" rule stays the only guard against brute force.
  - **Tools owner (medium).** **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120: `dvl.verus_spec`'s statement ends). `dvl.verus`'s E0, E0H and E1 variants strip a ghost `let` but keep a `proof { }` block that directly follows an `if … else …` statement (QPLAN §6 Q9). Q03.H1v avoids it by order (Δ112); any other annotation hole of that shape gets the verdict `error`.
  - **Tools owner (medium).** **Documented, still open as a tool limit** (Δ120): the Verus agent header states it, and the DV2b chapter repeats it. `by (compute_only)` checks run crate-wide at the VIR stage and ignore `--verify-function`, so the Verus spec-first agent file shows one failing check at a time and the contract's fast channel (`vcheck --symbol`) cannot isolate a check.
  - **Tools owner (medium).** **Hole counts decided by Δ115** (pinned per file in `expected/<ID>.counts`). **Built and closed** in the fix round (Δ120): the verify jobs compare every file's shipped state and gate every unit, exit, check and agent twin. Was open: CI gates neither the check units nor the exit, check and agent twins, and it does not compare the agent files' RED states. CI's verify jobs gate only the registry units. The exit files' hole counts, the check units and the agent files' RED states are compared by nobody (spec §2.4 rule 2 asks CI to pin them); the integration lane checked them by hand (above).
  - **Tools owner (low).** **Mostly built** in the fix round (Δ120): the `// after:` guard and sealed sidecars, per-item TRIV bodies, `specfreeze`'s open-body comparison and the code-T recorder. **Still open:** the region-level gate and the panic text. Card demos have no H5-style guard (QPLAN §6 Q10), and their `.expect` sidecars and in-file `EXPECT` lines are readable before the predictions they bear on; the C4dv Verus sidecars record the raw exit code. `firsttry --empty-body` passes no per-item `triv_body` (TRIV gates only unit, integer and bool returns). No region-level empty-body gate exists for annotation holes (QPLAN §6 Q6). `specfreeze` should drop `open`/`closed` for an open-body symbol (§6 Q7). Code T items have no first-try recorder (§6 Q4). `pins.py` sidecars do not keep a failing test's panic text.
  - **Q01 and Q02 lanes (low).** **Closed** in the fix round (Δ120: the Rosetta example is now a reversal). Q01.C6dv's Rosetta example `SumLimbs` / `sum_limbs` is, line for line, the delivery of one of the three definitions of Q02's spec-first agent files (Q02.A1d, Q02.A1v), so a contractor can copy it from Q01's files. Renaming the Rosetta example (and re-pinning Q01's freeze and counts) would remove it.
  - **Plan owner (low).** **Closed** in the fix round (Δ120), except Q02.X1d's and Q02.H5d/H6d's measures, which stay enforced only by review (DV2b's rubric): the Q02 specimen no longer coincides with the generic example (the detail is author-only), Q01.X5dv and Q03.P5dv's Verus half are re-posed. One Q02 obligation answer coincides with the generic obligation example that spec §3.8 and the docstrings of `scripts/pins.py` and `scripts/course_items.py` use (the item is named in the integration report); relabelling that specimen's lines, or changing the example, removes the coincidence. Q01.X5dv can be answered from code the learner has open (the Q01 lane's note; re-pose the item or accept it). Q02.X1d's `decreases` and Q02.H5d/H6d's explicit measure are enforced only for the form the prompt asks for (review). Q03.P5dv's Verus half is observable before the prediction is recorded.
  - **Orchestrator (low).** **Closed by Δ114, Δ116 and Δ119**, with Q1 deferred to DV3a and DV5. `CLAUDE.md` still says that no module is armed until DV2 arms the first ones, and `README.md`'s Status says nothing is authored yet. QPLAN §6 Q2 (Q03's empty agentic list), Q1 (F3 re-posed targets) and Q11 (exit thresholds outside Q01–Q15) remain the orchestrator's.
- **Touches:** Δ45, Δ58, Δ102, Δ103, Δ104; QPLAN §6, §7; `scripts/test_course_map.py`, `dafny/options/core.toml`.
- **Owner:** DV2 (integration lane); the open items' owners as listed.

## R. DV2a fix round: the orchestrator's decisions (DV2a)

### Δ114 · `/contract Q03` refuses by naming contract v0; Q03's empty `agentic` and `engines` stand — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on QPLAN §6 Q2, which settles the alternative Δ111 left open. Amended by Δ126 (DV2b-2, 2026-10-05): the list still lives with the skill, but as a data file beside it, `.claude/skills/contract/hand_written.toml`, which `/contract` and `scripts/chapter_lint.py` both read; the skill no longer quotes the refusal itself.
- **Change:**
  - Q03's record keeps `agentic = []` and `engines = []` (Δ111). The checker's rule that `contract` is an engine exactly when `agentic` is non-empty still holds, and `/contract Q03` stops at the contract skill's Step 0, item 4.
  - **The refusal names the exercise.** In `.claude/skills/contract/SKILL.md`, Step 0 item 4 now handles a module whose agentic exercise the learner writes by hand. Its one-line refusal names that exercise. For Q03 the line names contract v0, which the learner writes by hand with no AI session, in the `### Q-v0 — Q03` section below the `<!-- learner-owned below: contract v0/v1/v2 -->` marker in `CLAUDE.md`. The contractor offers no draft, rule or review of v0. Every other module with an empty `agentic` list keeps the generic line ("has no agentic exercise"). That line would be false for Q03, whose exercise exists and is the learner's own.
  - **The list lives in the skill, not in a registry field.** Q03 is its only entry. A later phase that ships another hand-written agentic exercise with an empty `agentic` list adds it to the skill. R01 does not need an entry: its chapter also has the learner write contract v0 (design §12, rung 3), but its record lists repair files, so `/contract R01` runs.
- **Why:** spec §5.1 (rung 3: "`/contract Q03` refuses by design") and §5.2 (v0 is written by hand, below the marker); design §12 rung 3; QPLAN §4.4 and §6 Q2.
- **Touches:** Δ111; spec §5.1, §5.2; QPLAN §4.4, §6 Q2; `.claude/skills/contract/SKILL.md` (Step 0 item 4).
- **Owner:** DV2a (the fix round's records lane wrote the skill rule).

### Δ115 · Each file's shipped hole count is pinned in `expected/<ID>.counts` — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on QPLAN §6 Q8 (where to pin the hole count that each file states, Δ104). It amends Δ96: the count pins gain shipped hole counts. It also settles the hole-count half of the Δ113 Open item on CI's verify jobs.
- **Change:**
  - **The pin.** `expected/<ID>.counts` pins, beside Δ96's verified counts, the shipped hole count of each Dafny and Verus file that the registry lists for the module in `exercises`, `exits` and `agentic`. That covers the unit files, the check units, the exit files and the agent files, but not the specimen directories that `exercises` also lists. Δ113 found that nobody compared the exit and agent files' shipped states. The number is the tool's count on the file as shipped. The author's pinning command writes it; nobody types it by hand. A specimen gets no hole-count pin, because a specimen's count can be the answer to one of its predictions.
  - **The check.** The tools compare the pin with the tool's count, and a mismatch fails. The check must also hold in the learner's checkout, where filled holes lower the count (this is why Δ112 pins no agent file's RED output). So it has to compare the files as shipped, using the baseline that `firsttry --report --strict` uses (the merge base of HEAD with `main`), or run only on the author's side. The tools owner records which when it is built.
  - **The chapters (DV2b) quote the pinned number.** The file headers already state it, so the tool count, the chapter, the header and the CI pin agree (design §14.4). QPLAN §7.5 lists the current counts.
- **Why:** QPLAN §6 Q8 and §7.5. In Δ113, CI compared only the units' counts with their items' shipped hole forms, and the integration lane checked the exit and agent files by hand. Design §14.4 requires "hole counts by tool = chapter = CI pin"; spec §2.4 and §3.10 (an exit header states the exact shipped hole count).
- **Touches:** Δ11, Δ96, Δ104, Δ112, Δ113; design §14.4; spec §2.4, §3.10; `expected/README.md`, `scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/pins.py`.
- **Owner:** DV2a (the decision); the tools owner (the pin, its writer and its check); DV2b (the chapters quote it); every arming phase re-pins.
- **Open:**
  - **Tools owner.** The pin was not built when this entry was written. The lane that builds it records the format and the check. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120), which records the format and the check.

### Δ116 · Exit thresholds of the practice tiers and of R06 and R11 — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on QPLAN §6 Q11. It settles the open part of Δ102 ("DV2 decides the exit thresholds of the practice tiers and of R06 and R11") and completes Δ102's threshold table.
- **Change:** `firsttry.sh <ID> --exit` scores these modules as follows:
  - **Q10–Q12 (intro practice):** at least 1 of the 2 timed items.
  - **Q13–Q15 (intermediate):** the 1 timed item.
  - **Q16–Q18 (hard):** no numeric threshold. The exit is the verifier part plus the reviews (`/review-code`, and `/grade-paper` before and after), so `firsttry` keeps printing the score with exit 0 for these modules.
  - **R06 and R11 (the R checkpoints, 2 Dafny and 2 Verus items):** at least 3 of 4, with at least 1 in each language.

  The verifier part (OD3), the lab acceptance and the vivas remain separate tier-gate checks, as for the earlier tiers.
- **Why:** design §7.1's tier table (intro practice, intermediate and hard rows) and §8.1 (R checkpoints: "2 D + 2 V, ≥3/4, ≥1 per language"); Δ102; QPLAN §6 Q11.
- **Touches:** Δ102; design §7.1, §8.1; spec §3.10; `scripts/firsttry.py`.
- **Owner:** DV2a (the decision); the tools owner (`firsttry`'s threshold table), before DV5 arms Q10 and before R06 is armed.
- **Open:**
  - **Tools owner.** The thresholds were not built when this entry was written. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120).

### Δ117 · Verdict pins are salted with the module and item id — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on the Δ113 Open item about unsalted verdict pins. It amends Δ72 ("only the answer's hash is ever pinned") and Δ102 (verdict pins).
- **Change:**
  - **The hash.** Each line of `expected/<ID>.pins` hashes the normalized answer together with its module id and item id. Equal answers of different items therefore get different hashes, and a table of well-known hashes of short strings matches nothing.
  - **Readers and re-pins.** Every reader of the pins hashes the same way: `firsttry --check`, `pins.py`, and `pins.py --ci` and `--ref`. The pins of every armed module are re-captured with `pins.py --update --ref --module <ID>`.
  - **What the salt does not do.** The salt is public. It removes the equality signal and precomputed lookups, but it does not stop a brute force over a small answer space. The rule "do not open `expected/` before your predictions are committed" remains the guard against that.
  - **The exact hash input** is the tools owner's to choose, recorded in `expected/README.md` when built.
- **Why:** Δ113: three Q01 exit items shared one hash, and one-letter and one-digit answers had well-known hashes.
- **Touches:** Δ72, Δ102, Δ113; `scripts/pins.py`, `scripts/firsttry.py`, `expected/README.md`, every `expected/<ID>.pins`.
- **Owner:** DV2a (the decision); the tools owner (the hash, its readers and the re-pins).
- **Open:**
  - **Tools owner.** The salt was not built when this entry was written. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120), which records the hash input.

### Δ118 · Planted trust leaps in S12 delivery specimens carry `// planted: <item id>`; only the shipping lints honour it — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on the first Δ113 Open item (high: it blocks a green `static` job). It amends Δ112 (fake-green delivery specimens) and the shipping lints of Δ102 (`firsttry --lint-hollow`) and Δ103 (`reference_lint.py`).
- **Change:**
  - **The marker.** Some fake-green delivery specimens of strand-S12 verdict items are built around a planted trust leap that the learner-mode lint flags. In such a specimen the leap carries the marker `// planted: <item id>`, which names the item the specimen serves.
  - **Who honours it.** Only the shipping lints honour the marker: `firsttry --lint-hollow` and `reference_lint.py` skip a marked planted line. `vcheck` and `vaudit` never honour it. The gate and the audit report the line as they report any other, because finding it with an instrument is the learner's exercise.
  - **The exact scope** is the tools owner's to fix and record when built: which line a marker covers, and what happens to an orphan marker or to a marker outside an S12 delivery specimen.
- **Why:** Δ113. `firsttry --lint-hollow --all` failed the `static` job on one planted line, and `reference_lint.py` has the same conflict with delivery twins, which is why Δ112 lets some delivery specimens go without a twin.
- **Touches:** Δ102, Δ103, Δ112, Δ113; spec §3.13, §3.14; `scripts/firsttry.py`, `scripts/reference_lint.py`, `specimens/Q03/`.
- **Owner:** DV2a (the decision); the tools owner (the lints); the Q03 lane (the markers).
- **Open:**
  - **Tools owner and the Q03 lane.** The marker and the lints' exemption were not built when this entry was written. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120), which records the scope.
  - **Orchestrator (medium: a hint in a learner-visible file).** **Mitigated** in the fix round (Δ120): every Q03 delivery carries exactly one marker, whatever its species, so the marker's presence carries no species information; the decision stands unless the orchestrator switches to one of the alternatives below. The marker is a course comment in a specimen that the learner reads before recording its diagnosis, and it points at the line the delivery is built around. Spec §3.14's rule that comments are contractor prose does not cover a course marker, so a learner will take the marker at face value. Two alternatives would keep the exemption out of the learner's view:
    - have the shipping lints skip the trust findings of a specimen whose existing item header names an S12 verdict item, which every S12 specimen carries, healthy ones included;
    - keep the exemption in a hashed list under `expected/`, salted like Δ117's pins.

### Δ119 · DV2a fix round: QPLAN §6 dispositions, the Q03-on comment and the status texts — adopted (DV2a, 2026-10-04)
- **Status:** closes the orchestrator item of Δ113's Open list, together with Δ114 and Δ116. The fix round then built the deferred items Q3, Q4, Q5, Q7, Q9 and Q10 (Δ120); Q1 and Q6 stay open. DV3a disposes of both again (Δ135): Q1 is decided for DV3a's part, and Q6 is built in DV3a with QPLAN §6 Q18.
- **Change:**
  - **QPLAN §6, item by item.** Each §6 item now ends with the same resolution line as here.
    - **Q1 (re-posed targets, F3):** deferred to DV3a and DV5. QPLAN §1.7 F3's two options stand until DV3a decides with DV5: the three re-posed Q02–Q03 targets either stay out of L3–L5, or Q10 poses fresh twins.
    - **Resolved by their own entries:** Q2 by Δ114, Q8 by Δ115, Q11 by Δ116.
    - **Deferred to the tools owner.** Δ113's Open list carries each of these except Q3, which this entry records:
      - Q3: `vcheck` has no test mode, and Q02's agent file keeps its static checks;
      - Q4: code T items have no first-try recorder, and the CALIBRATION note stays the first-run record;
      - Q5: the cost of `BV` on `Seq` goals;
      - Q6: there is no region gate for annotation holes (with Δ98);
      - Q7: `specfreeze` should drop `open`/`closed` for an open-body symbol;
      - Q9: `dvl.verus` and proof blocks, where Q03.H1v's order stays the workaround;
      - Q10: card demos have no H5 guard.
    - **Q12, done:** the comment above `dafny/options/core.toml`'s Q03–Q18 override now reads only "Manual lemma induction from Q03 on (Δ45)." The sentence it dropped said that the R track's induction setting is DVR1's call. That still stands, and this entry is now where it is recorded. `gen_units.py` copies the options, not the comments, so no generated unit changed.

- **Status:** records what the fix round's tools lane, its Q01, Q02 and Q03 lanes and its integration lane built for Δ114–Δ119 and for Δ113's Open items, from their reports and the files as built. It closes the "not built" Open items of Δ115–Δ118 and the deferred tool items of Δ119 (Q3, Q4, Q5, Q7, Q9, Q10), each marked in place; Δ113's Open items are marked in place too. QPLAN §6 (the **Built** lines), §7.2–§7.5 (the fix-round bullets) and §7.7 list the same changes item by item. Its Open items are marked in place by DV2b (Δ121–Δ124). Like Δ110–Δ119, this entry names no answer and describes no tool behaviour that a Q01–Q03 prediction asks about; the author-only detail is in `Solutions/plans/Q01-Q03-author-notes.md` §2.8, `Solutions/plans/Q02-author-notes.md` §8 and `Solutions/plans/Q03-code-notes.md` §7.
- **Change:**
  - **Tools** (`scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/firsttry.py`, `scripts/pins.py`, `scripts/reference_lint.py`, `scripts/specfreeze`, `scripts/ci.sh`, `scripts/dvl/verus.py`, `scripts/dvl/verus_spec.py`, `scripts/dvl/pins.toml`; `expected/README.md` documents the formats and markers):
    - **The planted marker (Δ118 as built).** `// planted: <item id>` trails the planted line or sits alone on the line directly above it. It is valid only in a specimen and only when it names an S12 verdict P item of that same file (`firsttry.sr_planted`). Only `firsttry --lint-hollow` and `reference_lint.py` drop their lint findings on the line it covers; a misused marker is a problem (`planted-marker` in `reference_lint.py`). `vcheck` and `vaudit` never read it, and a tool test shows that `vaudit` still reports the leap.
    - **Salted verdict pins (Δ117 as built).** A pin line is `<K><n><lang>  salted-sha256 <hex>`, the sha256 of `"<module id>:<item id>:<normalized answer>"`, and every writer writes this form. The legacy `sha256` form stays readable by `firsttry`, `pins.py` learner mode and `pins.py --ref` only while `pins.py`'s `LEGACY_PINS_READABLE` is true: DV2b's integration sets it false and deletes the reader. `pins.py --ci` reports every legacy pin as a problem in every context, so none can be merged.
    - **Per-file shipped state (Δ115 as built).** `expected/<ID>.counts` is schema 2: beside the `[[unit]]` tables, one `[[file]]` per non-specimen Dafny or Verus file of the module, with `path`, `role` (`exercise`, `check`, `exit`, `agent`) and the file's shipped `verdict`, `holes` and `errors` in the learner loop. `vcheck --ref --pin-counts <ID>` writes it from the learner files as they stand (run on the files as shipped). `vcheck --shipped <ID> [d|v]` compares the files with it; `scripts/ci.sh`'s verify jobs run it. Δ115 asked how the check behaves in the learner's checkout: `vcheck --shipped` checks the course as shipped and fails once a file is worked on, so it belongs to the author's CI (as the verify jobs' unit-hole check already did), while `pins.py --ci` checks only the pins' structure and roles and holds in any checkout. A schema-1 file is still read for its units, and `pins.py --ci` flags it.
    - **Exit thresholds (Δ116 as built).** `firsttry`'s `EXIT_TIERS` and `EXIT_MODULES`: Q10–Q12 at least 1 of 2, Q13–Q15 the 1 timed item, R06 and R11 3 of 4 with at least 1 per language; for Q16–Q18 `--exit` names the verifier part plus the reviews instead of a threshold. The concept tiers are unchanged.
    - **CI's verify jobs** run `vcheck --shipped --json <ID> <lang>` (every unit, check unit, exit file and agent file against its pin) and read the per-item hole check from the same output. Author runs also gate and audit every exit, check and agent twin; a missing twin, or a twin directory with no source file of its own, fails.
    - **QPLAN §6 Q3:** `scripts/vcheck --test [--json] [--name N …] <unit | crate | file | ID [d|v]>` runs the compiled checks (`dvl.dafny.test`, `dvl.verus.test`) and prints PASSED/FAILED lines. It refuses reference targets, specimens whose first tries are unrecorded, `after:` demos, and any target holding an enabled slot with no recorded first try.
    - **QPLAN §6 Q4:** `firsttry --check` records the first try of a code T item: the first `--check` at which its block holds the learner's own code beyond what shipped and no hole form, judged with the block's commented checks enabled in a scratch copy, and recorded as `predict` only while every shipped check is still commented. `--report` shows each test-out's state, and `--report --strict` fails on an unattempted or unrecorded one.
    - **QPLAN §6 Q5:** `scripts/dvl/pins.toml` sets `eb_mode_timeout_s = { BV = 120 }` (validated by `dvl.verus.pins()`); a caller may only shorten it, and a kill (exit 124) counts as the variant failing (Δ58). Every `BV` job that closed its goal took 0.4–1.2 s on the pin, so 120 s is a hundred times the slowest.
    - **QPLAN §6 Q7:** for a Verus symbol listed in `open_bodies`, `specfreeze` compares the statement without `uninterp`, `open` and `closed`; no existing freeze pin changed.
    - **QPLAN §6 Q9:** `dvl.verus_spec` ends a block-like statement (`if`/`else`, `match`, a loop, `unsafe`, a plain block) at its closing brace, so a `proof { }` block right after it is a statement of its own, and E0, E0H and E1 strip it with the ghost `let` it uses (fixture `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/eb_ghost.rs`).
    - **QPLAN §6 Q10:** `// after: <item id>` lines in a specimen make `vcheck`, `vaudit` and `vcheck --test` refuse it until each named item's first try is recorded (H5), and `pins.py --update` seals its sidecar: an `after:` line and an `outcome: salted-sha256 …` line replace the readable exit code, verdict, stage, counts and holes, and only the normalized diagnostics follow. `pins.py --ci` flags an unsealed sidecar of an `after:` demo and a sealed sidecar of a file with no `after:` line.
    - **Per-item TRIV bodies.** `firsttry --empty-body` passes TRIV the `{ … }` block written in `// triv:` lines inside the item's block of its reference twin; a malformed body is reported and nothing runs for that item.
    - **`pins.py`'s judges** (Δ113 Open): an error on a `compute_only` value slot's own line is a fail at any stage, slots a stopped run left unjudged are re-run with the failed ones commented out in a scratch copy, and a stop on no slot's line is "could not run"; an obligation slot requires a run that reached the verify stage.

## T. Chapters, written track and instructor kits of Q01–Q03 (DV2b)

### Δ121 · Chapter pins, the chapter lint and the sealed sidecars as built — adopted (DV2b, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** builds spec §3.15's pinned chapter outputs and spec §2.4.3's chapter skeleton as tool checks. It amends Δ102 (`pins.py`) and Δ104 (the `static` and `pins` jobs), and closes two of Δ120's Open items (the sealed sidecars' readable diagnostics, the legacy pin reader), each marked in place.
- **Change:**
  - **The fence syntax.** In a chapter, an output fence is bare (no info string, at column 0) and is preceded by the line `<!-- pin: <id> -->` (blank lines between are allowed); source fences carry their language tag. `<id>` names an `[[entry]]` of `expected/<ID>.chapter.toml` (schema 1). An entry gives `cmd`: a course tool as the learner types it (`scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/vaudit`, `scripts/specfreeze`, `scripts/firsttry.sh` with `--report` or `--lint-hollow`; never an author-only or state-writing mode) or the pinned `dafny` or `verus` binary on scratch files. It also gives `cwd` (`"root"`, or `"scratch"`: a fresh directory holding the files of `[entry.scratch."<name>"]`, each given as `text`, `copy = "<repo path>"` or `fence = "above"`, the nearest language-tagged fence above the pin line), `display`, `inputs`, `answers` (the predictions the output settles), `excerpt = [first, last]` (the fence then ends with a `...` line and the prose names the cut, spec §3.15 rule 4), `guards` (`"open"` or `"closed"`), `note` and `[entry.normalize]` (`prompt`, `exit`, `drop`, `replace`). The fixed normalization drops ANSI codes and trailing blanks and redacts the scratch directory, the course root and `$HOME`. `[entry.pinned]` holds hashes only (the fence body's and each input's, as captured) and is written by the tool. `expected/README.md` documents the format.
  - **Recorded quotes.** A `kind = "recorded"` entry quotes output that no course command can re-run (a guard refusal from the DV1c acceptance archive; an import outcome captured in a scratch course root). `source` names the record in the author probe archive, and the fence body is the author's copy. `pins.py --update` checks the copy line by line against the record. CI checks the body hash everywhere; only the author's context re-reads the record.
  - **Modes.** `pins.py --update --chapter <ID> [--entry E …]` (author-only) runs each entry, writes its output into the chapter's fence and the hashes into the pin file (a canonical rewrite, so notes live in `note`), names every entry that failed to capture (exit 1), writes nothing if the chapter changed during the run, and aborts on a toolchain error. `pins.py --chapter <ID> [--entry E …]` re-runs every entry and compares; `pins.py --ci` includes every armed module's chapter pins. An entry whose inputs changed since its capture is stale: in the author's context that is a problem (re-capture, spec §3.15 rule 9); elsewhere the entry is skipped with a count, since the change is the learner's own work. A `guards = "closed"` entry counts the calibration file of each prediction it waits for among its inputs, so a recorded first try makes its quoted refusal stale instead of a mismatch. Mismatches print locations only.
  - **The H5 hold.** Outside the author's context, `pins.py --chapter` and `pins.py --ci` do not run a `guards = "open"` entry whose output settles a prediction with no recorded first try. They count it as "not run" and say so, so a learner's checkout never computes such an answer, even unseen.
  - **`scripts/chapter_lint.py`** (new, static) checks the chapter skeleton: the title and the opening; `## 0`; a files table that names every exercise, check, exit and agent file of `expected/<ID>.counts` with its tool-counted hole count (a row of two files states `a + b holes` in file order, or `1 hole each`) and an agent file's planted errors; numbered sections, one per strand in strand order, with `---` above every H2 but the first; the required sections (paper exactly when `paper = true`; the exercise file; the agentic exercise with its CONTRACT blockquote, or without one for a hand-written exercise, which is Q03 by Δ114; the instruments; the exit; the touchpoints naming every skill; the harvest); one predict callout per P item; every pinned fence that settles a prediction (its `answers`, plus the `after:` lines and slots of its specimen inputs) placed after that item's callout and never quoting a later module's prediction; 3 to 5 mermaid diagrams, each with a sentence before and after; every bare fence pinned; every item id known; and `pins.py`'s static half. A missing chapter of an armed module is a problem. `scripts/ci.sh`'s `static` job runs `chapter_lint.py --all`, which passes loudly when nothing is armed.
  - **Sealed sidecars show no output line.** After `command:` a sealed sidecar has only the `after:` line and `outcome: salted-sha256 …`, one salted hash over the whole normalized output, diagnostics included. The hash input is unchanged from DV2a, so the 15 re-pinned sidecars kept byte-identical digests. `pins.py --ci` flags a sealed sidecar that still shows lines (the pre-DV2b form). A chapter that shows such a demo's output quotes a real run in a pinned fence, after the box.
  - **The legacy verdict-pin reader is retired.** `LEGACY_PINS_READABLE` is false and `firsttry.sr_answer_hash` is deleted. A legacy pin still parses, so that `pins.py --ci` can name it as a problem; none is left in `expected/`, which holds 31 salted pins (Q01 15, Q02 5, Q03 11).
- **Why:** the tools lane's runs: `tests.wrappers.test_tools_static`, 91 tests OK (1 skipped), with the new ChapterPins (8) and ChapterLint (7) cases; `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`, 27 OK; `tests.wrappers.test_tools_tool`, 33 OK in 375 s, with a ChapterPinsTool case on real runs (a module probe, a plain binary with its exit code, an `after:` demo with its guards open, the H5 hold, staleness, drift reported by location); the `tests/ci` tool tests, 7 OK; the 15 `after:` demos re-pinned through `pins.py --update`. The integration lane's re-runs are in Δ124.
- **Touches:** Δ36, Δ37, Δ59, Δ102, Δ104, Δ114, Δ117, Δ120; spec §2.4.3, §3.15; `scripts/pins.py`, `scripts/chapter_lint.py`, `scripts/ci.sh`, `scripts/firsttry.py`, `expected/README.md`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_static.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_tool.py`, `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`, the 15 sealed sidecars under `expected/specimens/`.
- **Owner:** DV2b (the tools lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Tools owner (low).** `chapter_lint.py`'s `HAND_WRITTEN = {"Q03": "contract v0"}` copies Δ114's list in `.claude/skills/contract/SKILL.md` by hand. A later module with a hand-written agentic exercise must be added in both places. **Closed** in DV2b-2 (Δ126: one list, `.claude/skills/contract/hand_written.toml`, read by both).
  - **Tools owner (low, accepted).** Entries that run the plain `dafny` or `verus` binary are not forced onto the module's pinned flags (spec §3.15 rule 8). Each one quotes a prediction about the plain tools (two in Q01, one in Q03), and its fence shows the command line. Every other entry runs a course tool.
  - **Tools owner (low).** `scripts/vcheck --test` reports a passing Dafny `@Test` method that calls `print` as "no test ran", and a failing one that printed loses its `FAILED <name>` line (the exit code stays 1): the parser misses `<name>: <printed text>` followed by `PASSED` on its own line. Q03's rubric avoids `print` in its grid test for this reason. The Q03 kit lane's repro was a scratch run (`/tmp/dvl0-dv2b-kit-q03/dvl/runs/dafny-test-20261005-074010-v2o0f929`, not archived). **Closed** in DV2b-2 (Δ126: `dvl.dafny`'s test parser, with three real runs pinned as tool-free fixtures).

### Δ122 · The Q01–Q03 chapters as built — adopted (DV2b, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** the course's first chapters. Spec §2.4.3's skeleton holds as `chapter_lint.py` checks it (Δ121), with the deviations below.
- **Change:**
  - **The three chapters.** `docs/Q01_Hello.md`: 13 strand sections, 11 predict items in 9 callouts, 3 diagrams, 28 pinned fences (26 command entries, 2 recorded refusals). `docs/Q02_FirstSyntaxI.md`: 6 strand sections, 10 items, 5 diagrams, 32 fences (30 command entries, 2 recorded quotes). `docs/Q03_FirstSyntaxII.md`: 7 strand sections (S7–S13), 14 items, 5 diagrams, 34 command entries. Every quoted output is a live capture on the pins, except the four recorded quotes.
  - **Length.** `chapter_lint.py` counts prose words, fence bodies excluded (tables, headings and pin comments included): Q01 5,584, Q02 5,192, Q03 5,538, inside both the phase brief's 4,500–6,000 and spec §2.5's 4,800–6,600. With the fences, `wc -w` gives 6,999, 7,100 and 7,281. The phase measures a chapter's length by its prose, because fenced source and output are worked through at the worked-example and predict rates, not read at 120 wpm. The drafts left by the killed first run (9,684, 7,422 and 8,315 by `wc -w`) were cut to these.
  - **Where the boxes sit.** Q01 asks for all eleven predictions in `## 0`: the module records every prediction before §1, and every failing Q01 demo and chapter specimen waits for a prediction by an `after:` line. Each strand section then states "**P<n>: answer**" and shows its pinned runs. Q03's `## 0` likewise asks for all fourteen before §1, and each section repeats its box and gives the answer after the words *Commit your prediction first*. Q02 puts each box in its own section. In all three, `chapter_lint.py` confirms that every fence that settles a prediction follows its box, and the integration lane's scan (Δ124) found no answer in prose before its box.
  - **Chapter specimens and probes.** A quoted scenario runs either as a chapter specimen, `specimens/<ID>/chapter-*.specimen` (the prediction's scenario verbatim, with an `// after:` line; Q01 has 15, Q03 8), or as a scratch probe (`cwd = "scratch"`, whose source is the nearest fence above, run as `scripts/vcheck --module <ID> probe.<ext>`; Q01 3 entries, Q02 5, Q03 7). The Q02 lane turned its four draft specimens into probes. The Q03 lane deleted two draft specimens that spelled out a Q03 prediction's answer in a learner-visible file; that check is now quoted from probes whose source appears only after the box. The integration lane's `specfreeze --pin Q01` and `--pin Q03` added the chapter specimens' statements (Q01 43 files, Q03 33); no existing freeze entry changed.
  - **Q01.** The survey opening carries both "Three ideas do all the work" and the "In this module" contents line. The chapter ends with "What Q01 planted" and a one-line pointer, not "Done when" and "Where this is going". The three-doors section quotes two real refusals verbatim from the DV1c acceptance archive (`dv1c/acceptance/refusals.json`; door b quotes the `b2_ref_build_verus` record, whose text the current hook still prints) and summarizes the third door; it dates the quotes and says that the guard's wording may change. Two sentences that nudged Q02 predictions were removed, and no message of a failing variant of the closed-book frame item is quoted, because a Q02 prediction asks about that rejection.
  - **Q02.** The fast channel is defined in §3 and demonstrated in §10 on the agent files, including the documented one-check-at-a-time limit of Verus `compute_only` checks (Δ120). P10dv's two outcomes are recorded quotes; their records were captured in a scratch course root and are archived under `dv2b/q02-chapter/` with a README that says how to re-record them. Each excerpt states its cut (`audit-d`, `audit-v`, `agent-d`, and the recorded `p10dv-v`). Wording that would hint at an exit item's measure was made neutral.
  - **Q03.** §11 (contract v0, written by hand: no CONTRACT blockquote, Δ114) gives the v0 slot format in prose and quotes the contract skill's Step 0 refusal as the text the skill prescribes, not as a captured transcript. The species-to-instrument table and the remark about the solver and induction sit after the boxes they would answer. `--analyze-proofs` runs through `scripts/vcheck <specimen> -- --analyze-proofs`, on the pinned flags.
  - **Every output fence drops the wrapper's last line,** `raw output: <dir>` (a fresh path on every run), and each chapter says so once.
  - **The integration lane's edits** (after the lanes; no fence changed): Q01's touchpoints no longer list `/grade-paper Q01 --post`, because Q01's pair holds no blueprint and its rubric says `--post` has nothing to re-score. Q03 §9 now names the pair's four problems (the S8 lab B1–B2, the H1d blueprint B3, the card B4) and `--post`; Q03's touchpoints list `/grade-paper Q03 --post`; and `## 0`'s minute line reads "blueprint and paper card 15".
- **Why:** the chapter lanes' captures (`pins.py --update --chapter`, one solver job at a time in a 16 GB scope) and their checks; the integration lane's re-runs and lint (Δ124).
- **Touches:** Δ114, Δ120, Δ121; spec §2.4.3, §2.5, §3.15; QPLAN §2–§4; `docs/Q01_Hello.md`, `docs/Q02_FirstSyntaxI.md`, `docs/Q03_FirstSyntaxII.md`, `expected/Q01.chapter.toml`, `expected/Q02.chapter.toml`, `expected/Q03.chapter.toml`, `specimens/Q01/chapter-*`, `specimens/Q03/chapter-*`, `expected/Q01.freeze`, `expected/Q03.freeze`.
- **Owner:** DV2b (the chapter lanes; the integration lane's edits).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Orchestrator (low).** Confirm that a chapter's length is its prose (above), or ask for cuts of paired source and output fences.
  - **Orchestrator (low, accepted by the lanes).** Q01's map exit is closed-book recall of what the chapter teaches, so the chapter states the facts that several exit items test (qualified names, the frame clause, the weakest precondition of an assignment, what a green run trusts, what a resource limit buys, holes per clause), each on an example of its own. It never poses an exit item's scenario or options. Confirm that this is the intended exit.
  - **Orchestrator (low).** Q01's quoted refusals name the guard layer and rule, which partly pre-answers the learner's ungraded THREE DOORS block; the chapter says the learner's shell door may meet a different rule.
  - **Author (low).** Recorded quotes are checked against their records only in the author's context. Re-record Q02's P10dv records (archive README) and re-check Q01's door quotes whenever the Q02 unit or crate, the given layers, the toolchain pins or the guard's wording change. Q01's `learner-loop` entry takes every Q01 module file as an input, so any change to a Q01 specimen makes it stale until it is re-captured with `--entry learner-loop`.
  - **Author (low).** Some chapter prose cites tool messages that no fence re-checks: Q01's C4dv table (pinned only through the card demos' sealed sidecars, whose hashes cover them), Q01 §6's "both snippets verify", and Q02's naming of Rust's error code for the C6v demo. A toolchain bump that changes them fails the sidecars, not the chapter.
  - **Q03 lane or orchestrator (low; Δ120's Open item, still open).** A Q03 WRITE-card failure demo's in-file `EXPECT` line bears on a Q03 prediction. Q03's `## 0` asks for every prediction before §1, which keeps the card files unopened until then; changing that demo would close it.

### Δ123 · Written pairs, rubrics, banks, calibration fixtures and leak patterns of Q01–Q03 as built — adopted (DV2b, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** the course's first written pairs and instructor kits (spec §4, §6.7, §6.8, §7.2). It settles the card pin format that Δ91 left to the first card module, and closes Δ120's rubric item (Q02's measures enforced by review). Rule 2 of the leak patterns' README is amended by Δ130 (DV3a, 2026-10-05): a given layer of a tier above a pattern's module is exempt from that pattern.
- **Change:**
  - **The pairs** (`paper/<ID>_problems.tex` → `paper/<ID>_answers.tex`; references in `paper/solutions/<ID>_solutions.tex`):
    - **Q01:** one problem, B1 [paper-card] 02.3 (15 minutes). As the course's first card, the problems file defines what each of the eight fields asks. Q01's answers skeleton labels the trust field `Trust:`; Q02's uses `What is trusted:`.
    - **Q02:** B1 [informal-proof-first] "Normalizing keeps the value" (a blueprint for Q02.H3d in (a) and for the loop invariant of Q02.H2v in (b); `--post` re-scores it), B2 [concept] "True is not inductive", and B3 [paper-card] 02.7: 6 + 3 + 6 = 15 minutes. QPLAN lists only the card and "the written pair"; B2 gives the flawed fixture a clean control.
    - **Q03:** B1 and B2 [hand-calculation], QPLAN's 20-minute S8 lab (the weakest precondition of loop-free code; the verification conditions of a loop whose invariant lacks one conjunct, beside a type that supplies it); B3 [informal-proof-first], a blueprint for Q03.H1d (QPLAN names no blueprint problem; H1d is the module's first manual induction); and B4 [paper-card] 02.13: 10 + 10 + 7 + 8 minutes. B2's first loop was replaced after a probe showed that the dropped conjunct was not needed; the shipped loop's claims were captured on the pins in both languages and with both integer types.
  - **Card pins** (the format Δ91 left to the first card module). A [paper-card] problem names `~/code/research/agentic-formal-veri/cheatsheets.md`, the card's line range (from its heading through its "Why it matters" paragraph: Q01 642–668, Q02 766–791, Q03 944–967), the command `sed -n '<a>,<b>p' <file> | sha256sum`, and the hash it prints. The hash is a real value, not PENDING, because the file is the learner's own and lies outside the repo. The solutions record, in paraphrase, which lines each field draws on.
  - **Rubrics** (`paper/rubrics/<ID>_rubric.md`). Card fidelity is CF.a–CF.f (CF.e: a figure stays attached to the population, model or aggregate that the card gives it; CF.f: the wording is free). A rubric has a `--post` section where its pair holds a blueprint (Q02 B1, Q03 B3); Q01's rubric says that `--post` has nothing to re-score and that `--report` does not apply. Q02's code-review lines C1 and C5 enforce Q02.X1d's measure and the explicit measures of Q02.H5d and H6d by review. Q03's "Contract v0 review — after you write Q-v0" (D1–D7: a self-review settled by `CLAUDE.md`, the transcripts and commands; no Claude session reviews v0) replaces the audit debrief, and its gate line A5 is `specfreeze --check Q03`. The integration lane filled the 16 rubric pins (Q01 6, Q02 6, Q03 4) with `update_rubric_pins.sh`.
  - **Banks** (`.claude/skills/instructor/banks/<ID>.md`):
    - **Q01:** 8 questions (2 reconstruct, 2 predict, 2 why, 1 connect, 1 transfer), all `viva: no` (the module has no viva). No earlier module exists, so no question has a backward edge: K7 points to the paper card instead, and the bank notes tell `/discuss` to close with K8 until B1 is written.
    - **Q02:** 9 questions, all `viva: yes`, split into three pairwise-disjoint groups A–C, each with a predict and a backward edge to Q01; K1 is spare.
    - **Q03:** 8 questions plus the tier-viva draw table: three pairwise-disjoint draws A–C over the Q02 and Q03 pools, each on 5 strands, from both modules, with 2 predicts and at least one backward edge. No fourth disjoint draw exists; the bank says that `/viva` then reports the gap.
    - **Parallel problems:** two per module (Q01 parallel to H2 and H3; Q02 to H2d/H2v and to H3d, H4d and H7v; Q03 to H1 and H2), each shipped as a hole form that verifies with exactly one hole. The reference bodies stay out of every learner-visible file.
    - **Every quoted bank output** is a capture on the pins through `scripts/vcheck`, run from `instructor/scratch/` in a scratch course root and re-checked against the code the bank shows. The probe sources are archived under `dv2b/q01-kit/`, `dv2b/q02-kit/` and `dv2b/q03-kit/` (the integration lane copied Q01's and Q03's from lane scratch, with READMEs).
  - **Calibration fixtures** (`tests/instructor/fixtures/<ID>_{flawed,gold}_answers.tex`; each compiles as a drop-in answers file). Q01's pair has one problem, so its flawed fixture plants three flawed fields and leaves five clean: the clean control of spec §4.7 sits at field level, with one EXPECTED line per field. Q02's flawed fixture plants flaws in B1 and B3 and keeps B2 clean; Q03's plants flaws in B1, B3 and B4 and keeps B2 clean. The gold fixtures are paraphrased away from the references, and every probe claim in them was captured.
  - **Leak patterns** (`tests/instructor/leak_patterns/<ID>.txt`, read-denied): 8 per module, cut only from H, X and A answers, never from an item that a blueprint problem covers (Q02.H1d, H2v and H3d; Q03.H1d), because a grader quoting a learner's blueprint would trip them. The README holds the format, the module table and the verifier. The verifier is stricter than spec §7.2: each pattern must match its own `# from` file, and rule 2 is checked against every learner-visible file of the checkout. It prints `OK|FAIL sol=N prob=N chap=N pub=N <pattern>`.
- **Why:** the kit lanes' runs: `make -C paper paper` (6 documents, 0 overfull boxes) and `make -C paper paper-solutions` (3); each fixture compiled as a drop-in answers file; every bank and rubric probe captured through `scripts/vcheck` (`--module`, `--gate`, `--test`) and `scripts/vaudit`, one solver job at a time in a 16 GB scope; the leak verifier; `check_course_map.py` with `--only vocab` and the I5 residue check. The integration lane's re-runs are in Δ124.
- **Touches:** Δ74, Δ89, Δ91, Δ114, Δ120; spec §4, §6.7, §6.8, §6.12, §7.2; QPLAN §2.5, §3.5, §4.5; the files above.
- **Owner:** DV2b (the kit lanes).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Orchestrator and the guard's owner (medium).** The calibration fixtures sit in `tests/instructor/fixtures/`, which no deny rule or hook rule covers and which neither the *Predictions first* list nor CORE's MAY NOT list names. A gold fixture is a correct answer set by design (spec §4.7). So Q02's gold B1 is a prose blueprint of Q02.H3d and states Q02.H2v's invariant (the flawed B1 states the invariant too), and Q03's gold B3 is a blueprint of Q03.H1d. Their headers say "open only after <ID> is done". Options: a fifth canonical deny rule plus a hook rule-1 path for the fixtures (only the author's calibration runs of spec §6.12 read them), or naming them in the *Predictions first* list and in CORE's MAY NOT list. The spec places the fixtures, so the choice is the orchestrator's. **Decided and built** in DV2b-2 (Δ125: the fixtures join the reference set under hook rule 1, in `CLAUDE.md`, `CORE.md` and the *Predictions first* list; no fifth deny rule).
  - **Author, before Release A (medium).** Spec §6.12's calibration was not run, because it calls a model: a headless `/grade-paper` over both fixtures of each module, the leak check of the instructor's output against the patterns, and a simulated `/discuss`. **Partly done** in DV2b-2: the simulated `/discuss` and `/viva` passed (Δ127); the harness for the grader runs and the leak check is built, but its 12 sessions were not run (Δ128), so this item stays open and holds Release A (Δ129).
  - **Author (low).** The card pins depend on the learner's `cheatsheets.md` staying at its path with those lines unchanged. When it changes, renew the range and the hash together in the problems file, the solutions and, for Q02 and Q03, the fixtures; `/grade-paper` refuses a stale card pin.
  - **Author (low).** The banks quote tool-format details (a gate's refusal line, for example) that drift with `scripts/vcheck`'s output: re-capture from the archived probes after a change of the tools, the pins or L1–L2. Q03's draw table depends on Q02's question ids, strands and `viva` fields.
  - **Skills owner (low).** `/discuss` requires one backward-edge question per session but says nothing for a bank that has none (Q01). Q01's bank documents its substitute; the skill could gain a one-line rule. **Closed** in DV2b-2 (Δ126: the rule follows the bank notes' substitute).
