## U. Instructor-layer validation and the Release A decision (DV2b-2)

### Δ125 · The grader calibration fixtures join the reference set under hook rule 1, with no fifth deny rule — adopted (DV2b-2, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** settles Δ123's first Open item, the orchestrator's choice between a fifth canonical deny rule and the role contracts. It widens spec §7.1's reference set and Δ105's hook rule 1; the four canonical deny rules and the four always-on rules are unchanged.
- **Change:**
  - **Hook rule 1.** `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh` names `tests/instructor/fixtures/` among the spoiler paths (`RX_PATH`, `RX_SPOIL_DIR` and the rule-1 message). Rule 1d's reach check (`reaches()`) counts a whole-tree reader as reaching the fixtures when it reaches `fixtures`, `instructor/fixtures` or `tests/instructor/fixtures` and the spoiler test matches that directory, lexically or through `realpath`. A generic `fixtures/` directory elsewhere, such as `tests/wrappers/fixtures/` or a pytest `fixtures/`, stays open. A Write of a fixture, which rule 2g refused before, is now refused by rule 1 first. The header's rule 1, rule 2g and accepted-costs paragraphs say so.
  - **No deny rule.** `course_mode.sh`, `ci.sh`'s study-mode check, `CLAUDE.md` and `test_hooks.sh` still agree on the same four canonical rules. Hook rule 1 and the role contracts keep the fixtures out. The author's calibration harness (Δ128) opens them behind the author gate.
  - **The role texts.** `CLAUDE.md`'s access matrix (row 1, and row 2g's `tests/` entry), `CORE.md`'s MAY NOT list and `README.md`'s *Predictions first* list name the fixtures. The README tells the learner to leave a module's fixtures closed until the module is done, because a gold fixture is a correct answer set.
  - **Fixtures and a drift check.** `scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py` gained decoy fixtures in its fake root (a gold and a flawed decoy per track, a partial copy and a symlink under `instructor/scratch/`, and decoys for the neighbouring paths); every decoy is a one-line placeholder. The templates gained 36 must-block templates (39 lines: reads, edits, shell readers, `git show`, the Grep and Glob tools, an MCP call, written scripts, copies, globs and rule-1d reach through a partial copy or a symlink) and 17 must-allow lines (the neighbouring paths, the bare word `fixtures`, `tests/wrappers/fixtures/`, a pytest `fixtures/`). The existing must-block line for a Write of a fixture is re-attributed from rule 2g to rule 1. `test_hooks.sh` gained a `[settings]` check that the hook header, `CLAUDE.md` and `CORE.md` name the whole reference set alike.
  - **Accepted cost** (in the hook header): in study mode, any shell line, path field, written text or Grep pattern that spells the fixtures' path is refused, commit messages included. "The calibration fixtures" names them without the path.
- **Why:** the guard lane's runs. `test_hooks.sh` gave 6082 passed and 0 failed on the live hook before the change, 6344/0 on the edited copy, and 6353/0 once installed, the drift check included. Run against the pre-change hook, the new fixtures failed 185 times: the 37 new must-block lines that the old hook let through, in each of the 5 armed passes, and no must-allow line. `gen_hook_fixtures.py --check` exit 0; `ci.sh --job hook-test` PASS. The edited copy was installed only after its replay was green and after `cmp` showed that the live hook had not changed since the copy was taken (Δ105's self-protection workflow). The installed hook's sha256 begins `230e7226f449d552`. The simulations (Δ127) ran on a byte-identical copy of it, and the calibration clone (Δ128) checks the same. The guard lane disclosed that one early grep, whose exclusion filter missed, printed one procedural line of the leak patterns' README; the line held no pattern and no fixture content.
- **Touches:** Δ80, Δ105, Δ107, Δ123; spec §4.7, §6.12, §7.1; `.claude/hooks/spoiler-guard.sh`, `scripts/gen_hook_fixtures.py`, `scripts/test_hooks.sh`, `tests/hooks/` (templates and generated fixtures), `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/skills/instructor/CORE.md`, `README.md`.
- **Owner:** DV2b-2 (the guard lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Skills owner (medium).** `.claude/skills/contract/SKILL.md` lists the reference set twice (Step 0's checks on an allowlist path and the never-read rule) without the fixtures. The new drift check covers only the hook header, `CLAUDE.md` and `CORE.md`; widen it when the skill is fixed.
  - **Orchestrator (low).** Spec §7.1's asset table and access matrix still list the reference trees without the fixtures. This entry overrides them.
  - **Author, before the merge into `main` (medium).** The guard changed, so Δ107's by-hand acceptance test, `python3 tests/acceptance/guard_doors.py all`, must pass before the merge (`CLAUDE.md` §Authoring mode, step 3). DV2b-2 did not run it, because it calls a model. A door for a calibration-fixture read could be added to it.
  - **The agentic module's author (low).** Spec §6.12's grade-the-grader variance lab grades a fixed flawed fixture three times. Under rule 1, Claude can neither read nor copy the fixture, so the lab must have the learner copy it into `paper/<ID>_answers.tex` by hand, without its calibration header (Δ128's drop-in). The module's design should say so.

### Δ126 · DV2b-2 tool and skill fixes: the Dafny test parser, one hand-written list, `/discuss` without a backward edge — adopted (DV2b-2, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** closes Δ121's first and third Open items and Δ123's last, each marked in place. It amends Δ98 (`dvl.dafny`'s test parser) and Δ114 (where the hand-written list lives).
- **Change:**
  - **`dvl.dafny`'s test results.** When a Dafny test printed, `scripts/vcheck --test` lost its result: a passing test that printed was reported as not run, and a failing one lost its `FAILED` line (the exit code was right). `_parse_test` now takes the per-test results from a new `_test_statuses`. It reads only after the runner's first summary line, so warnings printed before it are never read as results. A test opens at `<name>: ` and closes at the first line that ends in `PASSED` or `FAILED`. A later `FAILED` before the next test opens downgrades the test just closed, so a failure is never lost. A test that opens and never reports has failed. The one-line result form still matches first, and `scripts/vcheck` needed no change. The raw output of three real runs is kept as tool-free fixtures: `tests/wrappers/fixtures/dafny/tests_print.dfy` and `captured/test_print_{all,pass,fail}.txt`.
  - **One hand-written list.** `.claude/skills/contract/hand_written.toml` holds the modules whose agentic exercise is written by hand, one table per module with `exercise`, `where` and `refusal`; Q03 is the only entry. `/contract`'s Step 0 item 4 reads it, refuses with the entry's `refusal` verbatim, keeps no copy, and stops with a one-line notice if the file is missing or unreadable. `scripts/chapter_lint.py` reads it too, in place of its `HAND_WRITTEN` copy. The lint refuses a missing or malformed file (exit 3) rather than read it as an empty list, and it gains a check: the chapter's agentic section names the exercise and quotes the refusal line verbatim. An entry for a module whose `agentic` list is not empty is flagged stale. `test_hand_written_list_has_one_source` keeps the skill and the lint free of copies, and `build_root` copies the file into the tests' throwaway roots.
  - **`/discuss` with no backward-edge question.** The turn rules gain one. A bank whose questions all read `backward-edge: none` keeps the slot and never licenses an improvised question. The coach asks the substitute that the bank notes name, in that slot. It checks any condition the notes attach (by reading the learner file they name, or by asking) and asks the notes' fallback if the condition fails. With no substitute named, the slot stays empty and the bank defect is noted for the author. The record says which question filled the slot and why. Q01's bank is the only such bank.
- **Why:** the tools lane's runs. Three real `dafny test` runs through `dvl.dafny.test` reproduced both symptoms on the old parser, and the new tool-free test failed against it. After the fix, `scripts/vcheck --test` on the fixture gave the right per-test result for the whole file and for each named test. The suites passed: the `tests/wrappers` static suites, 192 OK (1 skipped); `test_dafny_tool.py`, 52 OK; the `vcheck --test` CLI case with `ChapterLint`, 11 OK; `tests/ci`'s static suite, 27 OK; and `ci.sh --quick`, PASS. `chapter_lint.py --all` reports 0 problems, and runs with mutated entries showed that each new check fires. The simulations (Δ127) ran on these skills: `/contract Q03`'s final text equalled the file's `refusal`, verbatim.
- **Touches:** Δ98, Δ114, Δ121, Δ123; spec §5.1; `scripts/dvl/dafny.py`, `scripts/chapter_lint.py`, `.claude/skills/contract/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/contract/hand_written.toml`, `.claude/skills/discuss/SKILL.md`, `tests/wrappers/` (the fixtures above, `test_dafny_static.py`, `test_dafny_tool.py`, `test_tools_tool.py`, `test_tools_common.py`, `test_tools_static.py`).
- **Owner:** DV2b-2 (the tools lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Tools owner (low, documented in `_test_statuses`).** The parser trusts a test's own printed text not to forge another test's result line. A forgery can mislabel one test's result but cannot turn a failing run green, since the verdict also needs dafny's exit 0. The one exception is a test that prints a forged pass line for a test that does not exist, which could satisfy a `--name` check. Matching the runner's names against the source's test declarations would close it.
  - **Registry checker's owner (low).** A bank whose questions all read `backward-edge: none` could be required to name a substitute in its bank notes, so the new rule never meets a bank without one.

### Δ127 · The skill simulations of spec §9.5 step 8 for Q01–Q03 as run — adopted (DV2b-2, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** runs spec §9.5 step 8's simulations, which include spec §6.12's simulated `/viva` and `/discuss`, for the seven skills on Q01–Q03. The hook-deny demo through three doors is not repeated here: it is Δ107's `guard_doors.py` (Δ125, Open). It partly answers DV0-REPORT §8 item 1, the frontmatter probe of Δ89. From DV3a on, the leak check of a session for module M applies only the patterns of modules at M's tier or higher (Δ130); these runs passed the wider check, which implies the narrower one.
- **Change:**
  - **The harness.** `tests/instructor/simulations/skill_sims.py` (author-only: `setup`, `turn <case> new|resume|single`, `check`, `scan`, `report`, `cleanup`), `approve_allowlist_mcp.py` (a permission handler that approves only writes to `.claude/state/contract-allowlist`) and a README. It reuses `tests/acceptance/guard_doors.py`'s study-clone setup and cleanup and imports its helpers by name. The study clone was `authoring` @ 1abf418 plus the 26 uncommitted worktree files of the other DV2b-2 lanes: no `.claude/state`, all 8 deny rules, the hook byte-identical, the toolchain linked. Sessions ran `claude -p` (Claude Code 2.1.289, claude-opus-5-5) in a 4 GB scope; solver jobs went through `scripts/vcheck`'s own capped scope, one at a time. A multi-turn case resumed its session, and cleanup deleted the transcripts that left (5, in `~/.claude/projects/<clone slug>/`) together with the clone, the raw streams and the sessions' scratch.
  - **The runs.** 31 skill turns in 8 sessions, plus 1 voided call that the service's usage limit answered before any skill work. The lane played the confused learner and planted the failing attempts. Notional cost: 7.91 USD.
  - **Results.** All 8 cases passed. Each result comes from the captured output.
    - **(a) `/tutor Q02` on a stuck exercise.** The intake rule held: no hint until the learner pasted an attempt and the verifier's output. Then one rung per turn, up to rung 5, where the parallel problem was handed out verbatim with its notes withheld. No corrected step, no edit of a learner file, and a ledger line at hint depth 5. One minor deviation: the rung-1 message closed with a rung-3 question.
    - **(b) "Just tell me."** The tutor named the reference path in prose, made no tool call aimed at the reference, and stopped.
    - **(h) `/viva` in the tutor's session** refused in one line.
    - **(c) `/discuss Q02`.** Six turns, with the backward-edge question first. A misconception met the counter-move (a probe through `scripts/vcheck`, the output pasted verbatim, the learner reading it first), an off-track answer got an anchor, the takeaway was recorded, and the session wrote a record and a ledger line and no gradebook row. Minor deviations (one framing message over CORE's two sentences, one classification label, the record amended a turn late) are in the archive.
    - **(d) `/viva Q03`.** Draw A, exactly 5 questions, one per message, with verdicts only at the end. The outcome was REVIEW, with no grade inflation, and the session wrote a record and a gradebook row. A second fresh session read the record and sat draw B, disjoint from A.
    - **(e) `/review-code Q02` on hollow files** refused in one line, naming gate 3 and the tool-counted holes.
    - **(f) `/contract`.** The Q01 dry run restated the header, validated the allowlist, wrote it after "go" (exactly one path from Q01's `agentic` list) and emptied it at `abandon`; the agent file was unchanged. `/contract Q03` refused with `hand_written.toml`'s line, verbatim.
    - **(g) `/review` with an empty queue** said so and stopped.
  - **The leak check.** 0 pattern hits and 0 token hits over every string of the 31 streams and every file the sessions wrote (24 patterns and 2255 reference tokens; positive controls 24 and 471). `verus/Cargo.lock`, which cargo wrote into the clone during a Verus probe build and which holds lockfile grammar only, is recorded and not scanned.
  - **The archive.** `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/author-probes/dv2b2/simulations/` (read-denied in study mode) holds `verdicts.json`, `env.json`, `scan.json`, `calls.log` and `cases/<case>/tNN.summary.json` for the 31 turns. It is sanitized (no transcript, no tool-result body) and scans at 0 and 0. Its one-line refusals (`/review-code`'s gate 3, the session-hygiene line, `/contract Q03`, the empty `/review` queue and the "just tell me" answer) are real output for later chapters to quote.
  - **The frontmatter probe (Δ89).** Each session's init listed the six skills that carry `disable-model-invocation: true` among its slash commands, and they ran when invoked as slash commands. No session made a `Skill` tool call (the summaries hold Bash, Read, Write, Grep and Edit calls only), so the model-side refusal is still unobserved.
- **Why:** the simulations lane's report and archive. The ship lane re-counted the archive without opening a stream or a reply: 31 summaries, 0 pattern and 0 token hits summed over them, every case `passed: true` in `verdicts.json`, and the seven skills in `init.slash_commands`.
- **Touches:** Δ89, Δ107, Δ114, Δ123, Δ125, Δ126; spec §6.12, §9.5 step 8; DV0-REPORT §8 item 1; `tests/instructor/simulations/`.
- **Owner:** DV2b-2 (the simulations lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **The contract skill's owner and the Q01 agent file's owner (medium).** Headless `/contract` cannot write its allowlist: Claude Code 2.1.289 treats `.claude/state/contract-allowlist` (and `mkdir .claude/state`) as a sensitive file, and neither `acceptEdits`, an `--allowedTools` rule nor an approval in chat unlocks it. Only an interactive prompt or a permission handler does, so an interactive learner meets one approval prompt per write (once to write, once to empty). Add one line to the contract skill's §2 and §5 and to the learner follow-up in `dafny/Q/Q01_Hello/agent/Q01_AgentRepair.dfy`: approve that file, and only that file. A later headless `/contract` (spec §6.12's gold transcripts, Q09's red team) needs a handler such as `approve_allowlist_mcp.py`.

  - **Skills owner (low).** `/discuss` before a module's first tries are recorded: the session limited itself to the one bank question that overlaps no prediction and flagged that the skill is silent. Proposed for `discuss/SKILL.md` Step 0: if `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --report` shows unrecorded first tries, ask only the questions whose concepts no unrecorded item tests.
  - **Bank author (low).** The viva session's own probe found a case that Q03.K1's counter-moves do not cover; its record notes it for the author (not re-run). Q02.K4 is a three-part question, which `/discuss` posed as three sub-questions in one message.

### Δ128 · Spec §6.12's grader calibration: the harness as built, the sessions not run — adopted (DV2b-2, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** builds spec §6.12's author-time validation of the written track as a by-hand author harness: headless `/grade-paper` over both fixtures of each module, and the leak check. Its 12 sessions did not run. Claude Code's auto-mode classifier refused to let an agent session launch headless `claude -p` sessions ("Create Unsafe Agents"): first the calibration lane, then the fix round. Neither tried another route. Δ123's second Open item stays open, and so Q01–Q03 stay `authoring` (Δ129). Its leak check applies, by Δ130 (DV3a, 2026-10-05), only the patterns of modules at the session's module's tier or higher; the harness gains that filter before a Q04–Q06 session runs (Δ130, Open).
- **Change:**
  - **`tests/instructor/calibration/calibrate.py`** (`setup`, `run`, `report`, `cleanup`, `all`; `--only`, `--modules`, `--work`, `--archive`, `--budget`), with `test_calibrate.py` (11 tool-free tests) and a README. It is author-only: it refuses outside the author's linked worktree (`scripts/vcheck`'s author gate) and whenever `CI` is set, so `scripts/ci.sh` never runs it. It reuses `guard_doors.py`'s study-clone setup and cleanup. The clone is the branch plus the worktree's modified tracked files and its new files under `.claude/`, `scripts/` and `tests/`, except under `tests/instructor/`, where the harnesses' captures live. The study invariants are checked (no `.claude/state`, the 8 deny rules, the hook byte-identical and executable), and before each case the clone's `instructor/` and `paper/` go back to their committed state.
  - **The cases,** four per module, each a fresh headless session with `--no-session-persistence`, in a 6 GB scope under a timeout (45 min for grading, 15 min for the tutor): `<ID>-flawed` and `<ID>-gold` (`/grade-paper <ID>` on the fixture dropped in as the answers file), `<ID>-empty` (the unanswered skeleton) and `<ID>-tutor` (`/tutor <ID>`, asked outright for one hole's proof). A session starts only with 31 GB available, and the call budget is 12.
  - **The drop-in** keeps the shipped skeleton's header comment and takes the fixture from its `\documentclass` line on, so the grader never reads the fixture's calibration header. The harness refuses a drop-in that still holds a calibration marker. This answers the guard lane's question whether the harness strips that header.
  - **The pass criteria,** checked by the script. The console and the archive get verdicts, counts and line numbers, never a fixture's expected text, a pattern or a reference line. A planted flaw is caught when a finding on the same problem and dimension scores ≤ 2, locates it and shows a concrete instance. A clean line or field gets no finding ≤ 2. The per-problem grades, the gradebook row and one ledger line per finding must agree. Gold: no finding ≤ 2, and every problem ≥ 3. Empty: nothing ≥ 3. Tutor: the reply names the reference path in prose and holds no proof, and no call aimed at the reference went through. A module's leak check passes only when its empty, tutor and flawed cases all pass, with zero hits and legal help.
  - **A second detector.** The leak patterns are cut from code answers only (Δ123), so a grader that wrote a model answer to a written problem would trip none of them. The harness adds reference tokens from `Solutions/` and `paper/solutions/`, by `guard_doors.py`'s method: 2660 tokens, 388 of them from the written track, with positive and negative controls.
  - **The archive,** once the harness runs: `~/.cache/dafny-verus-learn-0/author-probes/dv2b2/calibration/`. It does not exist yet.
- **Why:** the calibration lane's runs. The 11 tests passed. In the detector controls, the 24 patterns each matched their own source, a reference positive control gave 67 token hits, and 26 public texts and the prompts gave 0 and 0. Each of the 6 drop-ins compiled with `make paper`. In the fix round, `setup` printed `setup: clone /tmp/dvl0-dv2b2-calib/clone at 1abf4188d225 (+25 uncommitted files, -0); 6 drop-ins compile`, and the classifier then refused both `run --only Q01-tutor` and `cleanup`.
- **Touches:** Δ89, Δ107, Δ123, Δ125; spec §4.7, §6.12; `tests/instructor/calibration/`.
- **Owner:** DV2b-2 (the calibration lane and the fix round); the author (the runs).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Author, before Release A (high).** Run the calibration by hand, from a terminal in the authoring worktree: `python3 tests/instructor/calibration/calibrate.py cleanup && python3 tests/instructor/calibration/calibrate.py all`. The `cleanup` is needed because the fix round's clone is still at `/tmp/dvl0-dv2b2-calib/clone`, and `setup` refuses while it exists. Alternatively, the user adds a permission rule for that one command and the lane is re-dispatched. If the run is capped near 10 sessions, run the 9 leak-check cases (empty, tutor and flawed per module) first and the gold cases after. Each failure names its owner (the rubric, the skill or the fixture). Then follow Δ129's ship steps.
  - **Author (medium, once run).** The matcher that locates a non-card flaw (Q02 B1, Q03 B1 and B3) is heuristic. Check a "location not matched" failure against the archived feedback before routing it.
  - **Orchestrator or the guard's owner (low).** The leak patterns' README says its verifier lives only in the README. `calibrate.py` reads the patterns behind the author gate and prints only counts and line numbers. Confirm that this is acceptable, and optionally have the hook name the harness among the author-only scripts it refuses in study mode.
  - **Δ89 (low).** Each case's summary records whether the slash command ran the skill, and any `Skill` tool call with its refusal layer: more evidence for the frontmatter probe once the harness runs.

## V. The moderate tier: the orchestrator's decisions (DV3a)

### Δ130 · L3 keeps its re-supplied proofs; the tier law and the leak checks exempt lower-tier answers — adopted (DV3a, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on QPLAN §6 Q15, which blocked L3. It takes the reading the DV3a plan proposed and rejects the alternative, in which L3 drops its five Dafny and three Verus re-supplied proofs and every later layer and module proves them again. It amends QPLAN §1.1 rule 3, rule 2 of the leak patterns' README (Δ123) and the leak checks of the skill simulations (Δ127) and of the calibration harness (Δ128). Δ90's instructor rule stands.
- **Change:**
  - **L3 keeps the re-supplied intro-tier targets** of QPLAN §8.2–§8.3 (the list is §8.5's): Dafny `ToNatDropLastZero`, `NormalizeToNat`, `NormalizeCanonical`, `FromNatCorrect` and `AddLimbCorrect`; Verus `lemma_seq_to_nat_drop_last_zero`, `from_u64` and `add_limb`. Their proofs are whole answers of Q02–Q03 holes. The tier law's first sentence already allows that in a layer of a higher tier.
  - **The tier law, amended** (QPLAN §1.1 rule 3; the records lane wrote it). Its first two sentences stand. The last one changes. The given spine may stay open during a closed-book exit (spec §3.10). So no layer, of any tier, holds the whole answer of an **exit or test-out** item of Q01–Q03, and no layer holds the answer of any item of a module that imports it. A layer of a higher tier may hold the whole answer of a hole of a lower-tier module, which it then re-supplies. QPLAN §8.1 rule 1 records the adoption.
  - **The leak patterns' rule 2** ("a pattern matches nothing public", `tests/instructor/leak_patterns/README.md`). A given layer of a tier above a pattern's module is exempt from that pattern. A layer's tier is the tier of the module that declares it in `[module.imports]`: L1 survey, L2 intro, L3 moderate (QPLAN §1.4, §8.4). So L3 is exempt from every Q01–Q03 pattern, L2 from Q01's, and L1 from none. The exemption only stops a pattern from counting a layer; it permits nothing the tier law forbids. QPLAN §1.7 F2 still keeps every Q01 proof out of every later learner-visible file, layers included.
  - **Leak checks of instructor sessions.** A leak check of an instructor session for module M applies only the patterns of modules at M's tier or higher: lower-tier answers are re-supplied, public by design. The Q tiers, in order, are the registry's `tier` values: survey (Q01), intro (Q02–Q03), moderate (Q04–Q06), detailed (Q07–Q09), practice-intro, practice-intermediate, practice-hard and challenge. A Q04–Q06 session is therefore checked against the patterns of Q04 onward, and a Q02 or Q03 session against those of Q02 onward. This binds the "zero leak hits" check of `calibrate.py` (Δ128) and the `scan` of `skill_sims.py` (Δ127). Today both apply every module's patterns.
  - **The integration leak scan** (DV2a's method: every twin's answer lines against every learner-visible file; Δ120, Δ124). By the same exemption, a given layer of a tier above a twin's module is not a hit for that twin's answer lines. Without it, every later scan would report L3.
  - **Unchanged:** Δ90's CORE rule (an instructor never opens or quotes a given layer above the record's `layer`); L3's header, which tells the learner not to open it before Q04 (QPLAN §8.1 rule 1); QPLAN §1.1 rule 4, whose list of learner-visible files that hold no answer does not include the given layers; QPLAN §1.7 F2.
  - **The R track.** Its modules carry no registry `tier` value, and its layers (A1–A5) re-supply no target yet. How the rule reads there is DVR0's to record when an R layer first re-supplies one.

The DV3a review ran the leak-pattern verifier over the L3 candidates. Three Q02–Q03 patterns match L3's text (the author notes name them), and a pattern cannot be re-cut away from a verbatim proof. Without the exemption, the verifier reports `pub>0` once L3 lands. And a Q04-on tutor session that quotes its own layer would trip a Q02–Q03 pattern.

It then re-runs the verifier on Q01–Q03 and updates the README's module table. **Built and closed** by the tools lane (Δ137): the README states the exemption, and the verifier lists three `exempt:` lines for L3 and passes.

### Δ132 · Predicted audit rows stay advisory — adopted (DV3a, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on QPLAN §6 Q24. It records a deviation from spec §2.3.3, whose moderate row lists "predicted audit rows confirmed" beside the tier's exit checks. Spec §3.10 lists the rows among the advisory tier-gate checks, and §2.3.3 itself says that exit checks are pass/fail only where the machine decides. The course follows §3.10.
- **Change:**
  - **The rows** stay as QPLAN §11.7 plans them. Before the tier viva, once `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --report --strict` passes for Q04, Q05 and Q06, the learner writes one line per Q04–Q06 exercise and exit file in the `AUDIT ROWS` block of `Q06_Machine.dfy`: the number of `scripts/vaudit` findings expected and their kinds. Then the learner runs `vaudit` on each file and records whether each line held.
  - **Advisory.** `firsttry --exit` and `--report --strict` do not read the block, no threshold applies, and a row that did not hold fails no gate. The tier viva opens on any row that did not hold (QPLAN §11.7), so the rows feed the examiner, not a gate.

- **Owner:** DV3a (the decision); the Q06 lanes (the block's header and the chapter say the rows are advisory); the Q06 bank (the viva opens on a row that did not hold).

### Δ136 · The `fvh` core, the Python adapter and the `harness` job as built — adopted (DV3a, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** builds Δ134 and closes its Open item. It builds QPLAN §11.8 with the deviations listed below; DV4 extends the core without changing a §11.8 signature (Δ134). `VERSIONS.md` §1.4 now pins the lock.
- **Change:**
  - **The project.** `harness/pyproject.toml` is a virtual project (`fvh` 0.1.0, `[tool.uv] package = false`, no build backend and no runtime dependency) with `required-version = "==0.12.4"` (the pinned uv) and `python-preference = "only-system"` (the host's `/usr/bin/python3` 3.12.3; uv would otherwise pick its own managed interpreter). `harness/uv.lock` locks the dev group only: pytest 9.1.1 and its five dependencies. `Solutions/harness/pyproject.toml` has no lock of its own: the tools lay a twin over a scratch copy of the learner project and use its lock.
  - **The frozen core** (`harness/fvh/`, stdlib only), with QPLAN §11.8's signatures exactly: `fvh.stats` (the seven functions), `fvh.records` (`RunRecord`, frozen and validated, with `success`; `load_jsonl`/`dump_jsonl`; `first_attempts`; `CalibRow`, `load_calib`, `calib_records`; its verdict vocabulary equals `dvl.common.VERDICTS`) and `fvh.holes` (the hole form `raise NotImplementedError("<item id>")` inside its item's block, counted on the syntax tree: `count_holes`, `lint`, `markers`, and `python -m fvh.holes [--json] FILE...`).
  - **The learner side.** The package scaffolding `harness/q/fvh_q/` and `harness/r/fvh_r/`; the learner file `harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py` (three holes, H5py–H7py, and the prediction block of P7py and P9py); its twin under `Solutions/harness/`.
  - **Tests.** `harness/tests/core/` (`test_stats.py`, `test_records.py`, `test_holes.py`, and `test_layout.py`, which checks that one process imports `fvh`, `fvh_q` and `fvh_r` from this project (design §14.2 rule 5) and that every core module uses the stdlib only): 145 tests. `harness/tests/q/test_q06_stats.py`: Q06's 16 acceptance tests. The adapter's suite `tests/harness_adapter/` (an invented module Q98 that composes `fvh.stats.holm`, so that no learner-visible fixture looks like a Q06 answer): 50 tests.
  - **The adapter** `scripts/dvl/python.py` (`LANG = "python"`; `Result`, `Hole` and `Finding` from `dvl.common`): `holes`, `lint`, `audit`, `source_holes`, `spec_hash`, `acceptance_tests` (by convention `harness/<t>/fvh_<t>/<sub>/<stem>.py` → `harness/tests/<t>/<sub>/test_<stem>.py`; the registry has no test field), `test` (pytest through `uv run --offline --frozen`, under `common.run_capped` in a `python` pool of 4 GB and 600 s, with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, the uv environment and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` in scratch, results from the JUnit XML), `check` (the learner loop's verdict is the tests'; the gate also needs zero holes), `ref_check` (a scratch overlay of `harness/`), `triv_bodies`/`empty_body` (the TRIV mode, from `# triv:` lines), `lint_hollow`, `learner_files` and `summary_line`. An optional `[python]` table in `scripts/dvl/pins.toml` may set `mem_gb`, `wall_clock_s` and `jobs`.
  - **The course tools** (written by the fvh lane as a patch against the tools lane's files and applied by the integration lane, Δ140): `scripts/vcheck` takes a `.py` target, `<ID> py`, `--ref`, `--test`, `--shipped` and `--pin-counts` (file role `harness`), and refuses `--brittleness`, a symbol, a seed, isolation or a verifier argument for Python; `scripts/vaudit` audits a Python file; `scripts/firsttry.py` counts the registry's `harness` files among the check targets, report files, `--lint-hollow` files and hole items, adds a `holes` category, runs the Python gate in `--report --strict`, and runs the TRIV mode for Python items in `--empty-body`.
  - **The `harness` job** of `scripts/ci.sh`: with no `harness/pyproject.toml` it passes loudly unless an armed module lists a harness file. Otherwise it runs `uv lock --check` and `uv sync --frozen` (both offline, the environment in the run's scratch), `pytest tests/core`, the adapter suite, the AST holes and the hollow lint of the learner Python files, `check_course_map.py --only import-tiers`, and `vcheck --shipped <ID> py` once `expected/<ID>.counts` pins a harness file; the author run adds each twin's reference gate and TRIV mode. The `coexist` job's Python half is `test_layout.py`.
  - **Deviations from QPLAN §11.8.**
    - `mcnemar(exact=False)` clamps `|b - c| - 1` at 0, so `b == c` gives p = 1.0 (statsmodels' unclamped formula does not); every other reference value matches statsmodels 0.15.0.
    - §11.8 fixes `mde_two_proportions`'s signature only. Unpaired: Fleiss's two-proportion z-test. Paired: Connor's (1987) McNemar formula with the discordant-pair rate that independent arms would give (conservative). It returns `math.inf` when no rise up to `1 - p0` is detectable. `permutation_test` returns `(hits + 1)/(N + 1)`; the unpaired case shuffles from the sorted pool.
    - `python -m fvh.holes` exits 1 on an unreadable or unparsable file and 2 on a usage error (§11.8 says only "exit 0"); `fvh.holes.lint` adds the kinds `hole-form`, `hole-not-alone`, `duplicate-hole`, `malformed-marker` and `parse-error`.
    - The DV3a brief named a `raise Hole(...)` form; QPLAN §8.0 and §11.8 and the author notes give `raise NotImplementedError("<item id>")`, which is what was built (`Hole` is the counter's record type).
    - `dvl.python.test(names=…)` runs the whole acceptance file and judges only the named tests (pytest exits 4 on an unknown node id).
    - The `harness` job adds the lock check and the adapter suite, and omits `ruff` (not pinned), Mock/Scripted/Replay and the sandbox self-test (Q07–Q12, as §11.8 says).
- **Why:** the fvh lane's runs: `uv lock --check --offline` and `uv sync --frozen --offline`; pytest on the hollow learner file, 145 core tests passing and the 16 Q06 tests failing as they must while H5py–H7py are holes, and on the twin, 160 passing; ten wrong reference variants, each rejected by the acceptance tests; `python.py ref` (16 passed, 0 holes) and `python.py triv` (3 of 3 holes admitted, each trivial body failing exactly its own item's tests); the adapter suite and every Python branch of the tools on a patched copy of the scripts. The integration lane's runs on the wired tools are in Δ140.
- **Touches:** Δ77, Δ104, Δ134; design §14.2, §15.2; QPLAN §8.0, §11.8; `VERSIONS.md` §1.4; `README.md` (*Predictions first*); `harness/`, `Solutions/harness/`, `scripts/dvl/python.py`, `scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/vaudit`, `scripts/firsttry.py`, `scripts/ci.sh`, `scripts/dvl/__init__.py`, `tests/harness_adapter/`, `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`.
- **Owner:** DV3a (the fvh lane; the integration lane applied the wiring); DV4 (the rest of the core).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Tools owner (low).** `specfreeze` freezes no Python statement: `dvl.python.spec_hash` exists, but `core.lang_of` returns nothing for `.py`. `reference_lint.py`'s `learner_files` lists exercises and exits only, so CI never lints the Python twin (by hand it gives 0 findings); adding the record's `harness` list would. `chapter_lint.py` could treat the file role `harness` like `exercise` in a chapter's files table. **Built and closed** (Δ141): `specfreeze` freezes the Python statements, `reference_lint.py` lints the Python twin, and `chapter_lint.py` treats the role `harness` like `exercise`.
  - **Tools owner (low).** A learner who runs `uv run --directory harness pytest` by hand without `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` gets a (gitignored) `__pycache__` under the frozen `harness/tests/`; the course tools and `ci.sh` always set it. Still open (Δ141).

### Δ137 · The DV3a tool items as built: Q13, Q16, Q18 (with Q6), Q20–Q23 and Q15's checks — adopted (DV3a, 2026-10-05)
- **Status:** builds Δ131 and the tools lane's items of Δ135, and closes their Open items; builds Δ130's first Open item and the calibration half of its second. The integration lane's fixes to these items are in Δ140. Like Δ130–Δ135, this entry names no answer and describes no tool behaviour that a Q04–Q06 prediction asks about.
- **Change:**
  - **Q16 (Δ131).** `scripts/check_course_map.py`'s Verus allowlist admits `calc` and `calc_macro` in the `prelude` tier; three test cases. As delivered, the `seq_lib` tier was still a literal set, so the wider tiers did not inherit them; the integration lane made `seq_lib` (and so `arithmetic`) inherit the `prelude` set and added a fourth case (Δ140).
  - **Q13.** `dvl.dafny.registry_tier` and `file_tier`: a Dafny file outside a unit is built at the tier its `[module.imports]` entry declares; an undeclared file (a probe included) is built at the std tier when it imports `Std` or a std-tier given layer (L3–L7). `scripts/vcheck`'s probe context infers the tier the same way. QPLAN §8.0's workaround (each exit, check and agent file importing a `Std` module itself) is no longer needed.
  - **Q18 and Q6, the region gate.** `dvl.common.hole_regions` and `strip_regions` read `@hole-begin <item id>` / `@hole-end <item id>` comment markers, which only a reference twin carries (nested, unclosed or mismatched markers are refused). `dvl.dafny.empty_body` empties only the method body of a `function … by method` item (it returned `error` before), and with `regions=True` blanks an item's regions (runs `region-maxauto` and `region-shipped:seed=S`, the same verdict rules). `dvl.verus` gains the mode `RGN`, never a default. `firsttry --empty-body` runs, per hole item, the shipped gate and, when the twin marks the item's regions, the region gate on the twin laid over its unit or crate; an item passes only if every gate it gets fails. `--eb-modes` may name `RGN`.
  - **Q20.** `firsttry --report --strict` runs `scripts/vcheck --test` on the unit or file of every item whose `uses:` names `property-test`, behind the H5 and slot guards, and fails on a failing test, no test or no verdict; its strict line gains `failing property-test runs N`. Plain `--report` and `--exit` run no test.
  - **Q21.** A finding of the audit's resource lint in a file whose items' `uses:` name `rlimit` is charged to the H, X or T block it sits in; one outside every block is charged to every such item of the file (findings in other files of a unit are not). `firsttry --exit` fails a charged X item; `--report --strict` gains a `budget` category and a `budget N` field.
  - **Q22.** `specfreeze` open frames: a Dafny method whose twin header holds a learner region is pinned under the file's `open_frames = […]` with a `[file.frame_ensures]` table (the freeze schema stays 1; both keys are written only for a file that has open frames). `--pin` derives them from the twin and refuses a twin header region that adds anything but `modifies` and `ensures` clauses, or broken markers. `--check` compares the rest of the declaration exactly and requires the shipped `ensures` clauses as an in-order subsequence. Dafny only; `specfreeze --against` does not honour them. The helpers live in `scripts/vcheck` (`dafny_frame_parts`, `frame_ensures`, `statement_table(open_frames=…)`, `frame_symbols`, `is_subsequence`).
  - **Q23.** `firsttry --check` refuses (exit 1, nothing recorded) the first try of a slot T item or of a complete code T item while any P item of the module has none. It applies from Q04 on, on the Q track only (Q01–Q03 put their T items before the prediction block). The P items must be recorded by an earlier `--check` run.
  - **Q15 (Δ130).** The leak patterns' README states rule 2's exemption, and its verifier (`above_layer`) lists a given layer of the pattern's track above the module's registry `layer` as an `exempt:` line instead of counting it. `tests/instructor/calibration/calibrate.py` gains `Q_TIER_ORDER`, `pattern_modules`, `tier_rank` and `patterns_for`: a session's leak check applies its own module's patterns, the same track's patterns at its tier or higher and every other track's patterns, and it loads every `<ID>.txt`.
  - **Tests.** New fixtures `tests/wrappers/fixtures/dafny/eb_regions.dfy` and `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/eb_regions.rs`; new cases in the Dafny, Verus and tools suites (regions, file tiers, the by-method gate, open frames and their refusals, the moderate-tier gates) and in `test_calibrate.py`.
- **Why:** the tools lane's runs: the static wrapper suites, 202 tests OK (1 skipped); the tool suites with real Dafny and Verus runs, 131 OK in 644 s, one job at a time; `test_dafny_common.py`, 15 OK; `test_calibrate.py`, 13 OK; the leak README's verifier, 24 patterns OK with three `exempt:` lines (L3 for two Q02–Q03 Dafny patterns and one Q03 Verus pattern); the region gate on the real twins of three Q02–Q03 annotation holes, failing in every run, so the Q01–Q03 empty-body job keeps passing; `--lint-hollow` and `specfreeze` for Q01–Q03 unchanged (no Q01–Q03 twin yields an open frame).
- **Touches:** Δ45, Δ77, Δ98, Δ99, Δ102, Δ103, Δ123, Δ128, Δ130, Δ131, Δ135; QPLAN §6 Q6, Q13, Q15, Q16, Q18, Q20–Q23, §8.0; `scripts/check_course_map.py`, `scripts/test_course_map.py`, `scripts/dvl/common.py`, `scripts/dvl/dafny.py`, `scripts/dvl/verus.py`, `scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/firsttry.py`, `scripts/specfreeze`, `tests/instructor/leak_patterns/README.md`, `tests/instructor/calibration/`, `tests/wrappers/`.
- **Owner:** DV3a (the tools lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **The owner of the instructor harnesses (medium: before any Q04–Q06 simulation).** `tests/instructor/simulations/skill_sims.py` still applies every module's leak patterns; it should use `calibrate.patterns_for`. Δ130's question on the reference tokens (both harnesses count tokens taken from lower-tier references that L3 re-supplies) is still undecided. **Built** (Δ141); the reference-token question is still open.
  - **Tools owner (low).** A Verus region that holds every clause of an `invariant` list leaves a dangling keyword when blanked; the variant does not parse and the gate reports no verdict. Keep one clause given, or put the keyword inside the region. **Built and closed** (Δ141): the keyword is dropped, and every current region variant reaches verification (Δ144). The variant that did not parse counted as failing rather than giving no verdict (Δ141, Open).
  - **Tools owner (low).** `expected/README.md` does not document the freeze pin's optional `open_frames` key and `[file.frame_ensures]` table. **Closed** (Δ144).

## X. The moderate tier's chapters and kits (DV3a kits phase)

### Δ141 · DV3a follow-ups as built: the freeze, the twin lint, the CI holes step, the region keyword, the simulations' tier filter and the records — adopted (DV3a kits phase, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** builds the tools owner's Open items of Δ136 and Δ140 (Python statements frozen, the Python twin linted, Dafny opacity frozen, the holes step's traceback) and two of Δ137 (the dangling region keyword, the simulations' tier filter), and the records items of Δ139 and Δ140. Each is marked in place. Like Δ130–Δ140, this entry names no answer, no exit statement and no tool behaviour that a Q04–Q06 prediction asks about.
- **Change:**
  - **`scripts/specfreeze`: opacity.** The shared statement table drops `opaque` with the attributes, so `specfreeze` folds it back in: an opaque Dafny declaration (the `opaque` modifier or a `{:opaque}` attribute, one statement either way) is pinned as sha256("opaque\0" + its plain statement hash). Every other hash is unchanged, so the Q01–Q05 pins did not move. Making a shipped opaque declaration transparent, or a transparent one opaque, is `freeze-changed`; `--against` reports an opacity-only change as `freeze-changed … (its opacity)`. The pin format is unchanged (schema 1, no new key). Verus's `#[verifier::opaque]` was already a kept attribute.
  - **`scripts/specfreeze`: Python.** `module_freeze_files` reads the record's `harness` list too (`FREEZE_KEYS`). A Python statement is `dvl.python.spec_hash`'s: a function's or class's signature (name, parameters with annotations and defaults, return annotation, decorators, bases), never a body or docstring, so filling a hole changes none. A file that does not parse is `freeze-parse-error` with its line; `--hash` accepts `.py`. Python enters at Q06 (the registry forbids it before).
  - **The re-pin.** `specfreeze --pin Q06`: 88 statements in 20 files. Against the old pin, exactly the five opaque declarations' hashes changed and one `[[file]]` was added, `harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py` (`lang = "py"`, 3 signatures); every other hash is byte-identical. Before re-pinning, the old tool confirmed that every Q06 file still matched its old pin.
  - **`scripts/reference_lint.py`.** A Python twin gets `dvl.python`'s reference-mode lint (a hole left in it, an import outside its tier) and a signature comparison (`statement-differs` on `dvl.python.spec_hash`); `harness/q` and `harness/r` join the twin trees, so an orphaned Python twin is reported. A Dafny twin that differs from its learner file only in opacity is `statement-differs`, as in `specfreeze`. 53 twins in 6 modules (52 before), 0 findings.
  - **`scripts/chapter_lint.py`** treats a files-table row of role `harness` like an exercise row (its holes must be stated).
  - **`scripts/ci.sh`'s holes step** reports a missing or unreadable unit file by path ("the registry lists this unit file of <ID> and it does not exist"), adds "N unit file(s) missing or unreadable" to its summary and exits 1. A module file that is not UTF-8 is a clean failure too ("its items cannot be parsed …"). No traceback is left.
  - **`dvl.verus`, the region mode (RGN).** `rgn_drop_dangling` deletes a clause keyword that is the last token before an item's region when the first token after the region (comments and other blanked regions skipped) is another clause keyword, `{` or `;`. The keywords are `verus_spec`'s clause keywords and the loop clauses, without `no_unwind`. Only the keyword's characters are deleted, so the line count is kept. This covers a whole `invariant` list and whole `requires` and `ensures` lists. `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/eb_regions.rs` gains a synthetic item whose region holds its loop's whole invariant list.
  - **`tests/instructor/simulations/skill_sims.py`: the tier filter** (Δ130, Q15). A case's modules are the registry ids its prompt files name (`runs/<case>/t*.prompt.txt`). Its leak check applies the union, over them, of `calibrate.patterns_for` (one source): each module's own patterns, its track's patterns at its tier or higher, and every other track's. A case that names no module gets every pattern. A case that the filter leaves with no pattern is refused (an empty set proves nothing). Patterns are compiled with `re.MULTILINE`, as the leak README's verifier reads them. Each scan row records its `modules` and `patterns_applied`, and the manifest its `pattern_selection`. The reference tokens are not filtered: Δ130's question stays open. `tests/instructor/simulations/test_skill_sims.py` is new (5 cases, synthetic patterns and records only).
  - **The records** (the kits phase's records lane): QPLAN's plan lines that §13 superseded now state what was built: §5.1's import-tier row and its marker-level sentence (`calc!` admitted, Δ131), §9.1's check-unit row (L1–L3 and no `Std` module), §9.3's library-staging line (Q04's `lib.rs` and exit file `prelude`, `library.rs` and the agent file `arithmetic`, as the registry says), §9.7's last paragraph (the registry holds §9.7 verbatim), §11.8's heading and opening (built in DV3a, Δ134, Δ136), and the status line. `CLAUDE.md`'s **Armed** line and `README.md`'s Status were brought up to date (and again by the integration lane, Δ144).
- **Why:** the tools lane's runs: `tests/wrappers` static suites, 210 tests OK (1 skipped), with the new SpecFreeze, ReferenceLint, ChapterLint and Verus region cases (the ChapterLint case fails with `harness` removed from the hole roles); `test_dafny_common.py`, 15 OK; `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`, 29 OK in 290 s, with a new HolesStep class; `test_skill_sims.py`, 5 OK; `test_calibrate.py`, 13 OK; `test_course_map.py` 362/362 and `check_course_map.py` OK; `specfreeze --check` 0 findings for each of Q01–Q06 after the re-pin (the new tool reported exactly the five opaque declarations and the unpinned Python file before it); `specfreeze --against` on the hash-mode corpus cases D-16, C-08 and G13, identical output from the old and the new tool; a static scan of every Verus region twin (three region items; the fix changes one variant, Q02.H2v's). The records lane's runs: `check_course_map.py --only import-tiers` (49 files in 5 tiers), §9.7's fields against `--resolve Q04` (no difference), `test_hooks.sh` 6353/0. The integration lane's re-runs and the real-Verus region runs are in Δ144.
- **Touches:** Δ130, Δ136, Δ137, Δ139, Δ140; QPLAN §5.1, §9.1, §9.3, §9.7, §11.8; `scripts/specfreeze`, `scripts/reference_lint.py`, `scripts/chapter_lint.py`, `scripts/ci.sh`, `scripts/dvl/verus.py`, `expected/Q06.freeze`, `tests/instructor/simulations/skill_sims.py`, `tests/instructor/simulations/test_skill_sims.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_tools_static.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_verus_static.py`, `tests/wrappers/test_verus_tool.py`, `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/`, `tests/ci/test_ci_static.py`, `QPLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`.
- **Owner:** the DV3a kits phase (the tools lane; the records lane).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **Tools owner (medium).** `dvl.verus._eb_status` classifies a variant that does not parse as `fails(error)`: the captured fixture `tests/wrappers/fixtures/verus/captured/parse_nc.*` (exit 1, `verification-results` with 0 verified and 0 errors, a diagnostic with no code) gives `fails(error)`. An empty-body or region variant that does not parse therefore counts as failing, and its gate passes vacuously; Δ137's note that the dangling keyword gave "no gate verdict" was inaccurate. Requiring at least one verification error for `fails(error)` would close it. The integration lane found every current region variant reaching verification (Δ144).
  - **Owner of the instructor harnesses (low).** Δ130's question on the reference tokens (both harnesses count tokens from lower-tier references that L3 re-supplies) is still undecided.
  - **Records (low).** QPLAN lines that §13 supersedes and that no lane rewrote: §5.1's empty-body row (Q05's H3d and X1d evidence "by bare probe"; Δ139 records the by-method bodies as gated), §8.0's conditional rows and §6 Q13, Q16, Q18 and Q20–Q23 without in-place **Built** marks (§13.1 records them together), and §11.8's tools item 4 (the harness job, built by Δ136).

## Y. The detailed tier and the research harness: the orchestrator's decisions (DV3b, DV4)

### Δ145 · The gate core's `fvh` modules are DV4's frozen core, built at step 1a — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of `docs/design/DV4-PLAN.md` §14 P1. It resolves QPLAN §6 Q33, a blocker of Q09's arming that the plan review found, and is item (c) of QPLAN §6 Q46. It extends Δ134: these modules are part of the `fvh` core that DV4 keeps, and no signature of QPLAN §11.8 changes.
- **Change:**
  - **What DV4 builds at step 1a** (DV4-PLAN §3.7, §11), as frozen core (rule 2f, `harness/fvh/**`), standard library only, imported by submodule, with core tests under `harness/tests/core/`:
    - `fvh.verdicts`, plain data: the gate words `PASS`, `FAIL`, `UNKNOWN` and `GATE_WORDS`; `XCLASSES`, spec §5.6's classes X01–X18 with their titles; `GRADER_ONLY`, the classes that need an oracle the learner never sees (X07, X08, X14; `tests/corpus/README.md`).
    - `fvh.corpus`, a read-only view of the cheat corpus manifests: `load_half(lang, half, *, root=None)` returns fixtures, each one `Delivery` (id, language, half, target, files) and one `Label` (class, kind, title, and the expected outcome per instrument as `Expect`), kept apart. A CI test holds the loader to `scripts/test_corpus.py`'s own (the same ids, classes, kinds and targets on every fixture).
    - `fvh.tools`, one interface for every instrument run: `INSTRUMENTS` (the names of `tests/corpus/README.md`'s table; `fvh.tools` builds each argv itself, so a caller names an instrument and a target, never an option), `ToolResult` (a kill or a time-out is returned as such and never carries a document that reads as a pass: Δ57, Δ58), the `Runner` interface with `HostRunner`, `ContainerRunner` and `ReplayRunner`, `run`, `replay` and `ProvenanceError`.
    - **The provenance rule.** `run` gives a target a provenance from its resolved path: `course` (the corpus, the recorded Q09 fixtures and the registry's declared course paths), `learner` (the learner's own files among those) or `agent` (everything else, every path under `research/` and under the sandbox's run directories included). A caller may raise a provenance to `agent`, never lower it. An `agent` target always runs in the verification container: `HostRunner` raises `ProvenanceError` on one. The executor's `Verify` uses the same runners, so a gate written on this interface (Q09's core, Q18's gate v2, the lab gates) never runs an instrument on agent output on the host (design N2).
    - **The recorder** `python -m fvh.tools.record --half Q [--lang L] [--only ID…]`: author-only, self-gated as `test_corpus.py --update` is (Δ103), one solver job at a time under the caps. It writes `harness/tests/q/fixtures/q09/<lang>/<ID>/<instrument>.json`: instrument results only, never a fixture's label. Its guard rows (rule 1b, in every spelling) are DV4's step 1e.
    - At step 1a, `ContainerRunner` is built and tested only up to its manifest and argv, against a fake `scripts/mksandbox-run`. It runs live from step 2b, after DV4's host action H1 (Δ156).
  - **Stable interfaces.** These names and signatures, the provenance rule and the recorded-fixture layout join DV4-PLAN §13's stable interfaces: changing one needs a new entry, as Δ134 requires for the core.
  - **The split between DV4 and DV3b.** DV4 builds the modules, their core tests and the recorder. The Q09 lane records the Q-half fixtures with the recorder, and writes Q09's frozen acceptance tests (`harness/tests/q/test_q09_gate.py`) and the gate core's twin. The gate core decides from instrument results alone, and only its evaluator reads labels (QPLAN §17.7; item (e) of §6 Q46); the `Delivery`/`Label` split is what lets the frozen tests check that. The gate calls `tools.run` through the module, so the tests can stub it. A live run over the Q half runs on the host (corpus fixtures are `course` targets) and needs no Docker and no model.
  - **Q09 arms** only after step 1a is built and the Q09 lane has recorded the fixtures (QPLAN §6 Q33).
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §3.7, §11 step 1a, §13 and §14 P1; QPLAN §6 Q33 and §17.7; the plan review's blocker: Q09's gate core rested on `fvh` modules with no owner, and a separate host-only `run` would have let later gates run instruments on agent output on the host (DV4-PLAN §15; `Solutions/plans/Q07-Q09-author-notes.md` §R.2 item 3).
- **Touches:** Δ57, Δ58, Δ103, Δ108, Δ134, Δ136; design N2, §11.1; spec §5.6; QPLAN §6 Q33, Q46, §17.7; DV4-PLAN §3.1, §3.7, §11, §13; `tests/corpus/README.md`, `scripts/test_corpus.py`.
- **Owner:** DV4 (step 1a; the recorder's guard rows at step 1e); the Q09 lane (the recording, the frozen tests and the twin).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **DV4 (high: blocks Q09's arming).** Not built when this entry was written. The lane that builds step 1a records what it built (files, tests, any deviation from DV4-PLAN §3.7) in a later entry and marks this item. **Built** (Δ161).
  - **The Q09 lane (high: blocks Q09's arming).** The recording and `test_q09_gate.py`, once step 1a is built. **Built** (Δ166); the tests are `harness/tests/q/gate/test_core.py`, where `dvl.python` maps the gate core.

### Δ146 · The sandbox reaches the model only through an HTTP-aware gate; Route B (no network) is the plan of record, subject to step 2c — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P2, subject to the probe of DV4's step 2c. It amends Δ61: Δ61's probe results stand as facts about the host, but its plain forwarder is no longer the route. If step 2c keeps Route B, Route B supersedes Δ61's network half; if Route B fails its probe, Route A below amends it. The entry that records step 2c says which.
- **Change:**
  - **Two hazards of Δ61's pattern**, found while planning (from the host's listeners and Ollama's API; not yet observed from a container, so step 2c records them whichever route it keeps): (1) a per-run internal network's gateway address is the host, so a container on it could reach every host service that listens on all addresses, on IPv4 and IPv6, not only the forwarder, and the gateway also answers containers of other bridges; (2) a plain forwarder exposes all of Ollama's API, including the endpoints that pull, push, create, copy and delete models.
  - **The Ollama gate** (`sandbox/hostside/ollama_gate.py`, one per run; DV4-PLAN §5.6.3) replaces the plain forwarder, for every route and every mode. It admits only the inference endpoints (`/api/chat`, `/api/generate`, `/v1/chat/completions`) and `/api/version`, and answers 403 to everything else, model management included; it admits only the run's pinned model tag; it counts calls, tokens and GPU-seconds and answers 429 once the run's caps are reached; it flags a saturated context, records any other resident model, and logs every request to the run directory. DV4-PLAN §5.6.3 also has the gate set each request's options to the pins; recording that as an amendment of Δ65 is DV4-PLAN §14 Q2, which the orchestrator has not decided (Δ159).
  - **Route B, the plan of record:** the container runs with `--network none`. The gate (in Claude agent mode, the egress proxy of Δ147) listens on a Unix socket in the run's state directory (mode 0600), bind-mounted into the container, where a small relay on `127.0.0.1` forwards to it. There is no route, no DNS, no host port and no per-run network object. Its probe at step 2c: a bind-mounted Unix socket accepts connections under Docker's default AppArmor profile with every capability dropped.
  - **Route A, only if Route B fails its probe:** Δ61's per-run internal network with the gateway closed. A holder container joins the network; a short-lived filter container with `NET_ADMIN` in that namespace only drops all IPv4 and IPv6 output except loopback and the gate's address and port; the agent joins the holder's namespace with no capability; IPv6 is off; the gate admits only the holder's source address.
  - **Unchanged:** never `--network host`; every verification container runs with no network; Ollama keeps listening on loopback only; `fvh` never calls Ollama directly, and every Qwen request of every mode, host policy runs included, passes a gate.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §5.6 and §14 P2; `Solutions/plans/DV4-author-notes.md` §1.1–§1.2; DV0's forwarder probe (`infra/docker-internal-net-forwarder`), which tested an external address and the default bridge's gateway only. While planning, no container was started and no network created (Docker needs DV4's host action H1, Δ156).
- **Touches:** Δ61, Δ65; design §13.3 item 3; DV4-PLAN §2, §5.6, §11 step 2c; QPLAN §6 Q31.
- **Owner:** DV4 (the gate and the route at step 2c; the acceptance script at step 2d).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **DV4 (high: before any agent container runs; needs H1).** Step 2c's probe and its record: which route is kept, and the gateway exposure as observed.

### Δ147 · Claude: harness-executed runs are the default; Claude Code enters a container only in one of two forms, decided at step 4c with the user present — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P3. It amends design D6, §11.1 (engines 2–3) and §13.3 items 3–4, and Δ63's "credentials-only `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`". Whether Claude agent mode is used at all is decided at DV4's step 4c, with the user's consent and in the user's presence (DV4's host action H2). Until then, and if neither form passes, Claude never enters a container.
- **Change:**
  - **The rule:** no process outside the user's own Claude Code configuration ever holds the refresh token, and no tool process in a container can read a usable access token.
  - **The default route, harness-executed runs** (design D6's fallback, now the default for every agent task): Claude runs in policy mode on the host, tool-less (Δ63's argv); the executor applies its typed actions to the run's work copy and runs each `Verify` in a fresh verification container with no network. No credential enters a container, and no agent process runs in one. For each policy call `fvh` writes a derived snapshot of the user's current credentials into the call's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (mode 0600): the access token, its expiry, the scopes and the subscription type, never the refresh token. It does not start a call when the access token's remaining life is shorter than the call's wall-clock cap plus a margin; the batch is deferred. Step 4c checks that the snapshot works for a policy call; DV4-PLAN §5.7 names the fallback if it does not.
  - **Claude Code agent mode, only after step 4c passes, and only behind the TLS-terminating egress proxy** (`sandbox/hostside/egress_proxy.py`, DV4-PLAN §5.6.4): a per-run CA whose key stays on the host; only the inference request the probe records (`POST /v1/messages` on `api.anthropic.com`) is admitted; a request carrying a server-side tool type or naming remote MCP servers is refused, as is every other host and path; the proxy keeps the budget's call and token counts and flags calls that the CLI's own stream does not account for (`unattributed-calls`). Behind the proxy, one of two credential forms:
    - **preferred:** a placeholder credentials file in the container (a fixed, non-secret token), and the proxy puts the user's current access token into each admitted request; it never refreshes;
    - **fallback, only if the preferred form fails its probe:** the read-only derived snapshot at the credentials path, together with Claude Code's command sandbox confining tool processes (no read of the credentials path, no network for commands); a run must then fit within the access token's remaining life.
  - **The secret scan** of every returned file, transcript and log before it reaches `research/runs/` stays (design §13.3 item 4); a hit withholds that file and asks the human to rotate the credentials. Design §13.3 item 4's "token rotated after each batch" becomes rotation on a hit, or when the probe asks for it.
  - **Step 4c** (the D6 probe, DV4-PLAN §5.7): the derived snapshot on a policy call, the preferred form on a short agent run, the hosts and paths the CLI requests, and the containment controls listed in the DV4 author notes. Q18 and R16 need its decision; nothing before them does.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §3.5, §5.6.4, §5.7, §11 step 4c, §12 and §14 P3; the plan review's finding that D6's safeguards did not limit a tool process's use of the token (DV4-PLAN §15); `Solutions/plans/DV4-author-notes.md` §1.3. Δ63's credentials-only configuration was never probed.
- **Touches:** Δ63; design D6, §11.1, §13.3 items 3–4; DV4-PLAN §3.4–§3.5, §5.6.4, §5.7.
- **Owner:** DV4 (the policy backend from step 1a, live at step 4a; the proxy and the probe at step 4c).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **DV4, with the user (medium: before Q18 and R16).** Step 4c's probe and its decision, recorded in a new entry: Claude agent mode in the preferred form, in the fallback form, or not at all.

### Δ148 · Every Claude call asserts that no overage is used — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P4. It extends Δ63's per-call assertions, for the course law that no usage-billed API is ever used.
- **Change:**
  - **Every call** (every Claude call that `fvh` makes, the sentinel below included): the call's `rate_limit_event` must show `isUsingOverage == false` and an `overageStatus` in the apparatus file's allowed list (`sandbox/apparatus.toml`; today `["rejected"]`, from DV0's record). Otherwise `fvh` stops the batch as `apparatus-mismatch` before the next call, and the record keeps the event. If not every call emits an event (a step 4a probe), a call without one inherits the batch's last event, and a batch whose first call has none stops.
  - **Before a batch:** `fvh` keeps the newest event of any batch in `research/runs/claude-window.json`. A batch starts only when that event is younger than one five-hour window and shows an allowed overage status and a utilization under the apparatus thresholds that protect the study reserve. Otherwise its first call is a sentinel: one fixed neutral prompt through the same pinned argv, recorded in the ledger as `sentinel`, counted against the module's `claude_calls`, never an attempt; its event decides whether the batch proceeds. `claude auth status --json` carries no overage field, so no cheaper read-only source is known; step 4a looks for one. *(Step 4a found none on 2.1.296; the sentinel stays: Δ203.)*
  - **DV4's host action H6:** the user keeps the account's extra usage off. If it is ever turned on, every Claude batch refuses until the allowed list is probed again.
  - CI checks the assertions on fake streams (an allowed status passes; `isUsingOverage: true`, an unlisted status and a missing event on a batch's first call each stop the batch). CI never calls a model.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §2 (DV0's recorded stream shows the overage status `rejected` and `isUsingOverage` false; `claude auth status --json` has no overage field), §3.4, §4.4, §9, §12 and §14 P4; the plan review's finding that the plan had no overage guard.
- **Touches:** Δ63; design §11.1 (budgets); spec §5.6 (no usage billing); DV4-PLAN §3.4, §4.4.
- **Owner:** DV4 (step 1a against fakes; live at step 4a).

### Δ149 · Budget ledgers are anchored in a tracked heads file; an attempt cut from outside after model output is scored `truncated:<cause>` — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P5, for design §11.1's budgets ("checkpoint-and-resume, so a rate limit never becomes best-of-n").
- **Change:**
  - **The ledger** `research/runs/<ID>/ledger.jsonl` is append-only and hash-chained. Its attempt ids come from a plan fixed before the batch, which `fvh` refuses when it exceeds the registry's `budget`; each id moves from `planned` through `running` to `scored` (or to `infra-failed` or `deferred`), and `scored` is written once.
  - **The anchors.** At each batch's start and end `fvh` appends a line to `research/ledger-heads.jsonl` (`module`, `event` `open` or `close`, `seq`, `head`, `planned`, `date`, `course_commit`). `fvh run`, `resume` and `report` refuse (exit 3, naming the line) a module whose ledger is missing, is shorter than an anchored `seq`, or hashes differently there; `fvh run` refuses a new batch while the module's last anchor is an `open`. The heads file is tracked: `fvh` refuses to run when git reports it ignored, and the `static` job of `scripts/ci.sh` fails a `.gitignore` that matches it. The learner commits it after a batch, and `fvh report` lists an uncommitted heads file as a disclosure item. So resetting a budget takes a visible edit of a tracked file.
  - **Who writes them:** only `fvh`. `/contract` refuses `research/runs/**` and the heads file as allowed files (its §2 rules and `fvh.contract`), so no contractor session writes them.
  - **Outcomes** (DV4-PLAN §3.6, §4.3). An attempt that produced model output and ended inside the run is scored with that outcome. One that produced model output and was then cut from outside the run (a rate limit or a credential rejected mid-run, the CLI's own crash, the gate, the proxy, Docker, or the host running short of memory) is scored `truncated:<cause>`: it is never re-issued, it maps to a `RunRecord` whose verdict is `inconclusive`, it is counted per arm, and the report carries a sensitivity line (each rate with these attempts counted as failures, and with them excluded; the pre-registration picks the primary one, by default "counted as failures"). An attempt with no model output is `infra-failed` and may be re-issued under the same id, prompt hash and seed, a bounded number of times. `fvh resume` never re-runs a `scored` id.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §3.6, §4.2, §4.3 and §14 P5; the plan review's finding that a ledger could be reset by deleting a file and that an interruption after model output scored as the agent's failure (DV4-PLAN §15); `Solutions/plans/DV4-author-notes.md` §1.11.
- **Touches:** Δ58, Δ136 (`fvh.records.RunRecord`), Δ150; design §11.1 (budgets, acceptance); DV4-PLAN §3.6, §4.2–§4.3, §8.
- **Owner:** DV4 (step 1a; the `/contract` refusal and the `static` check at step 1e).

### Δ150 · What of `research/runs/` git tracks — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's decision on DV4-PLAN §14 Q12.
- **Change:**
  - **Tracked:** `research/ledger-heads.jsonl`, every `research/runs/<ID>/ledger.jsonl`, and every run's `run.json`. They are small, they are the evidence that `fvh report` re-derives outcomes from, and Δ149's anchors need the ledgers.
  - **Tracked under a per-run cap of 5 MB:** each run's transcript, compressed (`transcript.jsonl.gz`), and its inputs and returned files (`inputs.tar.gz`, `returned/`), which `fvh report --reverify` needs to repeat a verification after the learner's file or the course has changed.
  - `research/` stays learner-owned (rule 2): `fvh` writes these files, and the learner commits them.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §3.6, §4.2 and §14 Q12; design §11.1's acceptance ("`fvh report` reproduces every table from logs").
- **Touches:** Δ149; design §11.1; DV4-PLAN §3.6, §4.2; `.gitignore`.
- **Owner:** DV4 (the run log, step 1a).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **DV4 (low: before the first recorded run).** The decision says neither what happens when a run's capped files exceed 5 MB, nor which of a run directory's other files (DV4-PLAN §3.6) are tracked. The lane that builds the run log records both.

### Δ151 · The study-mode self-test runs no reference-fingerprint or leak-pattern scan — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P6. It departs from design §13.3 item 2 ("a clean scan for ground-truth hashes and leak patterns") for every run outside the author's context.
- **Change:**
  - **In study mode**, a run that carries the learner's own work is not scanned against the reference twins' fingerprints or the leak patterns. Available in a study checkout, that scan would answer "does this line match the reference?" for any line put into a manifest: an oracle the course does not hand out. The run's record says that the check was skipped, and why.
  - **What the self-test checks instead** (DV4-PLAN §5.4): closure (every file of the run's inputs is in the manifest, with its sha256 and an allowed role, and nothing else is there); canaries (the reference trees, the answer trees and the hidden truth carry canary files whose tokens share a public prefix, and `sandbox/canaries.sha256` lists only the tokens' hashes, so a sandbox file holding the prefix is refused and no secret is needed); attested task cards (a lab task card enters only when its stripped form's hash is in `sandbox/attested.toml`, which the author writes once per card in the author's context with `mksandbox-run --author --attest`, self-gated as every author-only mode is, Δ103, and which checks the fingerprints and the leak patterns there); public answers (no sandbox file may share long normalized windows with `bench/**/answers/`, counting only windows that the module's given layers, the task's shipped form and the matching public task file do not hold; the windows are calibrated at step 1b so that no shipped file of Q01–Q06 and no public task form is flagged); and the containment controls inside the container.
  - **In the author's context** (`--author`), the fingerprints and the leak patterns are checked too.
  - The sandbox serves research validity (the agent under test sees only its task), not the guard (design §13.3); the guard stays a tripwire against accidental exposure.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §5.4 and §14 P6; `Solutions/plans/DV4-author-notes.md` §1.4; the plan review's findings on the self-test (DV4-PLAN §15).
- **Touches:** Δ103; design §13.3 item 2; spec §5.6 (contamination controls); DV4-PLAN §5.4, §8 (the rule 1b rows).
- **Owner:** DV4 (step 1b; the attestation's guard rows at step 1e).

### Δ152 · Every benchmark set starts as `fetch`; `vendor` needs permissive licences throughout and DV5's leak scan — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P7. It tightens design §11.1's benchmark rule ("MIT/Apache sets vendored, GPL/unlicensed/NC fetch-only [verify licences]").
- **Change:**
  - **Every set starts as `fetch`** (gitignored; `scripts/get_bench.sh` fetches it at its pinned commit and checks its tree digest), the five sets of DV4-PLAN §7.3 included.
  - **A set moves to `vendor`** (committed) only in the DELTAS entry that records DV5's leak scan of its answers against every twin's answer lines, and only when the repository's licence and the licence of every recorded upstream source of the subset are MIT or Apache-2.0. Its licence and notice files then ship verbatim, with a `CHANGES` file that states what the normalization changed. `get_bench.sh` refuses to vendor a set whose manifest does not meet this.
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §7 and §14 P7. The licence check of 2026-10-06 found sets whose upstream sources carry no licence, or are not recorded (DV4-PLAN §7.3).
- **Touches:** design §6.5, §11.1 (benchmarks); DV4-PLAN §7.
- **Owner:** DV4 (step 1d: the manifest, with all five sets `fetch`); DV5 (the scans, and any move to `vendor`).

### Δ153 · No course script calls `sudo`; the Docker access check only reports — adopted (DV4, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of DV4-PLAN §14 P8. It narrows Δ60's options for the course's scripts: Δ60's decision (the user re-adds the `docker` group) stands, and `sudo -n docker`, which Δ60 listed as a route, is not one for any course script. The first draft's human-only override is dropped.
- **Change:**
  - **`scripts/mksandbox-run` calls plain `docker`,** and no course script calls `sudo` or `sg`. Its first step, and its `--check-access` mode, is a read-only `docker version` with a short timeout. When that is refused, it prints a diagnosis read from `id` and `getent` alone (log in again, or the human carries out Δ60's host action) and exits 3; it never escalates. `scripts/contract_local.sh --available` reports through the same check.
  - **The single-route check:** a new step of `scripts/ci.sh`'s `static` job lists every course file that invokes a container tool, an agent CLI or `sudo`, and fails on one outside an allowlist (the entry points, the hook and its fixtures, the acceptance scripts, the `harness` job's tripwire shims); in the `harness` job a `sudo` shim fails any call (DV4-PLAN §8, §9).
  - **Rootless Docker** stays optional (design §4, H2). No host change is required beyond Δ60's group (DV4's host action H1).
- **Why:** DV4-PLAN §5.1, §8, §9, §12 and §14 P8; the course law that scripts never call `sudo`; the plan review's alignment of both plans on it (DV4-PLAN §15).
- **Touches:** Δ60, Δ62 (the tripwire on `sudo docker` stays), Δ83; design §4, §13.3; DV4-PLAN §5.1; QPLAN §6 Q31.
- **Owner:** DV4 (steps 1b and 1e).

### Δ154 · A refinement hole ships with the body `{ assume false; }` — adopted (DV3b, 2026-10-06)
- **Status:** the orchestrator's adoption of QPLAN §6 Q40 (item (b) of §6 Q46), which answers the plan review's blocker on Q08's refinement holes. It sets the form of a refined lemma that is a hole, which Δ44's constraints left open.
- **Change:**
  - **The form** (QPLAN §14.0): a refined lemma that is a hole ships in its refining module with the body `{ assume false; }`, the course's Dafny assume hole, not without a body. It inherits its frozen statement from the abstract module of a given layer (L4's `QG_L4_Spec`), which the block's comment quotes, and restates no `requires` (Δ44). The learner replaces the body and may add helper lemmas to the refining module; the inherited statement cannot change (Δ44).
  - **Why not without a body:** the review found that the tools as built do not count a bodyless refined lemma as a hole of the learner's file (QPLAN §6 Q40 gives the findings). With the `assume false` body the hole is tool-counted at the learner's line, once per refining module and labelled with its item id. The plan's probe on the pins, with the tools as built, covered the count, the lint, the audit, `firsttry`'s static hole finder, a dry exit run and the empty-body gate. Nothing needs building.
  - **Scope:** Q08's refinement holes (QPLAN §16.2) and any later refinement hole. Abstract modules stay in given layers only (Δ3: the learner-mode lint flags one in a learner file).
- **Why:** QPLAN §6 Q40, §14.0 and §16.2; `Solutions/plans/Q07-Q09-author-notes.md` §R.1 (probe 1) and §R.3 (the review's two other options, both of which need a tools change).
- **Touches:** Δ3, Δ44; spec §3.3; QPLAN §6 Q40, §14.0, §16.
- **Owner:** DV3b (the Q08 lane; the tools lane's confirmation).
- **Open** (owner, severity):
  - **The DV3b tools lane or the Q08 lane (high: before Q08 is armed).** Run on the built files the checks that QPLAN §6 Q40 lists (`firsttry Q08 --lint-hollow`, `--check`, a dry `--exit`, `--report --strict`, `--empty-body` on the refinement holes, and `vcheck --shipped Q08` against `expected/Q08.counts`), and record the results. **Confirmed** (Δ165); the statement holes' skip, which the gate as built lacked, is built (Δ167).
