Text a learner-visible course file shows never counts (both tests). For the renamed test, shapes found in two or more installed public benchmark files and spans of declaration headers, brackets and comments only are also set aside. **The exact test stays fail-closed:** an exact copy of a twin's span, names and all, counts even when its shape is pool-common (Δ231; the alternative is Δ232 item 9). The index is built once per process (about 15 s). Refusals read "holds N span(s) of a reference twin's answer lines (W whole, R renamed; <twins>)".
  - **`attest_data`** (signatures unchanged): each entry records `scan = "DV5e"` and `pools = "<n> files, sha256 <16 hex>"` (the installed pool files the rule read); an entry of an earlier rule is re-scanned when attested again (rewritten in place on a pass, its `source` kept; withdrawn on a failure). The study-mode self-test still checks the hash only. On a machine without the installed pools the renamed test is stricter (three cards would be refused), never looser.

Its header says how the scan works. **Lab 2's ten public cards (Δ229) are not attested yet** (Δ233's open items).

### Δ232 · Decisions the DV5e lanes need from the user — awaits the user (DV5e, 2026-10-13); settled by the user's decisions D27–D35 (2026-10-14; Δ234)
- **Status:** as Δ224 for DV5d: the readings the round left open. Each stays as built until the user chooses, recorded in a new entry; each is put to the user as a multiple-choice question in the round's report. Like Δ195–Δ231, it names no answer, no reference statement and no weakened statement.
- **Change** (as built; options, recommendation first):
  1. **The S arms on Lab 2's public cards** (Δ229). 8 of the 10 forms do not verify with the reference statement alone (their code needs the upstream proof annotations), while S0 takes the first sample that verifies and S1's kill renders mutants of the shipped code. As built: the runs on the public strata wait. Options: (a) on a public card a delivery may add proof annotations (invariants, assertions, proof blocks, lemmas; never executable code), the lab gate checks the code unchanged, and S1's kill rate mutates the delivery's annotated code (recommended); (b) the public cards' S arms are judged on the specification alone (it must resolve and pass the witness and vacuity checks; body verification and the kill rate are not used); (c) redraw from tasks whose form verifies with the reference and no proof (not possible for HumanEval-Verus's pool, whose rule requires the proof-free form to fail the gate).
  2. **The definitions the public forms keep** (Δ229). As built, as the local cards do: the forms keep the specification's vocabulary; in dp2 and dp4 the reference ensures is one call of a shipped predicate, in dp1 and dp5 one such call plus a relation or length fact. Options: (a) keep as built; (b) also remove the non-recursive definitions only the specification uses and seal them with the reference as `definitions` (the grader still judges a delivery that states its own; recursive ones stay) (recommended); (c) remove every specification-only definition (deliveries over recursive ones would be judged `undecided`). (b) or (c) rebuilds the forms and re-runs C1, C4, C6 and C7 on the same draw, re-seals Q15 and re-confirms `HIDDEN_CHECKS_HELD`'s Q15 manifest; the ten cards are attested after it.
  3. **Ten files derived from fetch-mode sets are tracked** (`bench/local/q/lab2pub/`). Options: (a) keep them tracked with `NOTICE` (recommended); (b) gitignore the forms and have the learner build them after `get_bench.sh` (`lab2pub.py build`, which refuses a sha256 mismatch), tracking only `STRATUM.toml` and the draw; (c) move humaneval-verus and vericoding to vendor mode (a DELTAS entry and a leak scan).
  4. **Lab 1's HumanEval-Verus pool (83) contains Lab 2's five Verus tasks,** and Lab 1's task forms keep the specification, so a Lab 1 draw that takes one exposes its Lab 2 reference. Options: (a) Lab 1's draw excludes the five (its pre-registration rule and the Q12 chapter say so) (recommended); (b) leave the overlap and record it as a contamination case; (c) redraw Lab 2's Verus stratum after Lab 1's draw.
  5. **Lab 2's and Lab 3's own references are not twins of the attestation scan** (Δ228): Lab 2's reference specifications are the sealed hidden truth and Lab 3's reference deliveries live only in the author archive, so a card that shipped its own task's reference answer would not be caught by the scan (the leak patterns, Lab 2's empty specification and Lab 3's counted stub holes remain). Options: (a) at attestation time the scan also reads those references as twins, decrypted into memory and never written, and the entries are re-scanned once (recommended); (b) leak patterns for them instead; (c) leave it, recorded as a limit.
  6. **A hidden-check verdict the learner reports, in the code review** (Q13 C5, Q15 C4, Q17's reader; Δ230). As built: A7 records the reported word or `not run`, and no row's grade rests on it. Options: (a) as built; (b) a reported `weak`, `strong`, `vacuous` or `mismatch` anchors a C5/C4 finding at grade 2, an `undecided` stays the reader's call (recommended: it matches D21's "graded by its hidden two-way check"; `/review-code`'s calibration on Q13 and Q15 then re-runs); (c) the review asks for the verdict line first and caps C5/C4 at 3 without one.
  7. **Where Q17.H3d's hidden check sits in the learner's sequence** (Δ230). As built: §4's instrument row only, after the contract run and the AUDIT. Options: (a) keep that, so the contract run and the audit stay where a false statement is found (recommended); (b) say nothing in the chapter and keep the check as an admission instrument only; (c) run it before the statements are committed (the contract run's lesson weakens).
  8. **The hidden check's outcome words and how often the learner may run it** (Δ231 L2). The prose-item judge prints, per row, the verdict and six outcome words that show the side and direction of a difference, and the learner can run it any number of times. Options (the review-fix notes letter them b, a, c): (a) keep the output (the text now says what it prints) and add a chapter rule: run it once per item, after the statement is final, and report that first run (A7 records it), as with `firsttry` (recommended now); (b) `truthcheck` prints only the verdict and why for prose rows (an image rebuild and re-pins; recommended as well, at the next image rebuild); (c) keep both and record a known limit. Items 6 and 8 interact: under 6(b), 8(a)'s first-run rule is what makes the reported verdict a grade's anchor.
  9. **D25's reading of the scan's exact test** (Δ228, Δ231 T2). Options (the review-fix notes letter them b, a, c): (a) keep the exact test fail-closed, as built and documented (an invented card that equals a twin's span word for word is refused even when two public files show its shape) (recommended); (b) set pool-common shapes aside for the exact test too (measured: 15 of 626 whole-region and 235 of 2,934 single-line copies no longer caught); (c) (b) only for spans made of structure and keyword tokens.

### Δ240 · Decisions the DV6 lanes need from the user; readings taken as built — settled by D36–D44 (Δ243)
- **Status:** as Δ232 for DV5e. The release plan's freeze bar requires no open DECISION NEEDED, so each item below holds the freeze; each stays as built until the user chooses, recorded in a new entry, and is put to the user as a multiple-choice question in the round's report. Like Δ195–Δ239, it names no answer, no reference statement, no weakened statement and no sealed card's detail.
- **Change** (as built; options, the recommendation named):
  1. **S1's kill rate on a public Lab 2 card** (Δ236; high, before Lab 2's public runs). Of the 259 mutants the reference statement kills on the ten public cards, 229 also fail with the `requires` alone: the proof annotations stop holding for the mutated code, so the rate mostly measures the annotations; and 192 of the 299 mutants change only an annotation (static count, `labcode.code_mutant`). Options: (a) S1's feedback counts only the mutants that verify under the candidate's `requires` alone (reference-free; about twice S1's runs per sample); (b) keep D27 as built and record the limit (the report gives S1 − S0 per stratum); (c) S1's feedback on a public card drops the kill rate and keeps the vacuity and witness checks; (d) drop the mutants that change only annotations before any run (no solver cost; combinable with (a)). Recommended: **(d) with (a)**; the killed and killed-without-`ensures` counts on the 107 code mutants need one grader-container run.
  2. **A sealed definition the reference applies under a quantifier** (Δ236; medium). A faithful delivery that states its own, renamed version of a sealed definition is judged faithful on 5 of the 6 cards that lost definitions and `undecided` on one, where the grader's empty lemmas cannot instantiate across two differently named functions; `undecided` counts as unfaithful for a card. Options: (a) keep in the form any definition the reference's specification applies under a quantifier, as recursive ones are kept (one card gets its definition back: rebuild, C1/C4/C6/C7, re-seal, `HIDDEN_CHECKS_HELD`'s manifest, re-attest) (recommended); (b) keep as built and record the limit; (c) add each sealed definition's unfolding to the grader's lemmas as a trigger hint (an image rebuild).
  3. **The vericoding arm and the code check** (Δ236; high, before arm V runs; the user's, or the tools owner's if delegated). The plain lab gate now refuses a change to a Lab 2 card's code; arm V writes its own code, and `fvh.batch`, `ContainerRunner`, the agent route and `fvh report --reverify` pass only `lab_task` to the gate, so V's Verifies on Lab 2 cards fail. Options: (a) a per-arm plan field `lab_code = "own"`, recorded in the run and passed as `vcheck --lab-code own`, so a re-verify repeats it (a stable-interface addition) (recommended); (b) V gets its own task forms (bodies removed) in a third stratum directory; (c) the code check runs only when a plan opts in (S0, S1), the reverse default.
  4. **D30 across languages** (Δ236, Δ238 D-r3; medium). Lab 1's 78 drawable HumanEval-Verus members include the HumanEval problems of four of Lab 2's five public Dafny cards. Options: (a) Lab 1's draw also excludes the HumanEval ids of Lab 2's public Dafny cards (74 members; the stratum header, Q12 §4.3, B1, a `Lab1Draw` test) (recommended); (b) keep D30 as worded and record the overlap in Q12's disclosure field; (c) as built, and Q15's report flags such a card.
  5. **Q15.H2d/H2v's minutes** (Δ238, T-M12; medium). The pair is `admitted = false` with 0 minutes, but the chapter, QPLAN and the CALIBRATION block budget its line at 15. Options: (a) gate S12's line at 0 minutes (the module total 613; the chapter's §0, QPLAN §25.3/§25.7, the CALIBRATION block and the units pin re-captured); (b) build F-H2d/F-H2v (the proof given) with their four results; (c) admit the pair under the short-format band exception, as D2 did for H1, and add it to `OUT_OF_BAND` (recommended).
  6. **The host's rustup** (Δ238, T-M8; medium; DV4-PLAN §12's H8). rustup replaced itself (1.28.2 → 1.29.1), so `sandbox/build-images.sh --check` fails on both images' lock; the pinned 1.28.2 `rustup-init`, whose sha256 is the lock's, is archived (`dv6/fix/rustup/`). Options: (a) put it back (`install -m 755 <the archived file> ~/.cargo/bin/rustup`, then `rustup set auto-self-update disable`) (recommended); (b) re-lock at the next image rebuild, which D33 already defers the verdict-only output to; (c) rebuild the images now, so D33's deferred item lands with it.
  7. **Cutting `basic` without the history that keeps fixed answers** (Δ238, L-1; high; the cut needs the user's approval). Options: (a) cut `basic` as one squashed commit of the frozen tree on top of `main` (`git commit-tree <the frozen tree> -p main`), re-create the authoring worktree from `origin/basic`, never merge the old `authoring` history into a shipped branch, add a freeze-bar check that `git log basic` holds no commit but `main`'s design commit before the cut, and move Δ120's squash item onto the cut in `CLAUDE.md` item 3 (recommended); (b) keep the history after rewriting the leaky blobs (`git filter-repo`); (c) keep it and widen README's history rule to every file.
  8. **The freeze bar's model sessions and their budget** (Δ238, T-M3, T-M5, L-3; high: blocks the bar). About 150 sessions: `calibrate.py all` 72, the simulations about 50 turns, `guard_doors.py` 11 doors, the dry run's capstone under the final contract (6–10 calls) and 3 gold `/contract` transcripts, and the other agentic files' known-good transcripts (spec §12.1's course-level line). On 2026-10-14 the seven-day window stood at 0.58 (`allowed_warning`), where the window rule defers. Options: (a) wait for `allowed`, then run in priority order `guard_doors.py` → the simulations → `calibrate.py` → the capstone and gold transcripts → the other known-good transcripts, each batch stopping at a defer (recommended); (b) the user releases a stated number of calls from the study reserve now; (c) narrow the bar to the user's list (the simulations, `calibrate.py all`, `guard_doors.py all`, the dry run with its capstone and gold transcripts), and the other known-good transcripts become post-freeze fixes on `basic`. (A local-Qwen route for the gold transcripts, `contract_local.sh`, is not the `/contract` skill path the spec names.)
  9. **D32's `/review-code` calibration** (Δ237, T-M6, D-r5; medium). `/review-code` refuses unless every gate passes, so a case needs a gate-green Q13 and Q15 state. Options: (a) build invented gate-green states, each a module-sized independent solution (large); (b) run the cases on a study clone whose Q13 and Q15 learner files are their reference twins, the module's own patterns and tokens dropped from the scan, the reported first-run verdict in the prompt (`weak` → C5/C4 at 2; `undecided` → no anchor; a later run → recorded, no anchor) (recommended); (c) record D32's calibration as not runnable and rely on the rubric text.
- **Readings taken as built** (the user may overturn any; none holds the freeze): the release plan replaces Release A's "Q01–Q03 ship" step, and the modules stay `authoring` until the branch step (Δ234); a sealed twin's exact copy counts even when the pools show its shape (Δ237); merge bases with no order go to the latest committer time (Δ235); Q12's pilot reading (the first forty members per public stratum; Δ239); the practice chapters' Solutions-last timing (Δ239, T-M11); the code check on the local Lab 2 cards (Δ236); `get_bench.sh --members` beyond D30's text (Δ236); the scan's known limit for one Lab 3 card (Δ237).
- **Touches:** Δ221, Δ229, Δ234–Δ239; QPLAN §22.6, §25.3, §25.6–§25.7; DV4-PLAN §12 (H8), §13; `bench/local/q/{lab1,lab2pub}/`, `Hidden/labs/Q15/`; `scripts/dvl/{labcode,lab2pub,benchstrip}.py`, `harness/fvh/batch.py`; `sandbox/image/`, `sandbox/images.lock`; `MODULES.toml` (Q15); `CLAUDE.md` item 3; the author harnesses.
